## 0.3.x shipped — moved verbatim from `docs/EPICS.md` §3 on 2026-09-21 (releases 0.3.0 → 0.3.3, 2026-08-25 → 2026-09-20)

> **Headline: iSCSI (block storage) + Backup phase 2 (snapshot-consistent backups and restore).** Two independent chains: iSCSI's core needs nothing from Backup phase 2; only LUN backup/restore (`backup2.4`/`backup2.7`) joins them. Staged like 0.2.10 — dev builds deploy to the fleet as stories land; the release is cut when everything is live-proven. File restore (`backup2.5`/`backup2.6`) is the severable piece if the release drags.
>
> Riders: ~~**#46** (recurring replication task validates the target pool locally for a remote location)~~ **done 2026-09-03** (`15e708f`: the stage-1 copy of the target guards never learned locations — extracted to `services/replication-target.ts`, shared by the one-shot and task routes; pool existence asked where the target lives, PVE-managed/onto-itself local-only); ~~a never-run task reports `lastRunResult: success`~~ **done 2026-08-26** (`e4963fd`: `never-run` from the ONE `deriveRunResult` helper — enabled + unit readable + no retained history — additive enum member in all three run-result schemas, muted pill in all three grids; two pre-existing tests had encoded the bug as fixtures and were corrected); ~~the **`ANAS.editGuard` dedupe**~~ **done 2026-08-26** (`04283d5`: the two copies were behaviourally identical except `unblockSave`, now one superset helper in `10-api.js`; harnesses unchanged at 90/617). *(Both were built by LOCAL agents — the first code work delegated to the operator's own model — and reviewed here against the originals.)*
>
> Attention notes marked **⚠** came from the 2026-08-25 design review; the five that bit hardest in review (and the one live proof found — a fileio LUN restored as a silent empty disk, `iscsi.8`): LUN serial persistence (`iscsi.2`), target boot ordering (`iscsi.5`), initiator reconnect during a block restore (`backup2.7`), replication's base-snapshot discovery vs the transient backup snapshot (`backup2.3`), the FUSE hang trap in archive browsing (`backup2.6`).

### Backup phase 2 — snapshot-consistent backups, boundaries, restore *(continues Epic 16)*

> Epic 16 shipped every backup as a **live** backup (the snapshot step was designed and never built) and shipped **no restore** — the client has `restore` (whole or `--pattern`-selective, explicit `--overwrite*` flags), `mount` (FUSE browse), `map`/`unmap` (`.img` → loop device). Restore is wiring, not building. Two restore *types*, by nature: **files are selective; block images are whole.**
>
> **Decoupled, not here:** file-level restore from *local* ZFS/btrfs snapshots (no PBS client involved — its own future thing, §4).

##### Dev (ground truth first)
**backup2.1** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a dev, I want ground truth on a disposable PBS + the stunt node before code — so every parser and verdict is built on reality. **Result: `docs/BACKUP-RESTORE-GROUND-TRUTH.md` (62 facts) + 24 real fixtures under `fixtures/backup/` (client/server 4.2.5).** Headlines: metadata-mode continuity across the live→snapshot switch HOLDS (inodes identical, `had to backup 0 B`); `restore` refuses every existing target incl. block devices (`--overwrite` does not help) — devices are written via stdout (`restore … -`) or `map`+`dd`; a size mismatch is unguarded and destructive (writes until ENOSPC); a regular file is a first-class `.img` source; `--change-detection-mode` is a no-op for `.img`; `--pattern` is suffix-matched unless anchored with `/`, `[` unescaped silently matches nothing, a no-match is a silent success; FUSE `mount` daemonizes, a dead PBS yields fast `EIO` while a black-holed one leaves readers in D state that `timeout` cannot kill (`/sys/fs/fuse/connections/<N>/abort` is the only lever) and `stat -f` reports the dead mount healthy; **`catalog shell` is a scriptable non-FUSE browser (`ls`/`stat`/`find`/`select`/`restore-selected`) over a pipe**; a btrfs ro snapshot drops nested subvolumes with no client flag to recover them; `snapdev` restore is `zfs inherit`, not `set hidden`; a killed restore leaves a partial tree with no marker (in-flight file is `0600`); a bare group path restores the LATEST snapshot silently.

##### Enhancement — filesystem boundaries are visible, never silent
**backup2.2** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want nested filesystems under a backup source **detected, shown, and chosen — never silently skipped**: the wizard and the task detail list every nested filesystem under a source with its kind (child dataset, btrfs subvolume, local disk, NFS/CIFS mount, pmxcfs); a per-archive **`includeNested: none | all | [paths]`** (→ the client's default / `--all-file-systems` / one `--include-dev` per path); an alert when the current choice would back up a nested filesystem as an empty directory; and the client's `skipping mount point:` lines parsed into run warnings — so an omission is on the record, not in a log nobody reads. **Default = `none`** (the client's own; PVE's lead). *(GT: **detection is an `st_dev` walk of the tree, not `findmnt`** — btrfs subvolumes are not mounts but carry their own `st_dev` (GT btrfs: `@data`=66, `@data/photos`=67), and that is exactly what the client keys on; `findmnt` then names what the walk finds. Exclude `.zfs/snapshot` automounts; treat autofs placeholders as the mounts family does. Absent = `none`, and the edit dialog shows it as absent — never writes a default on an untouched save. Own detection is the authoritative warning; the log parse is secondary. Product-level example: backing up `/etc` today silently omits `/etc/pve`. **Ruling 2026-08-25:** an uncovered nested filesystem makes every run "completed with warnings" — so the 16.12 notification reads `warning` until the operator chooses `none` / `all` / paths — accepted for the first release as the never-silent intent; the mail stops the moment a choice is made. `all` resolves at run time to per-archive `--include-dev` flags, never `--all-file-systems`, which is per-invocation and would spill onto sibling archives.)* *(**Known limitation from live proof (F3):** ANAS's own detection is hang-proof (17 ms against a black-holed NFS mount, never stat'ed), but the client itself, handed a source that CONTAINS a dead remote mount, wedges even when told not to cross it — the run sits `activating` until the 600 s supervisor ceiling reports it. Candidate remedy for `backup2.3`'s live proof to ground-truth: emit `--exclude <relativePath>` for every uncovered remote nested mount, IF pxar skips an excluded entry before `lstat`-ing it; otherwise this joins the backup-source-guard candidate (refuse a source whose nested remote mount is unreachable at run start).)* **Result:** `services/nested-filesystems.ts` (`find -xdev` walk through the executor with timeout; `findmnt`/`btrfs subvolume show` naming), `includeNested` with absent-shows-absent proven byte-identical, `all` resolved to per-archive `--include-dev`, preview endpoint, wizard alert + detail pill, run warnings from own detection + client log; live-proof wave 1 PARTIAL (all option paths PASS incl. `catalog dump` truth; dead-remote-mount wedge = the known limitation above; `includedNested` forwarding fixed at source). **Fix-ups (2026-08-28, operator-found on a fleet node):** (1) **a source ROOTED on a remote mount (CIFS/NFS) is never walked** — the mount table already names the mount the source sits on and that row IS the answer, so the scan stops after the table (no `find`, no probe) and a remote-rooted task no longer spends its whole scan budget over the network; (2) **the task detail is instant, the boundary scan loads progressively** — the detail GET no longer runs the walk (that is what hung a remote-rooted source past the gateway's cross-node forward and read as a false "node unreachable"); the detail renders at once and the files-kind archives show a spinner while the window loads the scan through the existing preview endpoint (under a 10 s budget that fits under that forward), and a scan that cannot run says "unavailable" on the archive rows with the rest of the detail untouched; (3) **an uncovered nested filesystem is a NOTICE, not a warning** (operator ruling 2026-08-28, revising the ruling above) — it is the consequence of a deliberate `includeNested` choice, so the run stays `success`, the 16.12 notification stays at `info`, and the line rides the body as a "Notes:" block on success and warning alike; only a genuinely incomplete (truncated) scan is still a warning; (4) the gateway's forward timeout now says what it is — 504 "did not answer within 15 s: the request timed out. The node is reachable" — distinct from a real connection failure (502, unreachable).

##### Snapshot-consistent backups
**backup2.3** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want ZFS- and AHR-backed sources backed up **from a snapshot** — snapshot → back up from the snapshot path → destroy — so a multi-hour run captures one instant, with sources that cannot be snapshotted (remote mounts, foreign filesystems) backed up live and **labeled live**. *(Consistency is derived per source from its capability, shown in the wizard and the run result.)* **Reuse, don't re-implement:** `zfs snapshot` is already invoked in two places (`routes/datasets.ts`, `services/snapshot-schedules.ts`) — extract ONE helper first and make backup its third caller; AHR goes through `createAhrSnapshot`. **Nested filesystems force the shape on BOTH backends:** recursive snapshot → **one archive root per dataset / subvolume, expanded by the runner at run time** from the single stored source (deterministic names, e.g. `data` + `data__photos`, within PBS's archive-name charset; the wizard says "N nested filesystems → N+1 archives"); a plain subdirectory source maps to `<dataset>/.zfs/snapshot/<s>/<relative>`; excludes apply per expanded root. *(GT: a single `btrfs subvolume snapshot -r` of `@data` leaves every nested subvolume as an EMPTY placeholder and `--all-file-systems` cannot rescue it — so per-subvolume expansion on AHR is a correctness requirement, not an option; same for ZFS children.)* The `backup2.2` matrix is explicit: nested snapshottable filesystems = expansion; non-snapshottable nested mounts = live-labeled or skipped, per the option. **Lifecycle:** transient `anas-backup-<task>-<ts>` snapshots are destroyed in `finally`, a stale-prefix sweep runs at every start (recursive), **no holds**. ⚠ **Replication's newest-common-snapshot discovery and Schedules retention must ignore the `anas-backup-*` prefix** — otherwise a transient becomes an incremental base and its destroy breaks the chain. *(GT: continuity is PROVEN — inodes are identical live vs `.zfs/snapshot/<s>/`, metadata mode reports the tree unchanged after the switch (`had to backup 0 B`), both directions; `.zfs/snapshot/<s>` is reachable with `snapdir=hidden` and is a real `findmnt` entry.)* **Result:** ONE `services/zfs-snapshot.ts` helper (Datasets, Schedules, Replication and Backup all use it), `backup-consistency.ts` (derived, `findmnt`-based, never stats), `backup-expansion.ts`, `backup-snapshots.ts`; transient `anas-backup-<task>-<ts>` lifecycle (finally-destroy on success/failure/timeout, own-prefix stale sweep, no holds, no property writes); prefix ignored by replication base discovery + lag and by Schedules retention (in-situ tests); root archive name + backup-id invariant (tested); consistency chip + expansion preview in the wizard; +84 tests, harness 154. **Live-proof needed:** the AHR runner path end-to-end on a pool with a nested subvolume (primitives unit-tested only); `--include-dev` under a `.zfs/snapshot` root (reasoned a no-op — we never emit it); and the wave-1 F3 sidestep — a snapshot root contains no live mounts, so the dead-remote-mount wedge should not reach the root archive (live archives unchanged).

##### Block images
**backup2.4** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want a backup archive of kind **`img`** beside `pxar` — a block device or a raw image file backed up as a fixed-chunk image (the client's `.img` type) — so an iSCSI LUN (iSCSI epic), either backing kind, is a source in the same task wizard, on the same timers, with the same prune/notify/dashboard. *(GT: a regular file is a first-class `.img` source — no loop device. Zvol: snapshot device via `snapdev=visible` (node appears in ~55 ms; `udevadm settle` anyway) → back up `/dev/zvol/<pool>/<vol>@<s>` (hard read-only, stable point-in-time) → restore the property with **`zfs inherit snapdev`** (a `set hidden` leaves `source=local`); image file: from the dataset/subvolume snapshot per `backup2.3`. **`--change-detection-mode` is a complete no-op for `.img`** — the wizard states that the mode does not apply and that **every run reads the full image** (unchanged run = `had to backup 0 B … reused 100%`, still a full read); upload and storage are 4 MiB-chunk-deduped. Backup of a live LUN is crash-consistent — say so.)* **Result:** `kind: pxar|img` (absent = pxar, byte-identical round-trips), `backup-zvol.ts` snapdev cycle with `zfs inherit` / exact-prior-value restore, `<name>.img:<path>` argv, no change-detection flag for img-only, `GET /v1/backup/lun-sources` (foreign/unresolved/PVE never offered; absent-device LUNs listed with `backingExists:false`), Kind combo + LUN picker + the two honest statements in the wizard, image stats in the notification; +69 tests, harness 220. Merge fix-up: the `unresolved` kind from `iscsi.5` joins `foreign` in the picker filter. Live proof owed: end-to-end zvol image run (publish → pbc → inherit → destroy), an image file on an AHR `@data` pool, lun-sources against the real target.

##### Restore
**backup2.5** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want a **desktop-grade directory picker** (the 16.9 known issue) — PVE's expanding-tree idiom with breadcrumb + type-ahead over `GET /v1/fs/browse` — with **two backends: the live filesystem (backup wizard) and a PBS archive (restore)** — so picking a path or a file to restore works the way a desktop does. *(A selector, not a content browser — the content browser was rejected and this does not reopen it. GT: the archive backend is **`catalog shell` over a pipe** (`ls`/`stat`/`find`, works on default AND metadata archives) — NOT a FUSE mount; the picker must show hardlink groups (`h` entries) as one unit, see `backup2.6`.)* **Result:** `12-picker.js` (`ANAS.pathPicker` tree + breadcrumb + type-ahead, `live`/`archive` backends, multi-select option; `ANAS.snapshotPicker`), the flat listbox deleted from the backup wizard; `services/backup-reads.ts` + `backup-catalog.ts` (`catalog shell` over a pipe — `ls` + batched `stat`, unquoted backslash escaping proven against real names with space/`*`/`[`/`]`, errors on stderr at exit 0), `GET /v1/backup/tasks/:name/snapshots`, `GET /v1/backup/repos/:name/groups`, `POST /v1/backup/restore/browse`; new real capture `catalog-shell-browse.txt` + GT-8a..i; +85 tests, harness +82. GT corrected: `ls` carries no type info. Live proof owed: the form pbc prints for a cross-directory hardlink target (bare vs archive-absolute — both handled, which is emitted is unverified) and `Modify:` timezone on a non-UTC node (carried verbatim).

**backup2.6** `[done 2026-08-26]` As a user, I want to **restore files from a PBS backup** — pick a task (or, for archives whose task was renamed/deleted, a repository + group), a point in time (`snapshot list --output-format json`, user-initiated; the caller composes `<type>/<id>/<RFC3339 time>` and **always passes the full timestamp — a bare group path silently restores the latest**), an archive, then **select anything from one file to the whole tree** with `backup2.5`'s picker; restore runs as a job via `restore --pattern …` (native extraction). **Target: a new directory beside the source by default; in-place is an explicit toggle** (→ `--allow-existing-dirs --overwrite`, the minimal in-place pair — `--overwrite` alone dies on the first existing directory; in-place is a MERGE, never a sync: foreign files survive); ownership/ACL/xattr toggles surfaced honestly (`--ignore-permissions` creates files `0600`); a space check from the manifest's `files[].size` and a **pre-flight write test on the target** (a read-only target fails only at the first file). *(GT, pattern contract: emit `/` + path with `\ * ? [ ]` backslash-escaped — unanchored patterns suffix-match at any depth, `[` unescaped matches nothing at exit 0, and a no-match pattern is a silent success, so the job verifies the restored set against the catalog and reports what did NOT restore; a hardlink's second name picked alone fails the whole job — restore hardlink groups together. **The FUSE route is rejected for browsing**: a black-holed PBS leaves readers in D state that `timeout` cannot kill and `stat -f` calls the dead mount healthy; `catalog shell` sidesteps it. The 409 confirm gate applies only to an in-place restore of a TREE; a single explicitly picked file restored in place is a checkbox (still needs `--allow-existing-dirs` when it sits in a subdirectory). LUN backing paths are protected targets, like PVE territory. Progress arrives on stderr as `\r` lines at a doubling interval (6/16/36/79 s) — the job reports "running" honestly between them. A killed restore leaves a partial tree with **no marker** — the job labels the side-by-side dir partial itself or removes it. An expanded archive whose nested filesystem no longer exists restores side-by-side with an explanation, never auto-creates datasets. Failure taxonomy captured: missing snapshot/group/namespace collapse to one message; PBS down = `Connection refused`; no-perm token has its own wording.)* **Result:** `services/backup-restore-files.ts` + the `case 'files'` dispatch; shared `restorePatternFor()` (proven against the GT name set), the side-by-side directory helper (dropped by the 2026-08-29 ruling — see backup2.10); preflight in order (LUN backing via `heldByLun()`, PVE territory incl. every `storage.cfg` path, real write test, space check with both numbers, confirm gate ONLY for in-place + directory); hardlink completion + tree detection + sizes from ONE batched `catalog shell` `stat` session (live-read on the stunt: a same-directory hardlink prints the BARE primary name — backup2.5's owed proof half-settled); post-restore `find -maxdepth 0` verification with `restored[]`/`missing[]`; partial marker; task-detail Restore… + toolbar Restore from repository…; +95 tests, harness +49. Deviations: no `prlimit` on restore (never profiled), no job cancel API exists (killed client = the partial path), hardlink-inside-a-selected-directory with an outside primary not covered. **Live proof owed:** an actual restore per mode, the partial marker on a killed client, `--ignore-permissions` → `0600`, cross-directory hardlink form, progress cadence.

**backup2.7** `[done 2026-08-26]` As a user, I want to **restore a LUN image from a PBS backup** — whole image, by nature — onto its zvol or image file, so a block device comes back to a point in time. *(GT: **`restore` refuses every existing target — file, zvol symlink, resolved `/dev/zdNN` — and `--overwrite` does not help.** The working paths are `restore … -` piped into the device, or `map` + `dd`; restoring to a fresh path yields a sparse file. **Size mismatch is unguarded and destructive below ANAS** — a larger image writes until ENOSPC and leaves the target half-overwritten, a smaller one leaves stale tail bytes — so ANAS compares the manifest's image size with the target's size and refuses any mismatch, stating both. ⚠ **The LUN must be disabled for the duration, not merely checked**: initiators (open-iscsi, Windows) auto-reconnect — disable the LUN/TPG, write the image, **preserve the unit serial** (`iscsi.2`), re-enable. Live-session 409 as the entry gate. `unmap` with no argument lists and force-cleans stale `map` devices — the sweep.)* **Result:** `services/backup-restore.ts` + the `POST /v1/backup/restore` `kind:'image'` branch (preflight in order with nothing destructive before it: installed/not-degraded → ANAS-owned LUN of kind zvol|file with backing present → **manifest size == target size** → live-session 409 → confirm gate), `CommandExecutor.execToStream` (stdout → caller-owned fd, `fsync`), TPG disabled under `withIscsiLock`, read-back of serial + attributes, re-enable in `finally` except after a mid-stream failure (target stays disabled; explicit enable = acknowledgement), progress from the client's doubling stderr lines, journald audit with bytes written; LUN toolbar "Restore from backup…" (repo → ns → `.img` groups → snapshot, two-call shape over backup2.5's endpoints, size mismatch disables Restore in red); +73 tests, harness +43. **Contract correction:** LIO has no per-LUN enable — the WHOLE target goes offline; disable + hard session 409 is the pair. Deliberately no door on the Backup grid (one menu per feature). **Live proof owed — nothing here has touched a real PBS or LIO:** the end-to-end zvol restore with an initiator re-login, `O_WRONLY` on a zvol node LIO serves, `fsync` on a block fd, the partial-write path, the AHR image-file branch, progress cadence on a long image, `--rate`.

### iSCSI — block storage *(new)*

> As a user, I want to serve block storage over iSCSI from an ANAS node — a zvol or a raw image file exported as a LUN — so physical machines, VMs and other clusters get block devices with the same snapshot, clone and PBS-backup story my file shares have. **PVE only consumes iSCSI (`iscsi`, `iscsidirect`, ZFS-over-iSCSI storage types); it never serves it. TrueNAS does.**
>
> **Boundary (ruled 2026-08-25):** ANAS is the **target side only** — a generic, standards-compliant iSCSI target. PVE (host `iscsi:` storage) and guests (an in-VM initiator) are ordinary initiators; ANAS never provisions for them, never writes `storage.cfg`, never shows a `pvesm` snippet. DESIGN §5a stands (you cannot share a zvol by path); only its "out of scope" parenthetical is superseded by this epic.
>
> **Engine:** LIO, the kernel target (`target_core_mod`, `iscsi_target_mod`, `target_core_iblock`, `target_core_file` are in the pve kernel) driven by `targetcli-fb` (+ `python3-rtslib-fb`, in the trixie repo, installed like samba/nfs). Persisted config = `/etc/rtslib-fb-target/saveconfig.json` (structured, restored at boot by `rtslib-fb-targetctl.service`); live state = configfs. Read model from JSON + configfs, writes via `targetcli` — **one command per invocation** (real exit codes; stdin batching always exits 0 and auto-saves a half-applied state) — then `saveconfig`; LIO itself keeps 10 rotating gzipped copies of `saveconfig.json`, which satisfies the config-backup ruling without a second rotator. CHAP secrets never ride argv: they are written straight to configfs (argv-free, round-trips through `saveconfig`). No new code where LIO already does the work. Ground truth: `docs/ISCSI-GROUND-TRUTH.md`.
>
> **Backing kinds (final):** **zvol** (ZFS only; the ZFS default) and **raw image file via `fileio`** (both backends; **AHR's only kind** — a file on the btrfs volume IS the AHR block object, parallel to a zvol on ZFS). LV-backed LUNs rejected (outside AHR's btrfs data model); qcow2 rejected (unique but low value).
>
> **Threat model:** reduce exposure by default — portal bound to a chosen address, explicit initiator ACLs, CHAP offered (mutual too), LIO's demo mode (`generate_node_acls=1`) never.

##### Dev (ground truth first)
**iscsi.1** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a dev, I want ground truth on the stunt node with `targetcli-fb` installed and real initiators (the node's own `open-iscsi`, a PVE `iscsi:` storage; no Windows VM available — open item). **Result: `docs/ISCSI-GROUND-TRUTH.md` (48 facts) + 32 real fixtures under `fixtures/iscsi/` (targetcli-fb 2.1.53, rtslib 2.1.76; a real reboot was part of the proof).** Headlines: `wwn` is a **create-only** parameter and the unit serial is what initiators (and **PVE volids**) pin — recreate without it = a different disk, with it = byte-identical identity, but **attributes do not carry across a recreate** (replay contract = `{serial, attributes}`); **fileio size is fixed at creation** (delete + recreate with `wwn=`); zvol grow is live; boot restore with a missing backing device **reports systemd success** while the LUN silently vanishes (a whole missing pool ⇒ target up with zero LUNs); the unit has no ZFS ordering (anchor = `zfs-volumes.target`); target create auto-adds a `0.0.0.0:3260` portal (`auto_add_default_portal`); shipped defaults are `emulate_tpu/tpws=0`, **fileio write-back ON**, discovery open (`demo_mode_discovery=1`), no CHAP secret-length validation; sessions live in `acls/<iqn>/info` (not `dynamic_sessions`); ZFS already refuses destroy/export of a claimed zvol but **rollback, rename, volsize shrink and `rm` of a backing file succeed silently**; `fuser`/`lsof`/holders see nothing — the claim is only in configfs; the backstore name is the SCSI model string (VPD 0x83); LIO binds and keeps a portal on a non-existent address with no error; `/v1/disks` reports a zvol and even a self-served LUN as `available`; the zvol's `zd*` name moved across the reboot — only `/dev/zvol/<pool>/<vol>` is stable. **Windows initiator proven 2026-08-29:** Windows Server 2025 (Microsoft iSCSI initiator, virtio NIC behind a Hyper-V vSwitch) against stock ANAS target defaults — closed discovery honoured once listed, ACL login, NTFS format, data, reboot, reconnect; no target-side change needed.

**iscsi.2** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a dev, I want the shared schemas and the read layer: `IscsiTarget` / `IscsiPortal` / `IscsiLun` (kind, backing, size, **serial**, attributes, mapped initiators) / `IscsiInitiator` / sessions, parsed from `saveconfig.json` + configfs (sessions from `acls/<iqn>/info`; `vpd_unit_serial` carries a `T10 VPD Unit Serial Number: ` prefix; IPv6 portal addresses are stored bracketed; `alias` fields are random per create and never identity); **ownership derived without shadow state** (backing path on an ANAS-managed pool/dataset + naming convention ⇒ ANAS; anything else ⇒ tagged hands-off, the 3.25 pattern; **PVE's `vm-NNN-disk-M` zvols are never candidates**); and `GET /v1/iscsi/*` (resource table added to DESIGN.md when this lands). **Serial persistence is designed in here:** every path that (re)creates a backstore — boot restore, fileio resize, image restore — replays **both the stored serial (`wwn=`, create-only) and the attributes**, because initiators, ESXi, Windows and **PVE's own volids** identify a LUN by the serial and attributes are dropped on recreate. Backstores reference `/dev/zvol/<pool>/<vol>`, never `/dev/zd*`. **Result:** shared `schemas/iscsi.ts`, `parsers/lio-saveconfig.ts`, `services/iscsi-configfs.ts` / `iscsi-ownership.ts` / `iscsi.ts` / `iscsi-health.ts`, `routes/iscsi.ts`; +103 tests; live-checked byte-identical against the stunt node's configfs; live-proof wave 1 PASS (restore hole caught: `missingLuns`, `degraded:true`, serials carried). Findings routed: IQN domainless form (→ convention corrected), file-LUN-on-exported-pool ownership flip (→ `iscsi.5`).

##### Volumes
**iscsi.3** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want **ZFS volumes first-class in Datasets**: listed with size / `volblocksize` / used, create (size, `volblocksize`, sparse), grow (live under a LUN — the initiator rescans), snapshot/destroy through the existing verbs, "Share this" disabled (no path), PVE-owned zvols tagged hands-off — so the ZFS block object is manageable where the rest of the dataset tree is. *(Image files are created by the iSCSI screen on a chosen dataset or AHR pool, not here. **Rename, volsize shrink and rollback of a volume under a LUN are gated by `iscsi.6`** — ZFS lets all three through silently. The Datasets dialog-contract harness must cover the volume type.)* *(Live-proof note: a volume's detail `properties` block reports filesystem-only keys (`recordsize`, `quota`, `atime`…) as `0`/`false` instead of omitting them — a contract wart for the detail window to fix when `iscsi.4`'s LUN detail lands, not a correctness bug.)* **Result:** volumes in the Datasets tree/dialogs, create (`-V/-b/-s`) + grow-only, `assertVolumeMutable` seam, `Resize Volume` toolbar button, contract harness +57 checks; live-proof wave 1 PASS (sparse create, grow, shrink 409 without confirm code, gating, snapshot, confirm-gated destroy).

##### The iSCSI menu
**iscsi.4** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want an **iSCSI** menu: targets (IQN, portals, LUN count, sessions, enabled) with per-target LUNs (kind, backing, size, serial, mapped initiators, connected-now) and initiator ACLs; **create target** (stable ANAS-generated IQN, editable once, immutable after — a "rename" is a new target; portal address picked from PVE's network config, IPv6 ULA allowed, link-local refused by LIO; explicit ACLs; CHAP optional, mutual optional, secrets write-only); **add LUN** (pick an existing zvol, or create a sparse raw image file of a given size on a dataset / AHR pool); enable/disable; delete — every mutation a job. *(GT-driven contract: **the auto-added `0.0.0.0:3260` portal is removed/prevented on create** (`auto_add_default_portal`) — it violates the threat model and its presence is conditional, so verify rather than assume; **discovery closed** (`demo_mode_discovery=0`) alongside `generate_node_acls=0`; CHAP lives on the ACL under explicit ACLs and secrets are written to configfs, never argv; **secret length 12–16 bytes enforced in the schema** (LIO accepts 1 char); attribute defaults ANAS sets on every backstore — `emulate_tpu=1`, `emulate_tpws=1`, `max_unmap_lba_count` raised, **fileio `write_back=0`** (LIO ships it ON — data loss on crash) with write-back behind a warning, `block_size` chosen before the LUN is mapped (immutable after); **the backstore name is the SCSI model string initiators see** — it is the user-facing LUN name, validated, not an encoded pool/vol path. `targetcli` is not transactional: one command per invocation, LIO mutations serialized behind a daemon mutex, each change a journald audit line; LIO's own 10 rotating `saveconfig` copies are the backup — ANAS adds no second rotator. **`pvesm`'s `iscsi:` plugin has no CHAP field** — the dialog says so when PVE is the intended initiator (ACL-only).)* **Result:** `services/iscsi-mutate.ts` + `routes/iscsi-mutate.ts` (one `targetcli` command per invocation as plain argv, daemon-wide mutex, `saveconfig` last and NEVER while degraded, auto-`0.0.0.0` portal removed by read-back, TPG attrs set, CHAP via configfs with a structural no-secret-on-argv guard, `wwn=<uuid>` + attribute order + block_size-before-map, file-kind resize = recreate with the same wwn AND attributes at the same index, `iscsiClaims()` + `GET /v1/iscsi/claims` for `iscsi.6`); `75-iscsi.js` menu (2.5k lines) with the PVE-`iscsi:`-has-no-CHAP note; +133 tests, harness 250. Deviations accepted: the grow-only rule for LUNs is enforced at the iSCSI route (file kind has no `Dataset` row) — `iscsi.6` adds the LUN-held branch inside `assertVolumeMutable` for the other direction; the portal picker reads PVE's `/nodes/<node>/network` like SMB Details (no `/v1` network endpoint exists — my brief was wrong). Fixed en route: LIO's `NULL` cleared-credential sentinel read as "set" (GT-35 amended). **Live-proof owed** (`iscsi.7`): every mutation end-to-end incl. the AHR-pool image path (GT open question 4). **Fix-up 2026-08-29:** the target dialog dropped every initiator/portal row but the last-added one (duplicate `itemId` per row → ExtJS keyed `items` replaced the earlier entry while the DOM still showed it); rows now carry unique ids; plus two hints on the initiator section (discovery closed until listed; every initiator sees all LUNs).

##### Boot, lifecycle, gates
**iscsi.5** `[done 2026-08-25]` As a user, I want targets to **survive reboot correctly**: `rtslib-fb-targetctl` ordered **after `zfs-volumes.target`** (and AHR activation) with the reverse at shutdown (target down before pools export); and — because **a restore with a missing backing device reports systemd SUCCESS while the LUN silently vanishes** (a whole missing pool ⇒ the target comes up enabled with zero LUNs; no `Restart=`/`OnFailure=` can catch it) — ANAS **diffs `saveconfig.json` against configfs** at boot and on demand, surfacing every hole as a dashboard warning (new `iscsi` category), and **never runs `saveconfig` over a degraded restore** (it would persist the hole). *(GT: the 1.2 s ordering margin observed on the stunt reboot was coincidence, not a dependency. Modules: rtslib loads every backstore plugin at once — there is no "load on first use".)* *(Live-proof wave 1, F2: a file-kind LUN whose pool is not imported reads `kind: foreign` and flips its ANAS target to `foreign`/hands-off — exactly the restore-hole state this story surfaces. **Add a third backing tier `unresolved`** (path resolves onto no known storage NOW) that never flips ownership — the ANAS IQN convention is authoritative for `anas`; only a backing that positively resolves onto PVE-managed or non-ANAS storage makes a target foreign. Schema enum + `classifyBacking`/`deriveOwnership` + tests.)* **Result:** deps in `install.sh` (+README), ordering drop-in `packaging/systemd/rtslib-fb-targetctl.service.d/anas-ordering.conf` (pinned by a test), `unresolved` tier + ownership rule (a no-LUN target is ANAS's — the old rule made a fresh target un-addable), `iscsi-warnings.ts` → `iscsi` dashboard category (missing LUN / target serving nothing / degraded / dead portal address), **repair = surgical replay, never `targetctl restore`** (rtslib `clear_existing=True` wipes the live tree — read from the node's own rtslib source); +60 tests, harness 295. **Open (candidate, needs a migration):** AHR pools have no static boot-ordering anchor (`nofail` fstab mounts) — `x-systemd.before=rtslib-fb-targetctl.service` on the AHR fstab line is the right mechanism. Live proof (`iscsi.7`): the reboot on a disk-backed pool, the shutdown half (`targetctl clear` vs pool export), AHR-backed fileio.

**iscsi.6** `[done 2026-08-26]` As a user, I want the rest of ANAS to **know a LUN is there**: ZFS already refuses `destroy`/`export` of a claimed zvol (`dataset is busy`) — ANAS turns that into "held by LUN <target>/<n>" (**`busy-diagnosis.ts` gains an LIO branch reading configfs `CLAIMED`/`udev_path` — `fuser`, `lsof` and `holders/` see nothing**); the operations ZFS lets through silently — **rollback, rename, volsize shrink, and removing a backing image file** — are refused under a LUN (rollback under a live session outright); deleting/resizing a LUN with a live session is a 409; **portal addresses are checked against live interfaces by ANAS** (LIO binds and keeps a portal on a non-existent address with no error, ever); a PVE firewall that would block 3260 is warned about, never edited; **the disk inventory excludes `zd*`** (loop devices already are) and tags an iSCSI-transport disk whose serial matches one of this node's own LUNs as hands-off (seam: the one filter in `parsers/lsblk.ts`). **Result:** `services/iscsi-held.ts` (`heldByLun`, per-request claim cache), refusals wired into datasets/pools/ahr-mutate/mounts routes BEFORE any destructive call (asserted on call logs; ZFS pool destroy/export added — a deliberate widening), rename = seam + alarm test (no endpoint exists), backing-file removal recorded absent (no ANAS path deletes files under a dataset — structural test), AHR has no unmount verb (destroy/change-mount are the only unmounters, both gated); `busy-diagnosis` LIO branch + dataset extraction; portal-address picker warning; `pve-firewall.ts` advisory; `zd*` filter + `iscsi-served-here` disk tag + one `isComposableDisk()`; tooltips on every gated button from the row's `heldByLun`; +125 tests, harness +65. ##### Post-live-proof (wave 2 findings)
**iscsi.8** `[done 2026-08-26]` As a user, I want a file-kind LUN whose backing file was ABSENT when LIO restored **never to be served** — so a filesystem that failed to mount, or mounted late, yields a visible hole and a Repair, not a silent empty disk with the right serial. *(Live-proof F2, HIGH: `targetctl restore` CREATES a missing fileio file at the persisted size because the mountpoint directory still exists; the LUN comes up ACTIVATED, health `degraded:false`, the initiator reads zeros — and the only signal points the wrong way (the target flips to `foreign`). Reproduced unprompted when a power cycle dropped the AHR disks. Not AHR-specific: any late/failed mount under a fileio path.)* **Design (decided 2026-08-26):** (1) **Stub detection** in `iscsi-health.ts` — a fileio backing is a stub when its size is 0 against a persisted `size` > 0, OR its containing mount (`findmnt` longest prefix) is not the ANAS dataset / AHR pool the path belongs to (the parent filesystem is holding a placeholder); reported as `stubLuns[]`, `degraded:true`, and a critical `iscsi` dashboard card; a stub LUN is classified `unresolved`, never flips the target to `foreign`. (2) **Quarantine** at daemon start and on every health read, under `withIscsiLock`: unmap that LUN only (siblings keep serving — LUN-level, not TPG-level), delete the stub backstore, delete the 0-byte file ONLY when both stub signals agree, leave the persisted record untouched (no `saveconfig`) so the existing diff shows `missingLuns` and **Repair** (iscsi.5) recreates it with the same serial + attributes once the filesystem is back; journald audit line. (3) **Prevention where the mount is ours:** the AHR fstab line carries `x-systemd.before=rtslib-fb-targetctl.service` — written at AHR pool create, and added surgically (the fstab round-trip parser) to an existing pool's line the moment a LUN is placed on it (promotes the §4 candidate; existing pools without LUNs are left alone — guest philosophy). (4) `deploy-anas.sh` installs the ordering drop-in like `install.sh` (F14). (5) A target ANAS left DISABLED (e.g. after a failed image restore) shows an `iscsi` warning card naming why (F12). (6) Datasets and the iSCSI door agree: a zvol GROW under a live session is allowed in both (live, initiator rescans); a file-kind resize under a session stays refused (recreate) (F13). (7) A volume destroy refused by ZFS because snapshots exist gets the guiding message + the existing confirm door, not the raw ZFS text (F15). Live-proof: unmount the AHR pool / a child dataset → restart the restore service → stub quarantined within one health read, card shown, initiator gets NO disk; mount → Repair → data back, serial same; power-cycle the VM with the AHR disks detached → same outcome; the fstab ordering keeps a normally-booting AHR pool ahead of the target service (journal timestamps). **Result:** `services/iscsi-stub.ts` (ONE `fileStubVerdict` shared by read layer, health, repair), `iscsi-quarantine.ts` (detect → re-detect under the lock → `luns delete` + `backstores/fileio delete`, file removed only when BOTH signals agree, never `saveconfig`; at daemon start, every health read, warnings collection, ahead of repair), `IscsiHealth.stubLuns[]`/`disabledTargets[]`/`IscsiMissingLun.stubBacking`, AHR fstab `x-systemd.before=` at create + surgical `addMountOption()` when a file LUN lands, `deploy-anas.sh` drop-in, zvol grow under a session allowed on both doors, `destroyNeedsRecursive()` guidance; +40 tests. **All live-proven on the stunt** (F2 reproduced on the old build; quarantine at daemon start; unmount+restart → card, LUN-level unmap, initiator sees 3 of 4; mount → Repair → same serial, byte-identical data; ZFS child-dataset variant; fstab ordering on a normal boot by dependency; F12/F13/F15). Known limit: when the whole AHR pool is absent there is no expected mount, so only the size signal fires — the LUN is unmapped but the 0-byte file kept (both-signals rule); the durable card says PLACEHOLDER. GT: `luns delete lunN` removes that LUN's MappedLUNs from every ACL; a by-hand `mount` of an fstab entry creates a collectable mount unit (test-procedure trap).

(F9) a DISABLED backup task never reads `lastRunResult: success` / `never` from a run history systemd garbage-collected — status derivation says `disabled` and the detail states that history is not retained while disabled (the units-are-the-store ruling gains this caveat); a live archive still walks the dead mount.)* **Result:** F7 ownership filter reuses `deriveOwnership`; F9 = `deriveRunResult` in the ONE `systemd-status.ts` helper (replication's byte-identical private copies deleted), `lastRunResult: 'disabled'` as an additive enum member + `statusNote` on backup/schedule detail + muted pills in all three grids; Epic 18 ruling wins over the story wording). +28 tests, harness +12. Rider still OPEN: an ENABLED never-run task still reads `success` (unit `Result=success`, no timestamps) — only the disabled case is fixed.

##### Backup screen reshape (operator's hands-on round, 2026-08-27)
**backup2.9** `[done 2026-08-27]` As a user, I want a backup task to be **either files or block**, chosen first, with two distinct wizard panels — so LUN backups stop being a special archive inside a file task. **Design (agreed 2026-08-27):** task-level `kind: files | block` (absent ⇒ derived: all-pxar archives = `files`; exactly one `img` archive = `block`; mixed = `files` with a legacy note; no unit is rewritten until edited). **Block panel = ONE LUN per task**: the LUN picker (name, target, size, derived consistency) and nothing else — no path field (the pick IS the path + `lun` record), no excludes, no nested option, no change-detection (a no-op for images). Files panel = today's archive list. Common tail = repository/namespace, cadence, retention, notifications. **Identity from the serial (option A):** a new block task's `backup-id` is `lun-<unit serial>` and its archive name is fixed (`disk`) — the PBS group IS the LUN and survives rename/resize/restore; the human LUN name is display only, read live. **Names for people, derived at creation, not edited:** file archive names derive from the path's last segment (auto-suffixed on collision), shown, not editable; **existing archives are never re-derived** — the archive name is part of what metadata change-detection keys on, so a rename re-reads everything (the 10 TB question) — derive only at creation, keep stored names verbatim on edit. **Kind inference from the door:** the LUN toolbar's Back up… opens the wizard with `block` preset and skips the choice; from the Backup menu the choice is explicit. Grid: a Kind column + the LUN name for block tasks; the LUN badge/door (b75bb9e) matches by `lun` record or by the serial in the backup-id. Existing LUN backups (the operator's first, taken 2026-08-26) keep their stored ids and groups. **Result:** `925ad7c` — `BackupTask.kind` + `effectiveTaskKind` (derived when absent), block-task validation (one `img` archive `disk`, `backupId lun-<serial>` verified against the read layer), shared `deriveArchiveName` (creation-only; a stored name is never re-derived — `anasNameNew` gate in the wizard, pinned four ways in the harness), kind step / block panel / files panel / legacy note / Kind column, LUN door skips the step, badge matches by serial; existing LUN tasks keep their ids. +12 daemon tests (2836), harness 771. Built by a LOCAL agent in three contexts: daemon half (clean), UI half (compacted — died mid-edit), finish (fresh context, 18 min) — the 'build in one context, finish in another' pattern.

**backup2.10** `[done 2026-08-27]` As a user, I want to **restore elsewhere**: files to a chosen new directory (the dialog offers *original place* / *new location* explicitly — the machinery exists since backup2.6), and a LUN image **as a NEW LUN** — pick a target, a name, and a backing of ANY kind (zvol on a ZFS pool, or an image file on a dataset or AHR pool); ANAS creates the volume/image at the manifest's size, a backstore with a **new serial** (a copy is a new disk — two LUNs with one serial is a corruption waiting to happen), maps it, and streams the image in — the live LUN untouched, no offline window, the new LUN's group starting fresh. This is also the sanctioned way to convert a LUN's backing kind (zvol ⇄ image) via PBS. *(A same-serial "Move LUN" — copy, swap the mapping under the same identity in one short offline window — is a later verb, not this story.)* **Daemon half done 2026-08-27 (`21e14c4`):** files `target.mode: newLocation` (must not exist, created, no overwrite flags); image `target: {mode:'newLun', targetIqn, name, backing}` — backing created at the manifest size (zvol or sparse image), backstore via the ONE shared `createAndMapLun` step (`addIscsiLun` is now a wrapper) with a fresh UUID serial + standard attributes, mapped at the next free index, streamed via `execToStream`, `saveconfig` last; mid-stream failure undoes LUN + backstore + backing with no `saveconfig`; the source LUN is never disabled or written. +33 tests (2869). **UI half open** — the restore dialogs' *original place / new location / new LUN* choice. **UI half done (`475ab39`, DeepSeek-V4-Flash calibration job — one context, 35 min, no compaction):** files restore offers side-by-side / in place (merge, gated for trees) / new location (path + Browse, must not exist); LUN restore offers this LUN (in place, byte-identical body, no `target` key) / a new LUN (target combo of ANAS-owned targets, name validated like Add LUN, backing kind reusing the Add LUN pickers, size read-only from the manifest — a *known-size* gate, not equality); daemon refusals surfaced verbatim; result panel shows the new LUN's identity. Harness +56 (827). Live proof owed: a real restore-as-new-LUN on the stunt (both backings) and a files restore to a new location. **Unified Restore dialog (2026-08-28):** one `Restore…` dialog behind every door (LUN toolbar, task grid, task detail, repository) that asks only what the selection does not already know — files or LUN follows the archive kind; the separate LUN-restore dialog in 75-iscsi.js was removed. **Fix-up 2026-08-29:** the LUN toolbar's Restore door is no longer dead under a live session — only the *This LUN* (in-place) destination is, with the live reason; *New LUN* stays available (the zvol-resize parallel: a safe path must not be blocked by the gate for the destructive one). — the verdict is one helper (`ANAS.iscsi.lunInPlace`) applied by the dialog to the LUN it resolves, so every door (toolbar, task grid, task Details, repository) says it before the daemon's 409 does. **Ruling 2026-08-29:** `sideBySide` dropped entirely (no established backup tool has it as a mode — follow the patterns operators already know); destinations are *Into the original* (merge) and *Somewhere else* (path required; an existing directory is a confirm gate, then merge). Live-proven on the stunt node 2026-08-30: 409 confirm→merge with same-name overwrite + decoy survival, fresh-dir create, non-directory 400, inPlace, dropped-mode 400 at the boundary.

### Self-heal — userspace AHR repair *(new)*

> **Epic intent:** a userspace repair path where the btrfs csum arbitrates an md
> reconstruction on AHR arrays — md knows parity groups, btrfs knows what the data
> should be. The epic holds the ground truth (`selfheal.1`), the loop-device suite
> every implementation must pass, the scrub attribution that names the corrupt
> files, the repair engine, the operator-triggered repair job and the live proof.
> Each is a SEPARATE authorization — a shipped story here authorizes nothing after
> it, and the attribution half stands on its own whether or not a repair is ever
> built. Two boundaries hold across every story: the repair is never a read-path
> heal, and it is never automatic — an operator asks for it, on a named file, with
> the outcome reported in honest buckets.

**selfheal.1** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a dev, I want ground truth for a userspace AHR self-heal repair path (btrfs csum arbitrating md reconstruction) on loop devices — result: docs/AHR-SELF-HEAL-GROUND-TRUTH.md. Rig + stages in `test/self-heal/gt/` (00–08, loop devices only, disposable); 13 verdicts on kernel 7.0.14: GT-7 PROVEN — naive repair through md at default `rmw_level=1` restores the data block AND poisons parity (`mismatch_cnt` stays 8; failed-member stripe read returns exactly 1 wrong 4K block); GT-8/GT-11 PROVEN — `rmw_level=0` + XOR-of-other-members candidate + crc32c arbitration vs the stored csum leaves the array clean (`mismatch_cnt=0`, snapshot cold read matches, scrub 0 errors, failed-member stripe bit-identical); GT-12 PROVEN for RAID6 (kept-original write under `rmw_level=0`; P-member failure fully correct via Q; same poison at default rmw); GT-5/GT-6 PROVEN (md check sees below-md rot, blind to through-md rot); GT-13 PROVEN (`sync_max` trap: check suspends at the knob, the knob persists across re-runs). Kernel facts recorded: scrub dmesg names the 64K stripe + path but no csum values and not the failing 4K; filefrag "physical_offset" is the btrfs logical bytenr (chain needs the chunk-tree hop, per-chunk delta); stored csum leaf is readable through md and equals crc32c LE.

**selfheal.2** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a dev, I want the **loop-device suite** that every repair implementation must pass — grown from the `selfheal.1` drill scripts under `test/self-heal/suite/`, run on the stunt node, one PASS/FAIL report. Cases: (1) parity trap — corrupt a data block on a member → repair → fail a DIFFERENT member → every sibling block in the stripe reads back correctly; (2) zero-block mapping — rot in/adjacent to a zero-filled region of an image-like file: a wrong mapping onto a healthy identical-content block must ABORT ("not corrupt here"), never "succeed"; (3) compressed-extent cold read — `compress=zstd`, page cached BEFORE the rot: the end-to-end read must go cold through the fresh snapshot; (4) knob restore after an exception injected at EVERY step boundary, then a full md check covers the whole array again; (5) above-md corruption (rot written THROUGH md) → pre-check diagnosis, not a failed repair. **Harness rules (binding):** the injector locates bytes by a raw signature scan of the member files sharing NO code with the mapping helper (GT: on a zeros file the scan also hits the parity slot — disambiguate by a one-byte member flip + cold btrfs read, never by the formula); every case has its NEGATIVE CONTROL in the same run (parity trap with `rmw_level` default MUST break sibling blocks; compressed case must first show the warm-cache success/snapshot EIO pair); parity cases on RAID5 (6 members) AND RAID6 (7 members) with the P member among the failed-member variants. The repair under test is a pluggable command (`REPAIR_CMD <mountpoint> <file> <block>` → exit 0 repaired / 2 unrepairable / 3 diagnosed-above-md / 4 mapping-abort), default = a reference python implementation of the converged sequence (pin → re-resolve+re-verify at the computed member location → pre-check → rmw_level=0 → reconstruct+arbitrate vs the csum tree → read-back guard → O_DIRECT write+fsync → post-check → cold read via snapshot → finally restore rmw_level/sync_min/sync_max + destroy snapshot); GT-5 rule: a bounded check SUSPENDS at sync_max — wait for `sync_completed == sync_max`, write `idle`, then restore. A suite that can pass without detecting anything is worse than none.

**Result:** Suite at `test/self-heal/suite/` — `run-suite.sh` (dev-box entry:
rsync the suite to the stunt node, run it over ssh, pull the report back),
`suite.py` orchestrator (fresh RAID5 6×200 MiB / RAID6 7×200 MiB loop rigs
per run, torn down after), `cases.py`, `common.py` (md geometry from sysfs +
btrfs-tree mapping + bounded checks), `oracle.py` (the injector: raw
signature scan of the member devices, flip-test data/parity disambiguation,
no code shared with the mapping helper), `repair-ref.py` (reference repair
implementing the converged sequence), `README.md` (REPAIR_CMD contract +
node notes). 28/28 cases and 9/9 negative controls green on kernel
7.0.14-12-pve (mdadm 4.4, btrfs-progs 6.14); report committed as
`LAST-RUN.md`. Ground truth beyond the drill: md's stripe cache serves
recently written or recently checked stripes to bounded checks and degraded
reads alike — a check over a cached stripe reports `mismatch_cnt=0` while the
member block is junk, so `bounded_window_check` evicts first (shrink
`stripe_cache_size` — floor 17, lower values rejected with EINVAL — then
sweep ±200 stripes while small; at the default 256 a sequential sweep never
recycles the target's slot because released stripes are reused LIFO); with
the stripe cache-stale, a default-`rmw_level` write-through reads
pre-corruption bytes from cache and leaves parity untouched (the GT-7 poison
mechanism the negative control asserts); `mismatch_cnt` finalizes shortly
after `sync_action` returns to idle; and compressed extents carry one crc32c
entry per on-disk sector keyed contiguously from the extent's logical start
(verified: stored == crc32c of the blob sector), so the repair unit for a
compressed extent is the whole on-disk blob. Reference repair covers
data-member repair on RAID5 and RAID6 (including failing the P member after
the repair, verified through Q reconstruction), single-blob-sector compressed
repair, zero-block mapping abort, above-md diagnosis; documented not-covered
(all exit 2): RAID6 P/Q-member corruption, multi-sector rot inside one
compressed blob, NOCOW/prealloc extents. Hardened 2026-09-11: chunk size read
live (F4), RAID1 case 6 + control; reference and engine both PASS (34/34
cases, 12/12 negative controls). Two-band hardening 2026-09-13 (review finding
R1 — a repair that placed every block with the FIRST segment's geometry
passed every single-band rig, because a single-band LV has one segment and one
geometry, so the suite could not catch it): a third rig in the AHR pool shape
(`00-rig-twoband.sh`: two md bands as one VG's PVs, one LV spanning both in
band order, 64K vs 512K chunk on purpose, the LV's segments taken from the dm
table) with case 7 — corrupt a SEGMENT-2 (band B) marker block, the oracle
scans both arrays, the repair must exit 0 on band B's own geometry — and the
R1 assertion, run regardless of the repair's exit: no DATA write on band A
(the repair's own snapshot commits a btrfs transaction whose superblock and
metadata writes can all sit in segment 1; the superblock mirrors' positions
proved unstable across identical mkfs runs, so the suite measures them with a
tx-probe — the repair's own transaction, run before the baseline — and a
probe block inside a DATA chunk fails the suite rather than blinding the
assertion); plus the segment-1 negative control (both segments reachable).
Reference PASS (41/41 cases, 14/14 negative controls); the engine's record
(34/34, 12/12) predates this rig and it has not been re-run against it.

**selfheal.3** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a user, I want the **AHR scrub to name what is corrupt**: the scrub job result and its PVE notification list the affected files and the exact failing 4 KiB blocks, instead of an `Error summary` count. *(GT-3/GT-4: kernel 7.0 scrub lines in the kernel journal carry `logical`, `physical`, `root`, `inode`, `offset`, `length`, `(path: …)` for the 64 KiB stripe — no csum values, not the failing 4K; the exact block is found by 16 `O_DIRECT` reads inside the named stripe (EIO = bad); `root N` is a subvolume id → `btrfs inspect-internal subvolid-resolve`.)* Read `journalctl -k -o json` bounded to the scrub's own window (text `MESSAGE` parse — accepted where no structured alternative exists, same as `btrfs scrub status`); group per file; cap the list (first 200 files + total); `uncorrectable`/read errors without a path are reported as unattributed. Schema widening on `AhrScrubResult` only (`findings[]`), shared Zod; the Scrubs UI shows the findings on the finished scrub in ONE place; the existing warning notification carries the paths. No repair here — this story is useful alone (the August "scrub file-path attribution" candidate). Fixtures from `test/self-heal/gt/out/03-dmesg.txt` + `04-gt6-dmesg.txt`. **Result:** `services/ahr-scrub.ts` reads `journalctl -k -o json` bounded to the scrub's own window (`-S`, local wall clock stamped BEFORE `btrfs scrub start`) and parses the kernel `MESSAGE` text — the sanctioned exception, beside `btrfs scrub status` — for the GT-3 shape: `(device dm-N)`, `logical`, `on dev`, `physical`, `root`, `inode`, `offset`, `length`, `links`, `(path: …)`. Lines are filtered to the pool's own dm device (realpath of the LV; fail-open when it does not resolve) so another pool scrubbing at the same time is never attributed here; `root N` is resolved once per id through `btrfs inspect-internal subvolid-resolve` (cached) and then taken RELATIVE to the subvolume the pool actually mounts — read live from the mount through `subvolFromMountOptions`, the same helper the topology's `subvolLayout` comes from, with `subvolLayout` itself as the fallback when the option cannot be read: a §12 pool mounts `subvol=@data` AT the mountpoint, so `@data/f1.bin` is `<mountpoint>/f1.bin` (and `<mountpoint>/@data/f1.bin` exists nowhere), a nested `@data/photos` keeps its remainder as `<mountpoint>/photos/…`, and a flat pool's resolved subvolume stays a directory under the mountpoint. A scrub covers the WHOLE filesystem, so a finding under `@snapshots` is real and expected and has no path under the mountpoint: it is reported filesystem-relative with `outsideMount: true` and never probed, distinct from `missing` (the file is gone) — both say what they are in the window and the notification; the failing 4 KiB blocks come from 16 `dd … iflag=direct` reads inside each named 64 KiB stripe (non-zero exit = bad block), run BETWEEN the two phases so nothing reads the array while an md check does. Stripes group per `root:inode`, and the same stripe reported twice counts once. Caps and honesty fields: the list carries the first 200 FILES (`truncated` past that) while `errorsAttributed` keeps counting every stripe, `errorsReported` is the sum of the `Error summary` counters, and `unattributed` counts the path-less errors (`unable to fixup …`, read/super) deduped against the logicals already attributed — the kernel rate-limits its scrub warnings, so reported ≠ attributed is stated, not hidden. A path deleted since the scrub (or an unresolvable subvolume) comes back `missing: true` with no probe rather than a failed job, and an unreadable journal or a failed probe degrades to no attribution — never to a failed scrub. Schema: `AhrScrubResult` + `AhrScrubFinding` (`missing`, `outsideMount`) + `AhrScrubStripe` in `packages/shared/src/schemas/ahr.ts`, every new field optional (an old result still parses), validated at the daemon boundary before the result leaves. The existing warning notification is the same single call with a richer body (up to 20 paths with their bad-block counts, then "…and N more", plus the counts line). UI: ONE place — the Scrubs row. A real scrub outruns the Run now's job-poll budget by hours, so the row recovers the findings from the daemon's own `GET /v1/jobs?status=completed` (filtered in the view by operation, pool and has-findings, newest per pool — no new endpoint, no filter the route lacks): an AHR pool's Last scrub cell says "N files with checksum errors" and opens `anas-win-scrub-findings` (full paths, never truncated, bad-block count per file, block list in the tooltip, the reported-vs-attributed line above). That queue is in memory, so the cell says "last completed scrub since the daemon started" in those words — ANAS keeps no scrub history of its own, and the notification is what survives a restart. A running pass still outranks the cell, and an unreadable job list costs the indicator and nothing else. Fixtures are the drill's verbatim kernel lines, kept as `packages/daemon/src/fixtures/ahr/scrub-dmesg-gt3.txt` and `-gt6.txt` because `test/self-heal/gt/out/` is produced on the node and never committed. +30 daemon tests (3020 pass) and +29 dialog-contract checks (1028 pass); lint and typecheck clean.

**selfheal.4** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a user, I want the **periodic AHR scrub to be the whole two-phase scrub**, not the md half — today the Schedules toggle flips mdadm's node-global `mdcheck` timers only (parity, checksum-blind, ten days of 6-hour windows on pve5), and the phase that can attribute (`selfheal.3`) is manual. **RULED 2026-09-11 (operator, three rounds):** (a) *sequential, always* — one scrub, two phases, never severable, neither alone sufficient: **phase 1 = md parity check** per band, strictly sequential (§4), continuous under md's own `speed_limit` throttling (no daily window), then **phase 2 = btrfs checksum scrub + attribution + notification**, immediately after; the manual Scrub button runs the SAME order (flip it from btrfs→md to md→btrfs; one job, one code path). (b) **ANAS owns md checks on this node**: enabling periodic scrub for any AHR pool DISABLES the `mdcheck_start`/`mdcheck_continue` timers (never double-schedule; anything not nailed down by PVE is ours); disabling the last pool leaves them off (the operator asked for no scrubbing). An md array in `/proc/mdstat` that is not an AHR band is reported in the state `note` as "not scrubbed by ANAS", never silently. (c) **One node-level timer** `anas-scrub.timer`/`.service` (the snapshot-schedule unit pattern: embedded JSON = `{ kind: 'ahr-scrub', cadence, pools: [...] }`, `Persistent=true`, a runner beside `dist/snapshot-task.js` that POSTs `/ahr/<pool>/scrub` for each enabled pool IN SEQUENCE — AHR pools on one node share spindles (pve5: four bands on the same disks), so pools never scrub concurrently); the per-pool toggle adds/removes the pool from that list; **cadence** node-level, `monthly` (default, 1st Sunday 03:00 — matches PVE's ZFS cron and today's mdcheck) or `quarterly` (1st Sunday of Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct); when the list empties the units are removed. (d) **Surface it as two phases**: the Scrubs row for an AHR pool shows "phase 1 parity (md) → phase 2 checksums (btrfs)" with the next run; the job's progress names the phase; a `mechanism`/`note` that stays honest (mdcheck still enabled outside ANAS ⇒ "double parity check — mdcheck is on" warning). (e) **Notifications tell one story**: a phase-1 mismatch says "rot exists in <array>; phase 2 (running now) will name the files"; phase-2 findings name the files (`selfheal.3`), and the `11.17` md hook's "run a Scrub" advice is reworded to match. Rejected: `btrfsmaintenance` (journal-only, no attribution); split calendars (two-week gap bought nothing). Contract harness covers the toggle, cadence and row wording. The operator's decision point (bring workloads down, run Repair) is the notification, never an automatic repair. **Result:** `services/ahr-scrub.ts` runs the two phases in the ruled order — phase 1 checks each band's md parity strictly sequentially (labels from realpath at point of use, PENDING-band waits unchanged), then reads `/sys/block/<md>/md/mismatch_cnt` once the check is idle (+1 s, injectable for tests, fail-open to silence) and warns "rot exists in <band> — phase 2 (running now) will name the files" through the PVE notification system when it counts > 0, before phase 2's btrfs scrub + `selfheal.3` attribution runs exactly as before; the manual Scrub button takes the same path, so there is one scrub and one code path. The node-level units live in `services/scrub-schedule-units.ts` beside the snapshot-schedule unit store: `anas-scrub.service` carries the `X-ANAS-Schedule=` marker (`{ kind: 'ahr-scrub', cadence, pools[] }`, zod-validated on read, never adopted from a foreign file) and `ExecStart=/usr/bin/node /opt/anas/packages/daemon/dist/scrub-task.js <pools…>`; `anas-scrub.timer` is `Persistent=true` with `OnCalendar=Sun *-*-01..07 03:00:00` (monthly) or `Sun *-01,04,07,10-01..07 03:00:00` (quarterly). The runner `src/scrub-task.ts` (same shape as `snapshot-task.ts`, socket client, no shell) POSTs `/v1/ahr/<pool>/scrub` per pool IN SEQUENCE, polls each job to a terminal state before the next, skips a 404 pool ("no longer exists") with a journald line, and continues past a failed pool's job — one band's rot never costs the other pools their scrub; it exits 0 only when every submitted pool completed. The toggle (`PUT /v1/scrub/ahr/:pool`, now accepting an optional `cadence` — the only API change) edits the embedded schedule: enable adds the pool (units created when absent) and disables `mdcheck_start`/`mdcheck_continue` (best-effort — a failed disable is a journald line, never a failed job with both mechanisms on); disable removes the pool and the units go away when the list empties, mdcheck staying OFF. `readAhrScrubState` reports the pool honestly: enabled = in the list AND the timer enabled, `mechanism: 'anas-scrub-timer'`, node-level cadence, `nextRun` from `systemctl show -p NextElapseUSecRealtime` (null when off or unreadable), `phases: ['md-parity','btrfs-checksums']`, `lastScrub` still null (md keeps no completion record), and a `note` that names a still-enabled mdcheck ("double parity check — mdcheck is on") and any `/proc/mdstat` array that is no AHR band ("<mdN> is not an ANAS pool and is not scrubbed by ANAS"). Shared schema widened additively (`ScrubCadence`, `AhrScrubPhase`, `nextRun`, `phases`, `AhrScrubToggleRequest`, `AhrScrubSchedule`) — an old UI against a new daemon still parses. UI: the AHR row renders "phase 1 parity (md) → phase 2 checksums (btrfs); next <date>" with the "(md keeps no completion record)" caveat, the toggle confirm says the node-level scope and that mdcheck will be turned off, and the cadence selector (monthly/quarterly) sits in the existing toolbar — no new menu, no new window. `packaging/anas-md-event.sh`'s two "run a Scrub" advices now say the periodic scrub's phase 2 will name the files (its bash suite 56/0). The iscsi.6 file-deletion seam test whitelists `scrub-schedule-units.ts` beside `snapshot-schedule-units.ts` (it unlinks only the two unit files it rendered). +28 daemon tests (3048 pass), +14 dialog-contract checks (1042 pass), lint and typecheck clean.

**selfheal.5** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a dev, I want the **repair engine** as a daemon service — the shared mapping helper (btrfs logical → chunk-tree hop with per-chunk delta → dm table → md layout from sysfs, never hardcoded) and the converged sequence — passing `selfheal.2` as its `REPAIR_CMD`, on RAID5, RAID6 and RAID1 bands; refuses while degraded / resyncing / reshaping / pvmove; skips extents without checksums (NOCOW, prealloc). **Result:** Five daemon services and one dev entry point, no routes and no UI (that is selfheal.6). `services/selfheal-map.ts` is THE mapping helper both halves of the repair derive from: file block → btrfs logical bytenr (EXTENT_DATA from the file's own fs tree, read against the LV and never the md device — every tree offset read through md is shifted by the dm segment, and `filefrag` lies outright on encoded extents) → the COVERING chunk's own delta (GT-2; the rig's second data chunk is 60,358,656 off the first) → the dm linear segment (parsed for real, so a multi-band LV is a concatenation and not an assumption, and a non-linear target is refused by name) → member and offset from md geometry read live on every call, including each member's OWN `rd<n>/offset`. `left-symmetric` only; any other layout is refused with its name rather than mapped by a formula that does not describe it. The repair unit is the 4 KiB block when uncompressed and the whole on-disk blob when compressed, and every sector of it is placed INDEPENDENTLY through the full chain rather than assumed contiguous on one disk. **Every btrfs lookup is BOUNDED** — `services/selfheal-btree.ts`: `dump-tree -t 7` on an 80 TB pool is tens of gigabytes through the executor's 10 MB buffer, which is not a slow path but no path at all, so the engine never dumps a tree. It takes every tree's root from ONE `dump-tree -r` (624 bytes on the rig; a few hundred more per snapshot) and then reads one 16 KiB node at a time with `dump-tree -b <bytenr>`, descending into the child whose key is the greatest one ≤ the key it wants — so a chunk, csum or extent lookup costs tree-height bounded execs (1–2 on the rig, 4–5 on an 80 TB csum tree) no matter how big the pool is, and each leaf it reads is cached for the rest of the run. btrfs's symbolic key names are turned back into the numbers btrfs sorts by, and an unrecognised name is REFUSED rather than placed by guess: a key mis-ordered by one position answers confidently from the wrong subtree. `services/selfheal-csum.ts` reads the stored crc32c out of the EXTENT_CSUM tree through the LV (kernel 7.0 prints no csum in its scrub warning — GT-3), keyed by the greatest item start ≤ the logical byte so a compressed blob's entries are found across csum-item boundaries; crc32c is 40 lines of table lookup, no dependency, checked against the published vectors AND against the csum btrfs itself stored for a real captured block. `services/selfheal-repair.ts` runs the converged sequence — gates → pin → resolve → reverify → precheck → `rmw_level=0` → reconstruct → arbitrate → read-back guard → O_DIRECT write through md → postcheck → cold read through the pin snapshot → restore everything — with each md knob read before it is written and put back in a `finally` on every path out, including the stripe-cache size the bounded check shrinks to 17 to evict with. `services/selfheal-io.ts` is the low level all of them share: raw block I/O goes through `dd` (+ `base64` on the executor's pipeline stage for the bytes), because O_DIRECT needs an aligned user buffer and Node gives no way to allocate or even observe one — probed live, a `Buffer.alloc(4096)` read succeeded by luck of the 8 KiB Buffer pool while `allocUnsafeSlow`, `new ArrayBuffer` and every size ≥ 64 KiB failed EINVAL. **The pin is an AHR snapshot, not a private one:** a §12 pool goes through `ahr-snapshots.ts` — `listAhrSnapshots` for the stale-prefix sweep, `createAhrSnapshot` into `@snapshots/anas-selfheal-<ts>`, the cold read through `withTopLevelMount`, `deleteAhrSnapshot` in the `finally` — the same machinery and the same mount discipline a backup run uses, so there is one snapshot path on an AHR filesystem and not two. A file inside a NESTED subvolume is pinned by snapshotting that subvolume, because a read-only btrfs snapshot does not recurse (GT-52/55). `withTopLevelMount` serialises on one path per pool, so the gates REFUSE when that mount is already held — a backup in flight — rather than silently blocking behind it for hours. A flat pool (and a hand-built loop rig, which is what the suite runs on) has no `@snapshots` to put anything in and falls back to an in-place read-only snapshot of the file's own subvolume; both carry the same backup2.3 lifecycle and both name a leftover in `cleanupErrors` rather than swallowing it. **Covered:** RAID5 XOR, RAID6 P-XOR, RAID6 Q-syndrome reconstruction as the fallback when the P-based candidate fails arbitration (GF(2^8), poly 0x11D, coefficient `g^d` over the data disks in md's stripe order — the convention was verified live against a 7-member rig before it was written: computed P and Q matched the array's own P and Q members byte for byte on three stripes, and the Q-only solve reproduced a data member exactly), RAID1 bands (candidates are the other legs, torn reads retried 3×), and compressed extents. **`unrepairable` by design in this cut:** an extent with no stored csum (NOCOW, prealloc, `nodatasum` — nothing to arbitrate against); an INLINE extent, whose bytes are in the metadata tree and not in a data chunk; more than one bad on-disk sector inside one compressed blob; two bad blocks in one stripe (the candidate then fails arbitration, which is the point of arbitrating); a post-check that does not come back 0. Corruption that arrived THROUGH md is `above-md`, never "repaired", and the wording stays "implicates something other than the disks". Refusals (degraded, `sync_action` not idle, mid-reshape, an unwritable `array_state`, a pvmove in `dmsetup table`, a held top-level mount) report `unrepairable` with a `refused:` reason and touch nothing. Shared Zod in `packages/shared/src/schemas/selfheal.ts` (`SelfhealOutcome` + `SelfhealMapping`, `SelfhealStep`, `SelfhealDiagnostics`), validated at the boundary before the outcome leaves. `bin/selfheal-repair.ts` is the dev/test entry the suite drives (`node dist/bin/selfheal-repair.js <mountpoint> <file> <block>`, exit 0/2/3/4 + 70 injected + 1 internal, JSON on stdout, the suite's snake_case sidecar under `REPAIR_REPORT`); it resolves the pool from the live topology, and `REPAIR_FAIL_AT` is read THERE and never in the service, which takes a `beforeStep` hook and knows nothing about environment variables. **Suite: `SUITE: PASS (28/28 cases, 9/9 negative controls)`** with `REPAIR_CMD` pointing at the deployed engine on the stunt node, kernel 7.0.14-12-pve — report committed as `test/self-heal/suite/LAST-RUN-engine.md` beside the reference implementation's `LAST-RUN.md`. One suite fix rode along: `run-suite.sh` never actually forwarded `REPAIR_CMD` across the ssh boundary although its README said it did, so no alternative implementation could be run through it at all; `REPORT_NAME` keeps the two records apart. +98 daemon tests (3118 pass) against fixtures captured verbatim from two live rigs into `packages/daemon/src/fixtures/selfheal/` (provenance noted there) — the whole chain asserted against the selfheal.2 Python mapper's independent answers, the csum reader against the filesystem's own stored values, crc32c against published vectors, the GF(2^8) vectors against poly 0x11D (not AES's 0x11B, whose table would answer plausibly and be wrong for every syndrome md ever wrote), the tree walk against every node along one real path per tree from a rig filled until the csum and fs trees went multi-level (including a target past the last separator key of a node, and the inline extent the engine must refuse), the §12 pin branch — which the suite's flat rigs cannot reach — against the AHR snapshot verbs, and the knob restore with a failure injected at every one of the twelve step boundaries. Lint and typecheck clean.

**selfheal.6** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a user, I want **Repair from parity** on a scrub result with findings: a confirm-gated job (409 + `X-Anas-Confirm-Code`), per-file outcome in three honest buckets — repaired / unrepairable (restore from backup; e.g. two bad blocks in one RAID5 stripe, candidate fails arbitration) / diagnosed above md (parity already agreed with the bad data — "implicates something other than the disks", never a certainty) — and the outcome through the PVE notification. Operator-triggered only; never automatic; never a read-path heal. **Result:** ONE new endpoint, `POST /v1/ahr/:name/repair` (`routes/ahr-mutate.ts`, added to the `AHR-DESIGN.md` §4 table — AHR's API has never lived in `DESIGN.md`, whose only tick is the confirm-gate list). The body is EXPLICIT — `{ files: [{ path, blocks }] }`, shared Zod `AhrRepairRequest`, the exact files and 4 KiB block indexes the operator ticked out of the selfheal.3 findings — because this writes through md and what it writes over is named by the caller, never by the daemon's own idea of what is bad. Everything unsafe is refused BEFORE a confirm code exists: any pool state but healthy and idle (each of degraded / building / rebuilding / expanding / scrubbing / offline / failed / readonly answers in its own words), a scrub or another repair in flight on the pool, and a backup or snapshot job holding the pool's §12 top-level mount — the engine refuses that last one too, but a repair that blocks silently behind a backup for hours is worse than one that says why, so the route says it at the door. A path that is not a live file under the mountpoint is refused by name rather than skipped: the two ways a finding fails that are exactly the two flags it carries, `outsideMount` (a real, expected corrupt block inside `@snapshots/…` — filesystem-relative, and repair works on the live `@data` tree in this cut) and `missing` (deleted since the scrub). There is no per-pool AHR mutation lock in the daemon — `withTopLevelMount` covers the §12 snapshot mount and nothing else — so scrub and repair refuse each other at SUBMIT through the job queue's own `findByOperation` record, in both directions, with no shadow state and an honest "nothing in flight" after a restart. The confirm warnings say what actually happens: a read-only snapshot taken and removed, md's `rmw_level`/`sync_min`/`sync_max`/`stripe_cache_size` changed for the duration and restored, one 4 KiB block per finding written THROUGH md only after the reconstruction matches the stored checksum, nothing else on the array touched, and a block that cannot be proven left exactly as it is. The job (`services/ahr-repair.ts`) runs the engine strictly one block at a time, progress naming file + block + outcome, and an engine run that THROWS becomes that block's `unrepairable` with the error as its reason — the remaining blocks still get their attempt, and only something outside the loop can fail the job. `AhrRepairResult` carries per-file, per-block entries plus the three counts that always add up to the blocks attempted: `repaired`, `unrepairable` (a `mapping-abort` counts here and keeps its own "not corrupt here" reason), `aboveMd`. ONE PVE notification at the end — `warning` if anything is unrepairable or above md, `info` if everything repaired — with the per-file lines capped at 20 + "…and N more", the counts, "restore this file from backup" and "parity already agreed with the bad data — this implicates something other than the disks (memory, controller, software)", which stays an implication. UI: the selfheal.3 findings window and nowhere else (no new menu) — checkbox selection over the findings, a row with no live file under the mountpoint unticksable with the reason in its own Repair column, `Repair from parity` lit only by a repairable selection, the standard `ANAS.confirmAndRun` code flow, a poll budget raised past the 15 s default, and the job's result rendered back into the same window (per-file outcome on its row, the three counts, and an honest "still running — the outcome arrives as a PVE notification" when the budget ends first). +20 daemon tests (3138 pass) and +34 dialog-contract checks (1062 pass); lint and typecheck clean. Not live-proven — that is selfheal.7.

**selfheal.7** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a dev, I want the arc **live-proven** end to end on the stunt node through the real API (scrub → findings → repair → clean scrub), then rehearsed once on a disposable band of a real AHR pool with an injected fault, before any release carries it. **Result:** `docs/LIVE-PROOF-SELFHEAL.md` — the whole arc driven against the real daemon on its unix socket on the stunt node (kernel 7.0.14-12-pve), on a pool ANAS created itself: AHR-1, one RAID5 band over four 2 GiB spares, §12 subvolume layout (`subvol=@data` mounted, `@snapshots` beside it) — the pin path the selfheal.2 loop rigs never exercise. 512 MiB spares are unusable: §2.5 floors every disk to a whole GiB, so a sub-GiB disk bands to nothing. Rot was injected and every answer verified by the SUITE's own `oracle.py` (raw signature scan of the member devices, no layout math) and `common.py` (md geometry, O_DIRECT, snapshot cold reads, `mdadm --fail`/`--re-add`), never by ANAS's own mapping. **Proven:** the two-phase scrub in the ruled order with the phase-1 "rot exists in sh7-r1 — phase 2 (running now) will name the files" warning and the phase-2 body naming the file and its bad-block count; `findings[]` carrying the right file, `subvolume`, stripe and the exact 4 KiB block on a `@data` pool AND inside a NESTED subvolume (`@data/photos` resolved, remainder kept under the mountpoint); `errorsReported`/`errorsAttributed`/`unattributed` honest;
