the findings recovered from `GET /jobs?status=completed`; the confirm gate (409 + `X-Anas-Confirm-Code` + the five warnings) and the 202 replay; `repaired` with the member block byte-identical to the original, `mismatch_cnt` of that stripe 0 under a hand-run evicted bounded check, a fresh scrub reporting nothing and the file's sha256 unchanged; the GT-7 parity trap NOT sprung — with a DIFFERENT member failed, all 2048 blocks of the repaired file read back bit-identical, then `--remove`/`--re-add` back to clean; `above-md` for rot written THROUGH md, with nothing written and only ONE notification (md's check is blind to it, GT-6); a COMPRESSED zstd extent REPAIRED (single-sector blob, csum `0xf0449da8` = the kernel's `0xa89d44f0` little-endian); every refusal verbatim — repair during phase 1 (pool state) and during phase 2 (the job-queue record), scrub during a repair, repair during a repair, a `missing` path, an `outsideMount` path, and a degraded array; and the periodic path end to end — units with the `X-ANAS-Schedule=` marker, `OnCalendar` rewritten by `cadence`, mdcheck taken over (`enabled enabled` → `disabled disabled`) and left off when the last pool is disabled, both units removed, `nextRun` + `phases` in `GET /scrub`, a foreign `md126` named in the `note`, and `systemctl start anas-scrub.service` producing a real `ahr.scrub` job under the runner's own identity. Pool destroyed through the API (confirm-gated), spares detached and their images deleted; the node is back to its baseline. **Findings: F1** (MEDIUM, FIXED) every ANAS PVE notification with a non-ASCII character was double-encoded UTF-8 in the delivered mail — perl hands `@ARGV` over as bytes and PVE encodes the body again, so an em dash arrived as `â€"` in every message the product sends; `pve-notify.ts` now decodes `@ARGV` (`FB_DEFAULT`, so a bad sequence costs a glyph and never the notification). **F2** (LOW-MEDIUM, FIXED) a repair whose blocks all came back `mapping-abort` — "not corrupt here", nothing written — still told the operator to "restore this file from backup", which is advice to overwrite good data; `ahr-repair.ts` now counts the aborts separately for the notification tail and gives them their own sentence (no schema change; the three buckets are untouched). **F3** (MEDIUM, OPEN) a COMPRESSED file's finding carries `badBlocks: []` — the kernel's scrub warning reports `offset` relative to the compressed extent (0), not to the file, so the probe reads the wrong 64 KiB; the engine repairs the extent correctly when handed the block by hand, but `AhrRepairRequest` needs at least one block, so the Scrubs window's Repair action cannot reach a compressed file at all, and the notification reads "0 bad 4K block(s)" for a real corruption. Not fixed — reading a whole multi-TB file is unbounded and saying it honestly needs a schema field and UI copy; proposed as `selfheal.8` below. **F4** (observation) the suite's `bounded_window_check`/`evict_stripe_cache` hardcode the loop rig's 64 KiB chunk (128 sectors) and are refused EINVAL on an AHR band's 512 KiB chunk — suite-internal, ANAS's engine reads `chunk_size` live. Both fixes shipped with tests (+3 daemon tests, 3169 pass), were re-deployed and re-proven live; lint and typecheck clean. **Not covered:** the story's second half — the rehearsal on a disposable band of a REAL AHR pool with an injected fault — is operator-only (no fleet node was touched) and still gates a release.

**selfheal.8** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a user, I want a scrub finding on a **compressed** file to name its bad blocks — or to say plainly that it cannot (selfheal.7 **F3**). Kernel 7.0's scrub warning gives `offset` relative to the compressed extent, not the file, so the `selfheal.3` probe reads the wrong 64 KiB and the finding comes back with an empty `badBlocks` — which the schema's own doc reads as "the file was repaired, rewritten or removed", while the file is in fact corrupt. Detect the compressed case (the extent covering the named logical is `encoded`), resolve the extent's real file range from the extent item rather than from the kernel's `offset`, probe THAT range; when a block still cannot be named, carry a flag the findings window renders as "corrupt, block not identified (compressed extent)" with Repair disabled and the reason stated, instead of an empty list that reads as nothing found. No engine change — `selfheal.5` repairs a compressed extent correctly once it is handed the block (live-proven). **Result:** re-captured ground truth on the stunt node before coding (fixtures/PROVENANCE.md, "Compressed-extent attribution fixtures"): the scrub warning's `logical` is the 64 KiB STRIPE containing the failing blob — two corrupt blobs 16 KiB apart both reported at the same stripe start — and its `offset` is extent-relative (0) where the read-time `csum failed` lines carry the true file offset. Attribution now resolves ownership through the kernel's own route: `parseTreeRoots` gains the extent tree root, and the engine's mapping helper (selfheal.5, single source of truth) grew `extentsForStripe` — walk the extent tree for EXTENT_ITEMs intersecting the stripe, read each matching data backref (`root/objectid/offset`), then one bounded fs-tree hop per owner to the EXTENT_DATA item with its real file range. A compressed finding probes THAT whole file-block range with the existing O_DIRECT probe and reports `badBlocks` plus the additive `compressed: true` / `extentBlocks {first, count}`; Repair stays enabled and passes the extent's first block (the blob is the repair unit, live-proven in selfheal.7). When nothing can be named — extent unresolvable (`mappingError`), no extent of the file covers the stripe, or nothing failed on re-read — the finding sets `unidentified: true` with a stated `reason` ("corrupt, block not identified (<reason>)"), never an empty `badBlocks`; the findings window renders the compressed extent with its block count (Repair enabled) and the unidentified case muted with Repair disabled and the reason as tooltip, and the notification says "compressed extent, N blocks" / "block not identified (<reason>)" per file. Fixtures verbatim from the stunt-node rig (extent tree, subvol leaf with 16 zstd EXTENT_DATA items, kernel scrub + csum lines); +10 daemon tests and 2 harness findings (F compressed, G unidentified; 1084 checks), 3179 daemon + 48 gateway pass; lint and typecheck clean.

**selfheal.9** `[done 2026-09-11]` As a user, I want **Repair from parity reachable from the Scrubs grid toolbar as well as from the findings window** — **RULED 2026-09-11 (operator): both, gated by need.** A `Repair` toolbar action on the Scrubs grid acts on the selected AHR pool's last completed scrub findings (the same `GET /jobs?status=completed` recovery selfheal.3 uses) and opens the same findings window with the repairable rows preselected; both the toolbar action and the in-window button are enabled ONLY when at least one repairable finding exists (not `missing`, not `outsideMount`, not `unidentified`), disabled with a tooltip saying why otherwise. No new window, no new menu; contract harness covers enablement in both places. Resolves the selfheal.6 judgment call (actions on the grid toolbar vs a display-only detail window): the toolbar is the standing home, the window keeps its button because the selection is made there. **Result:** ONE predicate (`repairableFinding`) is the whole enablement rule — the window's per-row Repair column and both buttons are lit by it and by nothing else. The toolbar `Repair` (`anas-btn-scrub-repair`, beside Run now / Stop) is lit only for an AHR pool whose last completed scrub holds at least one repairable finding; otherwise it is greyed with the reason ON the button ("select an AHR pool" / "no scrub findings for this pool since the daemon started" / "findings cannot be repaired from here: …" with the window's own per-row reasons counted by kind), and a click opens the SAME findings window with the repairable rows already ticked, so the operator only confirms what the toolbar established. UI only — no daemon change, no new window, no new menu.

**selfheal.10** `[done 2026-09-14]` As a user, I want **Rewrite parity** for the one case ANAS can prove safe: a band whose md check counted mismatches while every file's checksum passed (parity-only rot; the design review's "thing that loses the data"). **RULED 2026-09-14 (operator): "if the probe passes, correcting the parity is the sane and safe thing to do"** — gated on GT-18 proving that a stripe-bounded `mdadm --action=repair` rewrites parity correctly when data is intact and BLESSES rot when data is not. Design: a confirm-gated job (409 + `X-Anas-Confirm-Code`) per band, offered from the Scrubs row's parity-mismatch indicator (`selfheal` reporting fields `parityMismatches[]`), NEVER automatic. Preconditions, all re-checked at submit AND immediately before the md write: (1) the LAST completed two-phase scrub of this pool has `parityMismatches` on this band AND zero data findings anywhere on the pool (phase 2 clean — the data is intact as far as checksums can say); (2) the array is clean, idle, not degraded/recovering/reshaping, no foreign sync op; (3) no scrub/repair job active on the pool. Sequence: a FRESH btrfs scrub first (phase 2 re-run; any finding aborts — data rot must be repaired by `selfheal.6` before parity is touched, because md repair would bless it) → `mdadm --action=repair` on the band (whole band: md counts mismatches, it does not say where) → wait idle (own-op only, never a foreign op) → md check → `mismatch_cnt` must read 0 → notification with the before/after counts. Warnings must state: parity is recomputed from the data as it is now, so any file WITHOUT a checksum (NOCOW, preallocated) would have its rot blessed — ANAS creates none, but a hand-set `chattr +C` is outside its protection; the run reads every member of the band (hours on large arrays; per-band duration estimate from GT timings). Standing rule refined: "never run md repair" stays for the data-rot case; this verb is the one exception and the code path must make the two indistinguishable-to-md cases distinguishable-to-ANAS (phase 2 clean = the proof). Suite: a P-member-rot case (GT-18 shape) with the negative control (data-member rot → refused, never repaired by this verb).

**Result:** `services/ahr-parity-rewrite.ts` + `POST /v1/ahr/:name/parity-rewrite` (one band per request, 409 confirm, 202 job `ahr.parity-rewrite`). The proof is read from the pool's last COMPLETED scrub job (`JobQueue.findLastCompleted`): that scrub must have counted a parity mismatch on THIS band and found no data corruption anywhere — otherwise a hard 409 with `reason` `no-parity-mismatch` / `data-findings-present`, `array-busy` (band degraded, busy, mid-reshape, or its sync window still bounded from an interrupted repair — GT-13's trap) or `job-active` (the scrub/repair/rewrite exclusion, now mutual in all three directions). Every one of them is re-taken inside the job immediately before the md write, because a scrub takes hours and a member can fail in them. The job then runs a FRESH btrfs scrub of the pool — the extracted phase-2 pass from `ahr-scrub.ts`, one scrub, two callers — and ANY finding aborts as `refused: data corruption found; repair data first (selfheal.6)` with the scrub's summary and its attributed files in the result; only then `mdadm --action=repair` over the WHOLE band (md counts mismatches, it does not locate them), a wait that writes nothing, `mdadm --action=check` over the whole band, and `mismatch_cnt` must read 0 — `rewritten`, else `still-mismatched`, never a claim. Both ops run with `sync_max` at its default so no window is narrowed and no `idle` is ever written; an operation md took of its own ends the run with every knob as md left it (`foreign-sync-op`, D2/`ownsSyncOp`). ONE PVE notification carries the before/after counts (`info` on `rewritten`, `warning` otherwise). The confirm warnings state that parity is recomputed from the data as it is now, that the fresh scrub aborts the run on any finding, that a hand-set NOCOW or preallocated file has no checksum and would have its rot blessed, and the per-band duration estimate with its assumption stated (band height at a deliberately conservative 60 MiB/s per member — the GT rigs are loop files and say nothing about disks). Suite case 8 (`test/self-heal/suite/`, its own RAID5 rig because the verb scrubs the whole filesystem): P-member rot in GT-18's shape → the verb rewrites, and the case independently re-proves GT-18(d) — bounded check 0, file still MATCH, parity row == XOR of the data rows; the negative control puts the rot on a DATA member and the verb must exit 3 with `data-corruption-found`, the refusal proven on disk (the stripe still counts the mismatch and the parity row is byte-identical to its pre-refusal digest — `last_sync_action` alone is not enough, since a repair-then-check sequence would leave it unchanged too); a third control runs the verb with no evidence at all and it must exit 3 with `no-parity-mismatch` having issued no md action. `PARITY_CMD` contract beside `REPAIR_CMD`, dev entry `packages/daemon/src/bin/selfheal-parity.ts` (`<mountpoint> <band>`; `--assume-mismatch` is dev-only and bypasses the evidence gate with a hardcoded count, `--evidence <file>` supplies the bounded-check proof the suite produces, and with neither the entry refuses). Run on the stunt node with the ANAS verb: `LAST-RUN-parity.md`. **UI shipped** (same round, once the `parityMismatches[]` reporting field landed): the Scrubs row's parity-mismatch indicator IS the door — clicking it opens `anas-win-scrub-parity`, which lists the bands md counted mismatches on (label, md array, count) and takes ONE, then the confirm-code flow, then the run's result — `mismatchBefore`/`mismatchAfter` and the outcome by name, with `still-mismatched` said as "not proven good" and a `data-corruption-found` refusal naming the fresh scrub's errors — in that same window. The verb is greyed with the daemon's own reason when the same scrub named corrupt files ("repair those from parity first — rewriting parity now would make the rot permanent"), and the handler refuses even if clicked. One seam was fixed with it: the scrub wrote `band` as the label `<pool>-r<n>` while the rewrite's evidence gate matched a number, so the row now carries `bandIndex` alongside the label (`AhrScrubParityMismatch`) and both sides read the same field rather than a regex over the label. +31 dialog-contract checks.

> **Ruling 2026-09-14 — uninstall and mdcheck (reverses round-2 F2):** the operator: a node left with NO parity check "is not helping or guarding". mdadm's `mdcheck` timers are ENABLED BY DEFAULT on a stock node (SCHEDULES-GROUND-TRUTH), so re-enabling them on uninstall RESTORES THE DISTRO DEFAULT — the right guest behaviour, not a guess about prior state. `uninstall.sh` re-enables `mdcheck_start.timer`/`mdcheck_continue.timer` whenever it removed an `anas-scrub` timer, prints that it did and how to disable them again (spindown operators). Same for the per-pool toggle turning OFF the last pool: mdcheck comes back on (the node returns to stock), with the note saying so.

**selfheal.11** `[done 2026-09-15]` As a user, I want a **mirror band's mismatch to be repairable** — the decision tree (2026-09-14, F1) found the R9 root has no reachable verb. **GT-22 (2026-09-14) reshaped the design:** (1) md's read-balance is NOT contractual — on the rig it served the rotten leg to btrfs on every cold read, and a plain btrfs scrub then SELF-HEALED the band through md (`corrected_errors: 1`, both legs good after: btrfs re-reads on a csum failure, md serves the other leg, scrub writes the good block back through md to BOTH legs) — so for a mirror the ordinary phase-2 scrub is an opportunistic heal, and repeating it raises the odds; the residual case is a rot md never serves. (2) `mdadm --re-add` of a failed-but-present member is a SILENT NO-OP (rc 0, no resync, rot survives) — the only resyncing verb is `--fail` → `--remove` → `--add` (full leg copy, ~6–7.5 s/GiB measured ⇒ a 20 TB leg is a ~35–40 h DEGRADED window). (3) `mdadm --action=repair` on a mirror copies LEG 0 blindly in both directions (blesses rot on leg 0) — never offered. (4) a degraded check cannot see leg rot (mismatch 0 with one leg out). **RULED 2026-09-14 (operator): APPROVED, two arms, cheapest first; and md's own `repair` is BLOCKED for mirrors so damage is never cemented — no ANAS code path may issue `--action=repair` on a RAID1 band (the parity-rewrite verb's `not-a-parity-band` refusal is that invariant; the mirror verb, the reconcile hook and any future verb must carry it; the decision tree's mis-applied rule encodes `md-action-repair` under a RAID1 root as a violation; the mirror notification and window say plainly not to run it by hand). IN THIS RELEASE (0.3.2 — operator 2026-09-14: all three rulings ship together). Design:** arm A "scrub until clean" — with `parityMismatches` on a RAID1 band and phase 2 clean, run the btrfs scrub again (bounded, e.g. up to 3 passes; each `corrected_errors > 0` is progress) then a bounded md check; residual mismatch ⇒ arm B. Arm B "compare legs, arbitrate by checksum, write through md" — NO degraded window: read both legs directly row by row (same IO as a check), for each differing row map the md byte back to the btrfs logical (reverse chunk hop — deterministic) and look up the stored csum (data: csum tree; metadata: the node's own header csum); write the passing leg's row through md to both legs; rows with no checksum (free space, NOCOW) are reported, never written; every step reuses selfheal-map/csum/syncop. The fail-one-leg scrub (GT-22 b/c) stays as the documented manual procedure for an operator who prefers md's own copy, with the degraded-window cost stated; it is NOT the ANAS verb. Suite: a RAID1 case for arm A (fresh rot healed by repeated scrub) and arm B (rot md never serves — inject on the leg read-balance avoids, or force it by failing/re-adding... no: construct by corrupting the leg that a prior cold read did NOT hit), negative control = md repair on the rig blesses leg-0 rot.

**Result:** `services/ahr-mirror-reconcile.ts` + `POST /v1/ahr/:name/mirror-reconcile` (one band per request, 409 confirm, 202 job `ahr.mirror-reconcile`), beside Rewrite parity in the Scrubs parity window — exactly one of the two verbs can ever be lit for a band, and the other says why it is dark. The proof comes from the pool's newest COMPLETED scrub OR repair (`JobQueue.findLastCompleted`): it must have counted a mismatch on THIS band, recorded the band as `raid1`, and left no data unrepaired anywhere — otherwise a hard 409 (`no-mirror-mismatch` / `not-a-mirror-band` / `data-findings-present`), and a row from a daemon too old to record the level is refused rather than guessed at. The other hard 409s are `bad-blocks-present`, `array-busy` (degraded, mid-sync, or a sync window GT-13 left bounded) and `job-active`, all re-taken immediately before arm B writes anything. **The invariant is a wrapper, not a convention:** every command the service issues goes through `mirrorGuardedExecutor`, which throws on `mdadm --action=repair` before the process is spawned, on all three executor doors — and the suite asserts the same thing against the KERNEL's log (`md: repair of RAID array mdN`), so it holds for any implementation. Arm A repeats `btrfsScrubPass` (up to 3) with a whole-band `--action=check` after each, reading its progress from the `Corrected:`/`Uncorrectable:` breakdown `btrfs scrub status` prints (captured on the node, btrfs-progs v6.14); a pass that corrects nothing while md still counts ends it. Arm B reads both legs in 4 MiB windows at each leg's own data offset, and for each differing 4 KiB row maps the md byte back through `logicalForMdByte` (new, in `selfheal-map.ts`, with `readAllChunkItems` — the chunk tree is read once because nothing in it is keyed by device offset) and arbitrates: DATA against `readStoredCsum`, METADATA/SYSTEM against the containing node's own `verifyNode` (cached per node), free space and csum-less rows counted and left exactly as they are, both-legs-fail reported as `unresolvedRows` and never written. The winner goes through md, which writes both legs. Unlike the parity rewrite, a fresh scrub's UNCORRECTABLE finding is NOT a refusal — it is named in the result and notification and the rest of the band is still reconciled, because this verb writes no row it cannot prove. One PVE notification per run (`info` only when the band came back clean and nothing was lost), and it repeats what must not be run by hand. Suite cases 9a (arm A), 9b (arm B, `--passes 1`) and 9-neg (both legs rotten → `residual`, `unresolvedRows: 1`, nothing written) on a RAID1 rig of their own, with `MIRROR_CMD` + `mirror-ref.py` (compare-legs only) and the dev entry `bin/selfheal-mirror.ts`; decision-tree root R9 grew from 39 nodes / 7 terminal leaves to 62 / 20, all of them carrying code AND a test, with no `md-action-repair` reachable from it.

**selfheal.12** `[done 2026-09-15]` As a user, I want to know when btrfs silently repaired a **metadata (DUP) copy** — GT-20 showed the RW mount fixes a rotten metadata copy from its mirror and the scrub reports nothing; the only evidence is the kernel's `read error corrected` line. **RULED 2026-09-14 (operator): approved, but NOT a fishing expedition — no standing watcher.** ANAS looks only in the two windows it already reads: the two-phase scrub's journal window (the selfheal.3 attribution pass already runs `journalctl -k` bounded to the scrub, filtered to the pool's dm device — count the corrected-read lines there) and a repair job's own window (its cold read can trigger a correction). Result: additive `metadataCorrected: { count, device? }` on `AhrScrubResult` / `AhrRepairResult`; one sentence in the existing notification ("btrfs corrected N metadata read(s) from the mirror copy during this scrub — a member is returning bad metadata; watch its SMART"); Scrubs row tooltip. No new verb, no writes. **[done 2026-09-15]** Result: one parser (`parseCorrectedRead`/`correctedReadsFromMessages`, tolerant of `info`/`warning` and the trailing parenthetical) serves both windows — the scrub reads its window at the END (so the run's own probe reads are inside it) and the repair reads its own; the sentence rides whichever notification the run emits, and a clean scrub whose only signal is the count earns a standalone warning (the exact GT-20 shape); Scrubs row tooltip shows the count. Decision tree R5 leaves regenerated.

> **Ruling 2026-09-14 — preset fragment (decision-tree F7): DECLINED.** Re-enabling the mdcheck timers ANAS took over requires an explicit `systemctl preset` by the sysop (GT-21: package upgrades do not); that is a deliberate action, and ANAS shipping a preset to override a distro default crosses the guest boundary. ANAS notes it and no more: the existing state note ("double parity check: mdcheck is on") plus one journald line at daemon start when the toggle owns mdcheck but the timers read enabled. No automatic re-disable. (Implemented: the note is emitted from the daemon-start reconciliation as a journald warning, pure-decision tested; it disables nothing.)

> **Candidate (not a story):** a journal watcher for btrfs checksum errors on AHR pools at READ time (a user hits EIO on a share before any scrub runs) → the same PVE warning as the scrub. Cheap; demand-gated.

### Identities — issue #60 fixes *(0.3.2 / 0.3.3)*

**identity.1** `[done 2026-09-14]` As a user, I want share users and groups to work the way the system underneath allows (GitHub #60): (a) **mixed-case names** — the identity name schema accepts what `useradd` accepts (`[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\$?`), UI validation mirrors it; (b) **SMB password presence is matched case-insensitively** — Samba treats names case-insensitively and may store a different case than passwd, so the Share Users list compares passwd and passdb names folded; (c) **no user-private group** — share users are created with `useradd -N` and the default group, so no phantom same-named group appears (existing users keep theirs; the list explains a private group when one exists); (d) **delete verbs** — `DELETE /v1/identity/users/:name` and `DELETE /v1/identity/groups/:name`, confirm-gated (409 + `X-Anas-Confirm-Code`) with warnings naming the shares/ACLs that reference the identity and the files it owns (ownership is NOT changed; the uid/gid stays on disk), refusing a group that is any user's primary group or a user referenced by a share until the reference is removed; `smbpasswd -x` for a user with a passdb entry; UI Delete buttons on both grids; DESIGN.md API table rows. Tests at both boundaries + harness. *(Reporter's 4 symptoms map to a–d.)* **Result:** `IdentityName` schema widened to `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\$?` (max 32) with the UI `NAME_RE` mirrored (create dialogs validate before send); shared case-fold helpers `sameIdentityName`/`passdbHas` in `parsers/pdbedit.ts` drive `smbEnabled` and the enable/disable gate (passwd vs passdb, so `Alice`+`alice` → present); create now runs `useradd -N -M -s /usr/sbin/nologin` (no user-private group) and `GET /identity/groups` marks an existing user-private group with additive `privateGroupOf` (exact name+gid match) that the UI labels; the two DELETE verbs share the local-identity validation of the update routes, then hard 409s (`referenced-by-share` via an `smb.conf` `valid users` scan, `primary-group-in-use`) with no confirm bypass, then the confirm gate (`identity.user.delete`/`identity.group.delete`) and a 202 job running `userdel` (no `-r`) + `smbpasswd -x` only when a passdb entry exists, or `groupdel`; UI Delete buttons are need-gated (local selection required) and flow through `confirmAndRun`, refreshing both grids on acceptance. Schema + route + job + case-fold unit tests, dialog-contract harness section for the name rule, private-group label, and both need-gated delete flows.

**identity.2** `[done 2026-09-20]` *(0.3.3)* As a user, I want **deleting a share user that has an SMB password to succeed** (GitHub #60 follow-up, 2026-09-20): the delete job ran `userdel` before `smbpasswd -x`, and smbpasswd resolves the passdb entry through the Unix account, so every SMB-enabled user failed with "Failed to find a Unix account" after the account was already gone, leaving an orphaned passdb entry (recovery: `pdbedit -x -u <name>`, noted in the 0.3.3 release notes). Fix: drop the SMB entry first, then `userdel`, so a failed SMB step destroys nothing; the unit test's argv-order assertion flips with it. Second defect: the user and group delete handlers pass only `onComplete`, so a failed job never reloads the grid (the create handler already reloads on `onFailed`) — both delete handlers reload on failure too. Coverage: the integration spec deletes an SMB-enabled user through the UI and asserts `getent` and `pdbedit -L` both no longer list it (the gap that let this ship: unit tests can't model the smbpasswd→passwd dependency). **Result:** commit on main; 3540 daemon tests; found along the way: the user/group delete handlers set a confirm-window class without `confirmWindow: true`, so the gate rendered the plain Yes/No box and the class never applied — now the widget window like pool/dataset/share/LUN delete (harness contract updated). **Live-proven on the stunt node 2026-09-20:** a failing `smbpasswd -x` (stand-in binary) → job failed with the smbpasswd message, account and passdb entry both still present; normal delete → `{deleted, smbEntryRemoved: true}`, `getent` and `pdbedit -L` both empty; Playwright share-users spec 3/3 twice consecutively including the UI delete step.

---

## 0.3.4 → 0.4.2 shipped — moved verbatim from `docs/EPICS.md` §3 on 2026-10-06 (releases 0.3.4, 0.3.5, 0.4.0, 0.4.1, 0.4.2; 2026-09-22 → 2026-10-06)

> Moved at the 0.4.2 retrospective (its ruling (h): the retrospective closes the cycle). Rulings that outlived these stories are in `docs/EPICS.md` §2; the 0.4.2 rider pools.r1 stayed in §3 as open work.

### 0.3.4 — point release: Mounts input normalisation (bug on a shipped feature, 2026-09-22)

**mounts.1** `[done 2026-09-23]` As a user creating a remote mount, I want the server and share fields to accept what I paste or type — `//10.0.0.212`, `10.0.0.212/`, `smb://host/share`, a share with a leading slash — and to be told plainly when the share is missing, so Test connection diagnoses the server and not my typing. **Found** on a production node (0.3.2): Test with a slash-prefixed server failed at the DNS stage with the raw string; an empty or slash-prefixed share reached the kernel as `//host/` / `//host//share` → "Malformed UNC in devname", `mount error(22)`, reported as *"Unreachable — no route to the server"* (EINVAL fell into the unreachable bucket); a test without credentials hung on `mount.cifs`'s password prompt and was reported the same way. Reproduced on the stunt node through the gateway. **Fix:** one shared normaliser (strip schemes, leading `//`/`\\`, trailing slashes; CIFS share = bare name, inner prefix path kept; NFS export = one leading `/`), applied at both boundaries (dialog on blur, daemon before any probe); empty CIFS share ⇒ 400 with the guiding sentence; new verdict `invalid` for EINVAL/"Malformed UNC"; probe without credentials passes `guest` so it never prompts; Test button disabled until server and share are filled. Live proof through the gateway on the stunt node: the six input shapes → normalised spec or the 400 sentence; the `invalid` verdict on a hand-built malformed spec is unreachable by design after the fix, so the unit test pins the errno mapping.

### 0.3.5 — point release: vdev classes missing from the pool view (bug on a shipped feature, 2026-09-24)

**vdevs.1** `[done 2026-09-24 — ships in 0.3.5]` As a user whose pool carries log, special or dedup vdevs, I want the pool view to show every vdev class ZFS reports, so I can see what my pool is made of. **Found** on a production node (GitHub #66, 0.3.3): a log and a cache vdev added on the command line never appeared in ANAS. **Ground truth (stunt node, ZFS 2.4.4 / PVE 9.2.20, 2026-09-24):** `zpool status -j` reports `logs`, `l2cache`, `spares`, `special` and `dedup` as POOL-LEVEL sections beside the `vdevs` tree; `parsers/zpool-status.ts` reads only `l2cache` (and declares `spares`), so **log, special and dedup vdevs are invisible** in the pool detail whatever their origin — ANAS's own composer included — mirrored or single, whole-disk or partition-backed. A user cannot see the special vdev whose loss would lose the pool. 3.21/3.22 shipped without a JSON live proof of the display side (no parser test mentions logs). Captured fixtures: `fixtures/zfs/zpool-status-all-vdev-classes-2.4.4.json` (six classes on one pool) and `zpool-status-mirrored-log-partitions-2.4.4.json`. **Fix:** parse the five pool-level sections uniformly (role map `logs→log`, `l2cache→cache`, `spares→spare`, `special`, `dedup`; each entry is a vdev — `mirror-N` with its children, or a single leaf — with its state; leaves through the existing `diskId` so a short kernel name such as `sdb2` is LISTED even though it cannot cross-reference to the Disks list; by-id `-partN` leaves resolve to their disk as today). The UI needs no change (role labels for all six exist and the topology tree is role-generic) — verify, do not assume. Partition-backed vdevs are DISPLAYED, never managed from the UI (operator 2026-09-24: whole-disk model stays). **Acceptance:** parser tests over both fixtures (six roles with the right leaf names and states; the mirrored log as one `mirror-N` vdev with two leaves; the degraded/removed fixtures unchanged); **live proof on the stunt node:** a throwaway pool built by CLI on one disk's six partitions with all six classes → `GET /v1/pools/<p>` lists six roles with the right leaves; a log vdev added through ANAS's own add-vdev API shows up the same way; Playwright: the pool detail topology shows Log, Special and Dedup nodes. **Consumer audit (2026-09-24, operator: "check for other gaps or blind spots"):** every consumer iterates `vdevGroups` generically, so the parser fix heals them all — and each is blind today: (a) **pool destroy cleanup** (`leavesFromStatus` → labelclear / wipefs / GPT zap) skips the members of log, special and dedup vdevs, which keep their ZFS labels and read as leftover afterwards; (b) the **Disks inventory** (`routes/disks.ts` `poolInfo`) carries no pool / vdev / role / state / error counts for a disk whose only leaf is in one of those classes — a FAULTED special-vdev member shows no errors and raises no disk warning; (c) the **dashboard vdev join** (`fetchVdevTopology`) falls back to role `data` for an unmatched vdev, so a special mirror's telemetry is labelled a data vdev; (d) the **attach / replace dialog** (`35-pool-attach.js` `buildVdevMap`) lists no leaf of those classes — a failed special-vdev disk, the one failure that loses the pool, cannot be replaced from the UI; (e) the **composer in expand mode** (`loadExistingTopology`) does not seed existing special / log / dedup vdevs, so the advisor offers "a mirrored special vdev" to a pool that has one and the special-vdev danger sentence is missing; (f) the pool-list `leavesOf` (iSCSI claims / counts) omits them. **Acceptance additions (vdevs.1 slice 2, after the parser lands):** unit tests over the all-classes fixture for `leavesFromStatus` (six leaves), the disks route (`poolInfo` role `special` with state and error counts for `sdb5`), the dashboard join (role `special`, never `data`), and harness entries for `buildVdevMap` (leaves of all six roles) and the expand-mode seed (`hasSpecial` true ⇒ no suggestion, danger sentence present); **live proof:** on `gtvdev`, `zpool offline` the special leaf → Disks tab shows the disk DEGRADED with its pool and role, the dashboard shows the pool warning, the replace dialog lists the special leaf; destroy `gtvdev` through the API → all six partitions labelcleared (`wipefs`/`zpool labelclear` find nothing; the disk reads available). Short kernel-name leaves (`sdb2`, CLI-added without by-id) SHOULD resolve to their disk in `resolveLeafKernel` by stripping the partition suffix (`sdb2 → sdb`, `nvme0n1p2 → nvme0n1`) so the Disks tab carries their role too; if that is not a two-line change, record it as a known limit instead. Ships as 0.3.5 (branch from v0.3.4, cherry-pick — the parser is untouched since v0.3.4), release notes with the recovery note "nothing to do: the vdevs were always there, the view is fixed". **Result:** slice 1 (Qwen): ONE `parsePoolVdevSection` helper for the five pool-level sections, fixed group order, `mirror-N` kept as one vdev, 32 parser tests over the captured 2.4.4 fixtures, `vdev-fixture.sh` (disk 9 → six-partition pool `gtvdev`), `vdev-classes-api.spec.ts` 2/2 ×2 incl. the UI topology showing Log/Special/Dedup. Slice 2 (opus, after the consumer audit): every consumer pinned AND four more live 0.3.4 bugs found by the proofs — destroy cleanup was a no-op on a pool built from `/dev/sdX` names (no by-id anywhere in `zpool status`; leaves now carry their real device path, `allVdevLeaves` = one source for destroy and the pool list); the dashboard's `zpool iostat -plv` parser read the unindented `logs`/`cache`/`special`/`dedup` headers as PHANTOM POOLS and lost the vdevs under them (sections folded back; single-leaf pool-level vdevs join by leaf name); the composer's expand advisor never counted the seeded topology (offered a second special vdev, no danger sentence); `mmcblk0p1` did not resolve and `mmcblk0` was mangled; the Disks row for a multi-class disk now keeps the most-deserving context (state, then errors, then class gravity). `vdev-consumers-api.spec.ts` 3/3 ×2 (log leaf offlined → Disks row/role/warning + status DEGRADED; replace dialog lists the special leaf; API destroy labelclears all six partitions). Limits recorded: a single special vdev cannot be offlined (ZFS refuses), `zpool labelclear -c` does not exist on 2.4.4, telemetry shows partition leaves by kernel name. Suite +30, harness +24. **Review fix batch (the operator's `/code-review 0.3.5`, 2026-09-24 — every ground truth had ridden a `/dev/sdX`-named pool, so the BY-ID PARTITION-BACKED layout the #66 user has was never proven):** section entries keep 0.3.4's container-per-section shape (one `cache`/`spares`/`logs`/`special`/`dedup` vdev holding all bare leaves, state = worst leaf; `mirror-N`/raidz stay their own vdevs — every consumer keys on `v.name`); a `file`-type cache/spare keeps its leaf (was `disks: []`); the dashboard join indexes `basename(disk.path)` as well as the stripped id so by-id partition leaves carry their own role; new fixtures `zpool-status-multi-cache-spare-2.4.4.json` + the matching `zpool iostat -plv` capture (two cache partitions, two spare partitions, one shared stripped id); `vdev-fixture.sh up-byid` / `up-multi`; `vdev-remove-byid-api.spec.ts` 5/5 (all four vdev specs 17/17 ×2).

**vdevs.2** `[done 2026-09-24 — ships in 0.3.5]` Remove a cache, log or spare vdev (GitHub #66; the add side is 3.21; pulled INTO 0.3.5 by the operator 2026-09-24 — "0.4.0 is getting full") — as a user who added an L2ARC or SLOG, I want to take it out again from the UI, so a change of mind does not need the command line. **Build:** `POST /v1/pools/:name/vdevs/remove` `{ vdev }` → `zpool remove <pool> <vdev>` where `vdev` is a cache leaf, a spare leaf, a log leaf or a log `mirror-N` (ZFS removes these instantly — no evacuation); refused up front (400, one sentence) for `data`, `special` and `dedup` vdevs (their removal is device evacuation, a different operation ANAS does not offer) and for a vdev the pool does not carry; a plain job (no confirm code — nothing is destroyed; a log removal only flushes the ZIL first). UI: per the one-menu rule, the action sits on the Pools grid toolbar as "Remove vdev…", a small dialog listing the selected pool's removable vdevs (role, name, leaves) with the one sentence "Data, special and dedup vdevs cannot be removed here"; the topology reloads after the job, success and failure. **Acceptance:** route tests (each role's allow/refuse, unknown vdev, PVE-owned pool refused as everywhere); **live proof on the stunt node:** add then remove a cache leaf, a spare, a single log leaf and a mirrored log through the API on a throwaway pool — `zpool status` and `GET /v1/pools/<p>` agree after each step; Playwright over the dialog incl. a refused pick. DESIGN.md pools table carries the row. Built on main after vdevs.1 (its live proof needs the fixed parser), cherry-picked onto release/0.3.5 with it. **Result (opus):** shared `RemoveVdevRequest` + `REMOVABLE_VDEV_ROLES`; `services/zfs-vdev-remove.ts` `resolveVdev` (a leaf inside a mirror/raidz resolves to its parent `mirror-N`, a bare leaf to its device path; data groups still matched so the refusal names the real reason); route with system-pool 409 + PVE-ownership 400, a settle loop after `zpool remove`, `ANAS_VDEV_REMOVE_SETTLE_MS`; `34-pool-vdev-remove.js` toolbar dialog reloading grid AND open detail on success and failure; 22 route tests, 17 harness checks; `vdev-remove-api.spec.ts` 7/7 ×2 (the spec owns its fixture since it consumes it). On release/0.3.5 the gate is 3.25's whole-pool `poolPveStorages` + `isRootPool` (pvepool.1 is 0.4.0-only). **Review fix batch (2026-09-24):** the dialog sends the leaf's device-path basename (`ata-SSD-part2`, `sdb3`), never the stripped by-id (on a split SSD both partitions share it and the first match was the LOG — removing the cache would have removed the SLOG; live-proven fixed: cache token → cache out, SLOG untouched); `resolveVdev` refuses an ambiguous token naming the candidates, never resolves a section container name, and refuses an in-use spare with its own sentence; the settle poll fails closed (a failed or pool-less `zpool status` keeps polling until the deadline, then fails — it used to read as "gone"); one shared `vdevTopologyGate` pre-flight for add-vdev AND remove (parity — add had no PVE gate). **Fix batch 2 (GLM static review, 2026-09-24):** the REPLACE/attach dialog and route use the same unambiguous leaf token (`ANAS.vdevLeafName` in `00-core.js`; `services/zfs-vdev-leaf.ts` = the ONE leaf matcher both routes call; a split SSD renders one distinct slot per partition and `zpool replace` gets the leaf's real device — live-proven by replacing the log partition on the by-id fixture); iostat section headers recognised by SHAPE (an unindented all-`-` row) so a pool named `logs` cannot break the fold; a leaf ZFS reports with neither devid nor by-id path keeps its unstripped name in the synthesized path; empty `path` never becomes a token; a grouping vdev is always one row; route tests for the in-use spare, the pool-absent settle branch and cross-class basename ambiguity. All four vdev specs 18/18 ×2 on the release build.

### 0.4.0 — accepted 2026-09-14 (operator: "a full and solid release"); groomed 2026-09-21

> Theme: the NAS reaches out, boots the fleet, and serves its clients' history. Stories below are AUTHORIZED in scope; each still gets its detailed story text (and PXE its design pass) before dispatch.
>
> **Dispatch order (grooming 2026-09-21):** user-driven stories first — `pvepool.1` → `smbsvc.1–3` → `rclone.1–3` → `ahrcache.1` — then `backup2.11`, `disks.1`, `ahrexpand.1`; `pxe.1` last. No version was promised on any issue (§2 rule); the issues stay open until their story ships.
>
> **Riders (ticks, not stories):** the add-LUN dialog's AHR-backing branch gets a one-line note — *image is checksummed and CoW; prefer a zvol for random-write workloads* (the 2026-09-11 CoW ruling, §2 AHR). **sched.stamps** `[done 2026-09-25]` — task timers: removing any ANAS timer unit deletes its systemd stamp and failed state; orphan stamps of our prefixes are swept at daemon start (ruling 2026-09-25, SCHEDULES-GT-16…21; 63532c8 + the fix batch). **Live-proven on the stunt node 2026-09-25** (`tests/integration/sched-stamps.spec.ts`, 3/3 twice): the first start carrying the fix swept the node's own 11 orphans (26 stamp files → 15, one journald line each, every foreign stamp and `stamp-anas-recycle.timer` untouched — SCHEDULES-GT-22), planted orphans of all four prefixes go and a foreign one plus `stamp-anas-scrub.timer` stay, a deleted task whose last run FAILED leaves no `list-units --failed` ghost (SCHEDULES-GT-23), and the same name re-created with a time of day that already passed today records no trigger and starts nothing in 45 s.

**rclone.1** `[done 2026-09-24]` Cloud sync — remotes (GitHub #57; design DESIGN.md "Cloud sync — rclone"; GT 2026-09-23) — as a user, I want to define the cloud destinations rclone can reach from the ANAS UI, with their secrets held on the node and never shown again, so I can send data offsite without a shell. **Build:** `install.sh` installs `rclone` as a hard dependency (Debian's package; `uninstall.sh` removes neither the package nor `rclone.conf`); shared `CloudProvider`/`CloudProviderOption`, `CloudRemote`, `CloudRemoteWrite`, `CloudRemoteTestRequest/Result` schemas; daemon `services/rclone-config.ts` = the ONE config-file module: path constant + `--config`/`--ask-password=false` argv builder, `rclone version` + `config dump` reads (encrypted ⇒ `encrypted:true`, `409 config-encrypted` on mutations), the secret-key rule (`IsPassword` ∪ name suffix), the surgical INI section writer (add/replace/remove, comments and other sections byte-identical, write-temp-rename 0600 under the per-file lock), `rclone obscure -` over stdin for password-typed values, the `config dump` gate with byte-identical rollback, the argv-secret guard lifted from `iscsi-mutate.ts` into a shared helper used by both; `services/rclone-probe.ts` = bounded `lsjson` Test with the verdict classifier (env-defined `ANASTEST` remote for an unsaved dialog — GT on 1.60 first; fall back to Test-after-save if env remotes do not honour obscured passwords); routes `GET /v1/cloud/providers` (trimmed schema), `GET/POST /v1/cloud/remotes`, `PUT/DELETE /v1/cloud/remotes/:name` (delete = hard 409 naming referencing tasks — the task store lands in rclone.2, so rclone.1 ships the check against an empty store), `POST /v1/cloud/remotes/test`. UI: the **Remotes** manager window (`72-cloud.js`, `anas-win-cloud-remotes`) — grid (name, type, set keys) + footer (rclone version, file path, the copy-back sentence naming `--config /etc/anas/rclone.conf`); Add/Edit dialog rendering from the provider schema (type combo with descriptions, provider-filtered rows, examples as editable combos, bool/int fields, password fields write-only with "(unchanged)" on edit, "Show advanced options" toggle, the OAuth sentence when the backend has a `token` option), Test with the verdict sentence in the dialog, Save → job → reload on success and failure; Remove with the referenced-refusal toast. The Cloud Sync menu entry itself lands in rclone.3 — until then the window is reachable from the dialog-contracts harness and Playwright only. **Acceptance:** unit tests over the INI writer (add/replace/remove keep every other byte; comments kept; a value with `=`, spaces and `;` round-trips as rclone writes it; obscure via stdin only — the argv guard test asserts no secret token in any executor call), the secret rule table (b2 `key`, s3 `secret_access_key`, `client_secret`, `token`, `sse_customer_key` secret; `access_key_id`, `key_file`, `sse_kms_key_id` not), the providers trim on a captured 1.60 `config providers` fixture (option count per backend, provider filters, IsPassword), the verdict classifier over the captured message set, encrypted-file behaviour with a fixture file; **live proof on the stunt node** (`tests/integration/cloud-remotes-api.spec.ts`): sftp remote to the node's own `sshd` — create with password → file section has `pass = <obscured>`, mode 0600, API answers `secretsSet:['pass']` and no value; Test `ok`; wrong password → `auth`; host 192.0.2.1 → `unreachable`; missing path → `not-found`; unknown remote → 4xx; edit without the secret keeps it (file byte-identical except the changed key); a hand-added section with a comment survives an ANAS write byte-for-byte; delete removes only the section. Playwright over the window (`cloud-remotes-ui.spec.ts`): the generated S3 form shows one `region` row after picking a provider, the password field is blank on edit, Test verdicts, reload after an injected failure. **Result (2026-09-23/24; slices 1–3 + three review fix batches, lanes swapped each time):** shared `schemas/cloud.ts`; `parsers/rclone-conf.ts` (byte-identical line-model doc, CRLF-safe, `upsertSection`/`removeSection`); `services/rclone-config.ts` (ANAS's own `/etc/anas/rclone.conf`, INI writer, `rclone obscure -` on stdin, `config dump` gate with byte-identical rollback, secret rule = `IsPassword` ∪ name suffix minus bools, `password` = the obscure set); `services/secret-argv.ts` = the ONE argv guard (iSCSI's lifted, value-membership added, base args skipped); `services/file-lock.ts` (five private copies unified); `services/rclone-probe.ts` (bounded `lsjson`, message-classified verdicts — every `NewFs` failure exits 1; an UNSAVED dialog probes as an env-defined remote on a private 0700 temp COPY of the store under `/run/anas`, swept at boot, because **rclone's sftp backend writes `shell_type`/`md5sum_command`/`sha1sum_command` into whatever `--config` it is given and creates the file when absent** — GT that cost a night); `routes/cloud.ts`; `install.sh` installs `rclone` like samba; `72-cloud.js` Remotes window with the provider-schema form (basic rows, provider-filtered rows, advanced toggle as a view not a filter, OAuth sentence, write-only secrets, required stored secrets never gate Edit, saved-remote Test from Edit, bools never secret and defaults omitted, provider pick required). Ground truth folded into DESIGN: env-defined remotes need the OBSCURED password; the CLI `config providers` prints a bare array; the fixture is that output. Reviews found and fixed: argv guard false positives (the daemon-suite "flake"), CRLF, dump bytes in errors, tmp residue, three UI defects, and a real-ExtJS-only failure — the provider row's `change` handler rebuilt the form synchronously and destroyed its own field, sticking layouts suspended (now deferred; harness models `Ext.defer`). Live proofs on the stunt node against its own sshd: `cloud-remotes-api.spec.ts` 10/10 (×3 builds), `cloud-remotes-ui.spec.ts` 7/7 ×2. Suite 3994 daemon / 87 / 48; dialog-contracts 1550. The Cloud Sync menu entry lands with rclone.3; the socket-perms test race found along the way is fixed. Human-pass finding (2026-09-25): the OAuth sentence said "paste its output" while `rclone authorize` prints marker lines around the JSON block, and a paste with the markers or with quotes failed inside rclone with a Go unmarshal error; the sentence now names the block, and the token field and the daemon normalise the paste (markers and one layer of quotes stripped) and refuse anything that is not a JSON object with one plain sentence.

**rclone.2** `[done 2026-09-25]` Cloud sync — tasks, runner, notifications (design as above) — as a user, I want a copy or sync of a path to a remote to run on a schedule and tell me how it went, so my offsite copy is a job I can see and not a cron line I forget. **Build:** shared `CloudSyncTask` (+ `TaskCadence` = the backup cadence schema renamed, `BackupCadence` kept as an alias) and `CloudSyncRunResult`; the backup unit-store generics (`deriveTaskStatus`, `readRecentJournal`, `readLastSuccessAt`, `gateRun`, `superviseRun`, `collectWarnings`, `deriveTriggerSource`) extracted into a prefix-parameterised `services/task-units.ts` that `backup-units.ts` and the new `cloud-units.ts` both call — **the extraction is its own FIRST slice, dispatched and merged alone (operator 2026-09-23: "make sure it's well tested"): backup's unit suite unchanged, the extracted functions get a second test pass under the cloud prefix, the existing backup live-proof specs re-run on the stunt node before the cloud store is started, and the review lane is swapped from the author's;** `anas-cloud-<name>.service`/`.timer` render + parse (`X-ANAS-Task=` marker, `Persistent=true`, `SuccessExitStatus=75`); runner `dist/cloud-task.js` (the backup runner's twin on `runner-poll.ts`); `services/source-guard.ts` = the ONE configured-but-unmounted check (backup2.11 wires it into backup later); `services/cloud-runner.ts` = the run job: guards (missing source, unmounted mount named, empty source in `sync` mode), consistency derived through `backup-consistency.ts`'s helper (transient `anas-cloud-<task>-<unix>` snapshot via the shared snapshot helper, destroyed in `finally`, swept at start), rclone `copy|sync` under `execFile` with `--use-json-log --stats 30s --stats-log-level NOTICE` and the optional `--bwlimit` / `--exclude` flags, `stats` objects → job progress, exit-code policy (0 and 9 complete; all else fails with rclone's error lines), the result object; `pve-notify` template `anas-cloud` (`packaging/templates/anas-cloud-{subject,body}.txt.hbs`; body = task, `source → remote:path`, mode, live/snapshot, bytes, files, checks, deletes, errors, duration, error lines; skips silent; delivery never fails the run); dashboard warning category `cloud` (failures + overdue); routes `GET/POST /v1/cloud/tasks`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /v1/cloud/tasks/:name`, `POST /v1/cloud/tasks/:name/run`; the rclone.1 delete-refusal now reads the real task store. **Acceptance:** unit tests for the unit render/parse round-trip, the extraction (backup tests unchanged, cloud tests over the same functions with the other prefix), the JSON-log parser (stats object → progress; the final stats → result; error lines collected), the exit-code table, each guard, the notification body; **live proof on the stunt node** (`cloud-tasks-api.spec.ts`, sftp remote to the node's own sshd, a ZFS dataset source): `copy` run → files present under the remote path, job progress carried bytes and files, the run's journal shows the JSON stats and the unit's own result; `sync` run after deleting a source file → the file is gone at the destination; the source was read from the transient snapshot path and the snapshot is gone after the run; empty-source `sync` refused with the sentence; a CIFS mount defined in fstab but unmounted as source → refused naming the mount; a wrong-password remote → failed run, error line in the job, notification emitted (`journalctl` shows the `PVE::Notify` call), dashboard warning `cloud`; timer fire via `systemctl start anas-cloud-<name>.timer` + a past `OnCalendar` → the same history; disable → status `disabled` with the history note. **Addendum (operator 2026-09-24): dry-run preview endpoint** `POST /v1/cloud/tasks/preview` (DESIGN table row): inline task or `{name}` → `rclone <mode> --dry-run --use-json-log` on the live source, 120 s ceiling, `{transfers, bytes, checks, deletes, deletedFiles[] (first 200 + total), errors, truncated}`; the unmounted guard refuses, the empty-source guard does not (that is what the preview is for); reuses `buildRcloneArgs` + the JSON-log reader; route tests over captured `--dry-run` lines (the `skipped: delete` objects from the 2026-09-23 GT); live proof: preview of a sync after deleting a source file lists it. **Result (2026-09-24; slices 1–3 merged ca8298a / d340bd8 / 743858b + two lane-swapped review fix batches; the preview addendum merged 71c7624 + this fix batch: `cloud-preview.ts`, three verbatim 1.60.1 dry-run fixtures (`skipped: copy|delete` objects, final stats always printed), 120 s `timeout` ceiling ⇒ `truncated`, reader caps the delete list at 200 with a separate count, a non-JSON failure carries the run's own sentence, inline arm wins over `name`; API spec 9/9 ×2 on the node; failing-preview capture `dry-run-auth-fail-1.60.1.log` landed ced1758 and OVERTURNED the hand-written shape: rclone 1.60.1 fails to build the filesystem before its JSON logger exists, so the line is plain text (one raw line, null stats) and the preview's `rcloneFailureMessage` fallback is what carries it — correct on the first live execution; its tail varies per run (`connection reset by peer` / `EOF` / `no supported methods remain`), only the `NewFs: couldn't connect SSH:` prefix is stable; a second capture (`dry-run-read-error-1.60.1.log`, exit 6 with real JSON error objects) now backs the error-line-wins test; `fixtures/rclone/NOTES.md` carries provenance for all six rclone fixtures):** `services/task-units.ts` = the ONE unit store, parameterised by a `TaskUnitKind` descriptor (prefix, marker, runner script, warning category, label, title, view, cause hints, log tag) — backup's wrappers keep every export and its tests were untouched; shared `schemas/task-cadence.ts` with the `BackupCadence` aliases; `cloud-units.ts`, `cloud-task.ts` runner, `source-guard.ts` (exported for backup2.11), `cloud-runner.ts` (guards, snapshot source through the shared consistency helper, rclone JSON progress via a new executor stderr tee — spawn-based, 64 KiB bounded tail, 256 MiB cap — exit 0/9 complete), `cloud-notify.ts` + the `anas-cloud` template pair, dashboard `cloud` warnings, task routes, the remotes DELETE referenced hook now reads the real store. Along the way `isTransientRunSnapshot` learned both prefixes and is consulted by replication and retention (a real hazard). Ground truth from the live proof: `PVE::Notify` logs only `notified via target …` (never the template) and returns 0 even when a template pair is missing (`could not notify … failed to render`) — the spec proves the pair rendered by asserting zero `could not notify` lines over a journald cursor window; rclone prints its final stats at exit even on a sub-second run (`countersReported: true` is deterministic, an in-flight progress line is not); a wrong-password sftp run takes ~29 s of rclone's own retries before `exit 1`; `enable --now` stamps a timer's `LastTriggerUSec` without firing, so a real timer fire is proven by `LastTriggerUSec >= scheduledAt` on an absolute `OnCalendar`. The proof found one defect: `failureDetailFromJournal` treated only systemd's `<unit>.service:` trailer as boilerplate, so the `Failed to start <unit> - …` sentence won and every guard refusal reached the operator as the bare failure DESIGN forbids — fixed for both stores (unit-name match anywhere in the line; the last-resort branch is now the last thing the UNIT itself said). Reading settled by the proof: a guard at Run now answers 202 and the run FAILS with the sentence (the mechanism paragraph), which the dashboard and the row's tooltip carry; the dialog-side sentence is the preview's job. Live proof `cloud-tasks-api.spec.ts` 8/8 ×2 on the stunt node (9/9 ×2 once the preview test joined) over `cloud-tasks-fixture.sh` (copy/sync semantics with sha256, snapshot source gone after the run, empty-source and unmounted-mount refusals verbatim, failure notification + `cloud` warning, real timer fire, disable/delete + referenced-remote 409); node restored to its recorded pre-state. Suite 4380 daemon.

**rclone.3** `[done 2026-09-25]` Cloud sync — the Cloud Sync workflow (design as above → Workflow) — as a user, I want one menu where I see my offsite copies, their last result and next run, and create or run one without leaving the PVE UI. **Build:** `72-cloud.js` grows the **Cloud Sync** menu (registered after `backup` in `90-register.js`): Tasks grid (name, source, `remote:path`, mode, schedule spelled out as backup spells it, last run, next run, enabled) + toolbar (Create, Edit, Remove, Run now, Preview, Enable/Disable, Remotes… — Preview dry-runs the SAVED task in the display-only `anas-win-cloud-preview` window); the task wizard — Source via `ANAS.pathPicker` (live backend, PVE-owned paths refused), the consistency line and the nested-filesystems note from `preview-nested`, Remote combo (empty ⇒ the "Add a remote first (Remotes…)" toast), Path on remote, Mode with the one sentence under it (copy default), Excludes, Bandwidth limit, the backup cadence widget reused (extracted to `69-schedules-common.js` if it is still private to `68-backup.js`), Notify; detail window (config, consistency, nested-not-included list, unit and timer text, recent runs labeled recent-only, the disabled-history note); Run now → dashboard jobs strip with rclone's progress; the grid reloads after every job of its own, success AND failure, with the new row selected on create; failed Last run cell in red with rclone's last error line as the tooltip. **Acceptance:** dialog-contracts harness entries for the wizard and the remotes dialog; **Playwright on the stunt node** (`cloud-ui.spec.ts`, over the rclone.2 fixture): create with the nested note visible on a dataset with a child dataset, Run now → progress on the strip → Last run refreshed on completion, sync-mode sentence present and copy the default, empty-source refusal shown in the dialog with the sentence, reload after an injected job failure (stand-in `rclone` exiting 7), Remotes… opens the rclone.1 window, Remove of a referenced remote refused with the task named, disabled task shows the history note in detail; toolbar overflow rule as pvepool.2 (run at 2560×1080). **Rider (operator 2026-09-24): the wizard's Preview button** — `POST /v1/cloud/tasks/preview` with the unsaved form (or `{name}` from the grid toolbar) → the dry-run summary line under the Mode field: would transfer N files / M bytes, delete K files at the destination, the first deletes listed (capped, with the total), `truncated` shown as "stopped after 120 s — the full run will do the rest"; on `sync` a preview that deletes everything is shown in red with the empty-source sentence. Harness: the summary render for zero / some / everything-deleted / truncated; Playwright: preview a sync after deleting a source file shows that file under deletes. **Result (2026-09-24/25; GLM slice 2bcb029 + opus review fix batch 85e00fd + Preview rider 9e9bbe8 + its fix batch 38fdf9c + live proof 7cb117e):** `72-cloud.js` grows the Cloud Sync menu (registered after Backup): the Tasks grid (name, source, `remote:path` whole with a title, mode, schedule, last run, next run, enabled), toolbar Create / Edit / Remove / Run now / Preview / Enable-Disable / Details / Remotes…, the task wizard (Browse through the path picker — it hands back a string; live `preview-nested` scan with the consistency chip and the nested note; Remote combo fed from the fetched remotes; Mode with the sync sentence, copy the default; excludes, bandwidth limit, the cadence widget, notify), the display-only detail window, Run now on the jobs strip with reload on success AND failure, the Preview button under Mode and on the toolbar (dry-run summary: transfers and bytes, would-be deletes listed from the reader's own count, `truncated` with the honest sentence, a failed preview never headlines a zero forecast, a sync that would delete everything in red, a 400's sentence in the line; per-wizard request token, button disabled in flight, the line cleared by any field the body is computed from). The backup cadence widget moved to `69-schedules-common.js` (`ANAS.sched.cadence`, `68-backup.js` delegates through lazy wrappers — the bundle loads 68 before 69, proven in true order), which fixed a latent backup bug on the way: `cad.of()` emitted `parity: ''` for weekly/monthly, so Enable/Disable on such a task was a 400; the session run-error store (`ANAS.sched.runErrors`, keyed to the run) is shared, so backup's failed pill gained the tooltip and the reload-after-a-failed-run it lacked. `GET /cloud/tasks` joined the other cloud reads under the rclone-absent 503. Reviews found and fixed: the Remote combo had no store (nothing pickable in real ExtJS), Browse read `.path` off the picker's string, the parity bug, seven spec defects, the failed-preview headline, truncated-as-empty-source, deletes gated on the wrong counter. Harness 1834 (payloads validated against the real shared schemas; a static guard proves every `confirmAndRun` verb targets a confirm-gated route). Live proof `cloud-tasks-ui.spec.ts` 8/8 ×2 on the stunt node (create with the nested note, Run now → strip → Last run, empty-source refusal in the dialog, stand-in rclone exit 7 → red cell + reload, Remotes window, referenced-remote 409, disabled history note, Preview names the deleted file in the wizard and on the toolbar); no product defect — the two first-run failures were spec locators (`x-grid-item-selected` sits on the row's item table; login once). Spec name is `cloud-tasks-ui.spec.ts` (the Build text said `cloud-ui`). Ground truth: ExtJS 7 stamps no `itemId` as a DOM id (address components by `cls`); the message box is `.x-message-box`; the spec uses an absolute future date as its schedule because a re-used task name inherits a leftover systemd stamp (SCHEDULES-GT-17/21; ruling pending). **Human-pass findings (2026-09-25, Google Drive, fix batch pending):** (1) Run now is not gated on a run already in progress — a second click attaches to the running unit (the shared supervision deliberately supervises the run it finds rather than starting another) and sits in the job list as 'already running — waiting'; the route must answer 409 with a sentence naming the running run and its progress, the toolbar button must be disabled on a running row, and backup's Run now gets the same gate (parallel construction; the shared helper keeps its attach behaviour for the timer race). (2) A long run reads as eternal: the daemon's own direct run job carries rclone's live progress (bytes of total, files, ETA — measured on the node: 17 MB in thousands of small files at Drive's few-files-per-second rate = a 17-hour ETA, the run was progressing), but the supervising Run-now job stops at its 10-minute ceiling with 'still running' and the grid shows only a running pill; the task status must carry the running direct job's progress text so the row and the detail window show 'running — N files, X of Y, ETA' until the unit ends, and the ceiling message must say where to look. **Fixed 2026-09-25:** Run now (`POST …/run`, non-direct) answers `409 CONFLICT` with "<Cloud sync|Backup> task '<name>' is already running since <time>; it continues under systemd — wait for it to finish" — the running direct job's live progress appended after an em-dash when one is in flight — from a new `readRunActive(executor, kind, name)` on the shared task-units module (one `systemctl show` of ActiveState,InactiveExitTimestamp, fail-open to not-running; the supervision's attach behaviour untouched for the timer race), wired into BOTH the cloud and the backup run doors (backup parity, one sentence builder fed by the kind descriptor); the toolbar Run now button is disabled on a `running` row in both grids (`cloudTaskRun`/`backupRun` — display, never the safety, the gate stays at the API); the task status gained the additive optional `runningProgress` (`CloudSyncTaskView`/`BackupTaskEntry`/`BackupTaskDetail` in shared), filled only while the unit is active AND a running direct job for that task exists in the queue (`JobQueue.findActive` grew an additive `withParams` filter; ONE `runningDirectJobProgress` helper feeds the status routes and the 409), and the row's Last run cell plus both detail windows render "running — <progress>" through one renderer function per UI file; the supervision ceiling sentence now says "still running after 600s — systemd continues it; the task row shows its progress until it ends". Verification all green: build:shared, typecheck, eslint on the changed daemon files; daemon suite 4659/4659, shared 113, gateway 48; dialog-contracts harness 1892 checks (+20 new: a running row disables Run now with Preview/Details/Edit still enabled, success/failure/never-run rows enable it, the progress text renders after the pill with the same tooltip, the bare pill without it). Found the same day by the review-fix deploy: a daemon restart mid-run left the runner polling the vanished job's 404 for up to 24 h (unit activating forever, row 'running', Run now 409, nothing to cancel); the runner now treats 404 as terminal and bounds reconnect attempts, so the unit fails with the interruption sentence and the row reads failed.

**pxe.1** PXE boot images — DESIGN FIRST (own session): images stored on a dataset, served by wiring up existing tools (dnsmasq proxyDHCP + TFTP, HTTP for UEFI boot), an iPXE menu generated from what is on the share; proxyDHCP ONLY, never own DHCP; scoped per bridge; two uses: (a) VM installers/live images, (b) other PVE nodes — the Proxmox installer with an answer file served over HTTP (automated install of a new/replacement node from the NAS) and a rescue image (the bare-metal end of DR). The node hosting ANAS cannot netboot from itself. **Slips to 0.5.0 if the release is otherwise ready** (grooming 2026-09-21): the one story with no user demand behind it and a design pass in front of it.

**smbsvc.1** `[done 2026-09-23]` Previous Versions on SMB shares (design DESIGN.md "Self-service on SMB shares"; GT 2026-09-22; trimmed 2026-09-22) — as a user, I want to restore my own files from the schedule's snapshots through Windows' Previous Versions on the share, so recovery does not go through the admin. **Build:** `vfs objects` composer + managed feature keys in `parsers/smb-conf.ts` (one composer for all three features — smbsvc.2/.3 reuse it; a user-verbatim `vfs objects` line ⇒ refuse 400 "remove it first"); `SmbShare.previousVersions?: { bucket }` (additive; bucket chosen by the daemon = finest schedule on the dataset, `daily` when none); daemon writes `shadow:format = anas-<bucket>-%Y-%m-%dT%H%M%SZ` + `localtime=no` + `sort=desc` + ZFS `snapdir=.zfs/snapshot` & `snapdirseverywhere=yes`, AHR absolute snapdir + ONE read-only `@snapshots` fstab mount at `/mnt/anas-ahr-snapshots/<pool>` (`nofail`; added on first enable, never removed); refuses (400, reason) a flat-layout AHR pool or a path on neither ZFS nor AHR; `testparm` gate + byte-identical rollback on failure. UI: the Self-service fieldset row with the read-only bucket sentence / the "empty until a schedule runs" note / greyed-with-reason states; detail; reload on success and failure. **Acceptance:** parser tests (composer order, managed keys, verbatim refusal, byte-identical no-op edit); route tests (ZFS vs AHR shapes, refusals, bucket choice); **live proof** on the stunt node: ZFS share with `anas-daily-*`/`anas-hourly-*`/manual snapshots → `smbclient allinfo` lists exactly the finest bucket's snapshots, `get @GMT-…/f` returns the old content; AHR loop pool → same through the `@snapshots` mount; Playwright over the dialog + a failed-job reload. **Result:** `composeVfsObjects` + managed self-service keys + `# anas:recycle-purge-days` marker in `parsers/smb-conf.ts` (one composer for all three features; custom `vfs objects` line ⇒ 400); `services/share-selfservice.ts` (path → dataset/AHR pool, finest enabled schedule bucket else `daily`, the AHR `@snapshots` read-only fstab mount at `/mnt/anas-ahr-snapshots/<pool>` added once, `testparm` gate inside the edit transform so a failing config writes nothing); per-share `previousVersions` derived from the stanza. UI: Self-service fieldset (checkbox + note), submit on change only, detail line, reload on success AND failure (also added to the remove flow). Live proofs on the stunt node: ZFS `tests/integration/smbsvc-api.spec.ts` 4/4 + AHR 1/1 on a real-disk AHR pool (fixture `smbsvc-fixture.sh up|down|ahr-up|ahr-down`), `tests/integration/smbsvc-ui.spec.ts` 6/6 twice. **The AHR live proof found two defects the unit mocks hid:** `ahr-destroy` removed only the first fstab line for the pool, so the snapshots mount survived and held the LV open (destroy would have failed at `lvremove`); and the mount-ensure step never created the mountpoint and swallowed `mount`'s exit code, so the enable job reported success over a mount that never happened. Both fixed with tests (f069544). Suite 3762 daemon / 87 shared / 48 gateway; dialog-contracts 1397.

**smbsvc.2** `[done 2026-09-23]` Recycle bin on SMB shares (GitHub #62; design as above) — as a user, I want deleted files to land in a `#recycle` folder on the share I can browse, purged after a period I choose. **Build:** `SmbShare.recycle?: { purgeDays: 7|14|30|90|null }`; daemon writes `recycle:repository = #recycle`, `keeptree`, `versions`, `touch` through the composer and the marker comment `# anas:recycle-purge-days = <n>` in the stanza (parser preserves/rewrites only that line); static `anas-recycle.timer` + `.service` + runner shipped in `packaging/` and installed/removed by `install.sh`/`uninstall.sh` (daily; scans `smb.conf` for `recycle:repository` + marker; `find <path>/#recycle -type f -ctime +<days> -delete`; empty dirs pruned; per-share counts to journald; nothing for `never`/no marker); turning off leaves `#recycle` in place. UI: row with the purge combo + the one sentence "files deleted from #recycle are gone for real; deleting the folder empties the bin" (GT'd: no loop, bin recreated on the next delete); detail; reload on success and failure. No empty-now verb, no size cap. **Acceptance:** parser tests (marker round-trip, byte-identical when untouched); runner tests on a fixture directory (young file kept, old file and its emptied directory removed, share without marker untouched); **live proof**: client `put`/`del` → `#recycle/<tree>/file` with ctime = now, `Copy #1 of` on a second delete; the runner on a fixture with an aged ctime (the fixture script creates the file, then sets the VM clock forward or uses a pre-aged fixture file) removes only the old one; timer installed by the deploy. **Result:** daemon writes the four `recycle:*` keys through the composer plus the `# anas:recycle-purge-days = <n|never>` marker (25fbb58); ONE static `anas-recycle.timer` (daily, `Persistent=true`) + `.service` shipped in `packaging/systemd`, installed/enabled by `install.sh`, removed by `uninstall.sh`; the runner `packages/daemon/src/recycle-purge.ts` is a Node script reusing the smb.conf parser (`recyclePurgeTargets`), walks each bin with `find -ctime +N`'s whole-day rounding, prunes emptied directories, keeps `#recycle` itself, logs `share=<s> removed=<n> pruned_dirs=<m>` / `skipped`, exit 1 only on a per-share failure. UI: Recycle bin row + purge-age combo + the one-sentence note, three-valued submit, detail line (e79e94e; dialog-contracts 1414). Live proof `tests/integration/smbsvc-recycle.spec.ts` 5/5 twice (8bac089): smbclient delete → `#recycle/<tree>`, `Copy #1 of`, delete-from-bin is real, `deltree` empties, recreated on next delete; runner `removed=0` on a young bin and `removed=1 pruned_dirs=1` after a clock jump on the stunt node; `never` ⇒ skipped; disable leaves the bin. Ground truth from the proof: the timer's persistent catch-up fires the purge as soon as the clock moves past a missed daily — the spec stops the timer across its jump.

**smbsvc.3** `[done 2026-09-23]` Time Machine target on SMB shares — **BETA** (design as above; shelved idea; operator 2026-09-22: "we can ship time machine as a beta feature — this is one place we have to lean on the community to test") — as a user, I want a share to work as a Time Machine destination with a size cap. **Build:** `SmbShare.timeMachine?: { maxSize: bytes }` (cap required); composer adds `catia fruit streams_xattr` + `fruit:time machine = yes` + `fruit:time machine max size`; no avahi; dialog row labelled "Time Machine target (beta)": cap prefilled with half the dataset's available space, the dedicated-share sentence, the ZFS-quota hint, the Finder/`tmutil` sentence, and the beta sentence (server side proven, client side community-verified); release notes carry the same beta wording and ask for reports. **Acceptance:** parser/route tests (cap required, units, composer order with the other two features on); `testparm -s` on the stunt node shows the share with the fruit keys and Samba's enforced `durable handles` / `posix locking = no`; Playwright over the dialog prefill. Client-side proof = community (no current macOS on the bench). **Result (aa4eb94):** composer emits `catia fruit streams_xattr` + `fruit:time machine = yes` + `fruit:time machine max size = <N>T|G|M` (largest exact unit, never below `1M` — Samba reads 0 as unlimited); `SmbShareDetail.capacity.availableBytes` (ZFS `available` / AHR free) feeds the dialog's half-of-available prefill; the row is labelled beta with the dedicated-share, ZFS-quota, Finder/`tmutil` and community-verified sentences; three-valued submit; detail line. Suite 3809 daemon / 87 shared / 48 gateway; dialog-contracts 1433; live proofs `smbsvc-api.spec.ts` 6/6 and `smbsvc-ui.spec.ts` 6/6 on the stunt node. **Ground truth correction:** on Samba 4.22 `testparm` cannot show the settings `vfs_fruit` enforces for a Time Machine share (`durable handles`, `posix locking`, `kernel oplocks`) — they are connect-time module behaviour, invisible to the config dumper — so the proof asserts the stanza ANAS writes (exactly the composed line and the two fruit keys, nothing else) and the runtime side stays community-verified, as the beta label says.

**ahrcache.1** `[done 2026-09-25]` AHR read cache — lvmcache writethrough (GitHub #63; design `AHR-DESIGN.md` §13, resolved 2026-09-24 by GT-18…GT-24) — as a user with an SSD to spare, I want a read cache in front of an AHR pool that I can attach and detach from the UI, that the node survives losing, so hot reads come off flash without a new way to lose data. **Build (daemon):** two §5.1 detect-then-delta step kinds — `cache-attach` (one GPT slice by by-id via the create path's `sgdisk` + `udevadm settle` → `wipefs -a` → `pvcreate` → `vgextend` → `lvcreate -n <pool>-cache` linear across the cache slices → `lvconvert -y --type cache --cachevol … --cachemode writethrough`) and `cache-detach` (`lvconvert --uncache` → `vgreduce` → `pvremove` → `wipefs` → `sgdisk -d` so the disk reads available); routes `POST /v1/ahr/:pool/cache` `{disks[]}` and `DELETE /v1/ahr/:pool/cache` (jobs; attach refuses a non-composable disk and an already-cached pool; a spinning disk is legal and gets one advisory sentence); **health = `dmsetup status`** on every AHR read of a cached pool (`cache Error` ⇒ `cache.state: failed`, pool `degraded`, dashboard `ahr` warning); **auto-uncache on detection through an event**: a udev rule on the cache disk's removal (`packaging/`, the `anas-md-event.sh` pattern) calls a daemon endpoint that runs the recovery (uncache → `vgreduce --removemissing` → `anas-ahr` notification), the boot rung runs the same at activation; `GET /v1/ahr/:pool` carries `cache: { devices[], sizeBytes, mode: 'writethrough', policy (from lvs), state: healthy|failed|absent, hits, misses, usedBlocks, totalBlocks, dirtyBlocks (0 by construction; non-zero flagged), counters gated on state }` plus `filesystem.readOnly` with the reason; **Remount** verb (`POST /v1/ahr/:pool/remount`: unmount + mount; confirm-coded — open share handles break; refused while a LUN holds the pool) for the btrfs forced-readonly aftermath; a returning cache disk's outdated PV label is wiped by attach and by the repair. **Consumers:** `GET /v1/disks` classifies the cache disk `in-use`, role `cache`, attributed to the pool (today `other`, unattributed); capacity derives from the LV — audit every `vg.sizeBytes` reader; destroy adds the cache slice to the wipe list; expansion (`lvextend` on the cached origin works live) needs no split. SSD wear is NOT rendered in the Cache block — wear is a per-disk SMART fact the Disks tab owns (`disks.1`); one place, no duplicate. **Workflow (§2 rule):** *Where:* the AHR menu — the pool detail gains a **Cache** block; the pool toolbar gains **Attach cache…** / **Detach cache** (and **Remount** when read-only). *Happy path:* select a pool → Attach cache… → the composable-disk picker (SSDs first; a spinning pick gets "a rotating cache adds a seek, not speed") → the two-sentence advisory (hotspot cache: repeated random reads benefit, sequential streams do not; the SSD is a consumable) → job → the Cache block shows devices, size, hits/misses climbing on later loads (wear: the Disks tab (SMART), not repeated here); Detach cache → confirm ("reads continue from the pool; the SSD is wiped and returns to available") — a PLAIN `Ext.Msg.confirm`, since the route is deliberately not confirm-gated: a 202 verb that wipes a consumable SSD earns a click-through, not a confirm code (and `confirmAndRun` would show no dialog at all against a 202) → job → the block reads "no cache". *Timeliness:* the block's counters refresh with the detail load (the pull); grid and detail reload after every job, success AND failure. *Failure as the user meets it:* the cache device fails while live → the event uncaches within seconds; the AHR warning card and the notification say "cache device <id> failed on <pool>; reads restored uncached" and, when btrfs flipped read-only, "writes are stopped until the pool is remounted" with the Remount verb on the toolbar (confirm text names the open-handle break; refused while an iSCSI LUN holds the pool, naming it); a failed attach (stale signature, disk vanished) fails the job with the step's sentence and leaves the pool uncached and clean. *Proof:* unit tests for both step kinds (detect-then-delta over `lvs`/`pvs`/`dmsetup status` fixtures incl. `fixtures/ahr/lvs-cached-pool.json` and the `cache Error` token), the health read (stale counters gated), the disks classification, the udev handler's endpoint and the boot rung;
