The run's progress and the schedule detail report pruned/held counts per dataset, not one number. **Not recursive:** unchanged. **AHR:** unchanged (whole `@data`). **Proof:** unit tests on the per-dataset plan (child converges; held child skipped and reported; excluded dataset cleared; excluded dataset covered by another same-bucket schedule left alone; non-recursive untouched); live on the stunt node through the API over the pvepool fixture, extending `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts`: pre-seed droppings by hand (`zfs snapshot -r` with older `anas-hourly-<stamp>` names on the target tree, including the excluded child), run the schedule once, assert the target and kept sibling hold exactly the retention count, the excluded child and its grandchild hold zero, and a held child snapshot survives and is reported. **Shipped (d1174f6):** 11 service tests + 2 route tests + notify test; `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts` droppings test (seeded `-r` droppings incl. the excluded child and the PVE-owned nested storage root, one held sibling snapshot) green ×2 on the stunt node — target and sibling at the policy count, excluded subtree and the owned root at zero, the held one reported in `skippedHeld`, per-dataset counts in `datasets[]`.

**taskstatus.1** `[done 2026-10-06, f088778]` **(GitHub #72, filed 2026-10-06 on 0.4.1 by the #70 reporter while 0.4.2 was being cut — the same symptom from a European time zone; reply asks for confirmation on 0.4.2.)** **(bug on a shipped feature, operator 2026-10-06 on a production node after the day's PVE reboot: "All jobs show never run. Next run shows correct timing")** As a user looking at the Backup, Cloud Sync or Snapshot Schedules grid after the node rebooted, I want the last run and its verdict to survive the reboot, so a task that ran yesterday does not read "never run" until it fires again. **Found:** `deriveTaskStatus` (`task-units.ts`, backup + cloud) the schedule status derivation (`snapshot-schedule-units.ts` ~278) and replication's own copy (`replication-units.ts` ~381/423) take `lastRunAt` from the SERVICE unit's `ExecMainExitTimestamp` / `InactiveEnterTimestamp` and the verdict from its `Result` / `ExecMainStatus`; systemd does not persist service runtime properties across a boot, so every task reads "never run" after a reboot while the next-run column (timer, persistent) stays right. Confirmed on the node: service timestamps empty, timer `LastTriggerUSec` and the stamp file intact, the runner's JSON result line in the journal. **Fix (one shared derivation, used by all four: backup, cloud, schedules, replication):** when the service carries no exit timestamp and is not active, take the last run from the journal's last runner result line (`{"task":…,"result":{"status":…}}` — `readLastSuccessAt` already scans for it; generalise to "last result of any status", returning status, timestamp and the cancelled reason) and set the verdict from it; if the journal holds no result line (rotated, or the unit ran under a build that printed none), fall back to the timer's `LastTriggerUSec` with `lastRunResult: 'unknown'` and a `lastRunNote` "ran at <time>; result not retained across the reboot" (honest, not a fake success). Live service properties win whenever present (a run since boot). `lastSuccessAt` / overdue keep their current journal path. **Proof:** unit tests with injected properties for the three shapes (live props; empty props + journal result; empty props + no journal line + timer stamp) in the task-units and schedule-units suites; live on the stunt node: a backup task, a snapshot schedule and a replication task each run once (replication at least by unit test if no local-target replication fixture is cheap), the node is rebooted, and GET status shows the pre-reboot last run and verdict for both, with the API spec asserting it. **Not in scope:** persisting anything ourselves (stateless), changing what the runner prints. **Shipped:** the whole derivation extracted into `unit-run-status.ts` (operator: "no reason for this kind of bug to be distributed around"); backup/cloud, schedules and replication are thin callers keeping only their kind-specific layers (result map + cadence overdue; exit code only from the live rung; ZFS lag). A rung-2 guard: a timer stamp newer than the last journal result line by >5 s falls to the timer rung (a failed run prints no result line). Residual: a failed manual Run-Now followed by a reboot shows the earlier success. Proof: 4-rung unit suite + one wiring test per caller; `task-status-reboot.spec.ts` (schedule via its unit + a cloud task, node rebooted, status identical after) green ×2; backup by unit test (no PBS on the stunt node).


**Batch D — identity fixes that drive destructive actions (operator 2026-10-06, from `docs/IDENTITY-AUDIT-2026-10-06.md`: "the most critical ident bugs that drive destructive actions, and related changes needed to clean those").** Four stories on disjoint files; each bug is on a shipped feature; each destructive path gets a live proof on the stunt node (sequenced-mutation rule) before the tag.

**GLM review of batch D (2026-10-06):** no unconditional data loss; ten findings, six fixed before the tag as batch E: replication hold release is all-or-nothing per side and never on an unreadable task list (a failed hold on the new base must not release the old one); the datasets router never falls through to a snapshot route on a read failure (a transient `zfs list` error had reverted `tank/snapshots/old` to snapshot destroy); the source guard discloses missing ZFS facts and refuses rather than silently losing the unmounted-dataset protection; recycle containment anchored at the share path; the UI confirm resend re-prompts on a fresh code (IDENTITY_MISMATCH) instead of alerting; untested gate failure paths covered. Recorded as intended: a deleted or disabled schedule's stamped snapshots are kept (the delete dialog already says so) and a schedule re-created under the same id adopts them (ids are user-supplied); `blkid` failing on a file vdev skips the file (fail-closed); a legacy-named cred file of a line that names no file survives edit (identity epic).

**ident.1** `[done 2026-10-06, 941b6ad + 56f6505]` **ZFS: ownership stamping + stable-id confirms + wipe-by-identity + reserved names (audit #1, #2, #11, #12, #20).** (a) Every scheduled ZFS snapshot is taken with `-o anas:schedule=<id>` (one atomic verb, same call); prune destroys only snapshots carrying THIS schedule's stamp; an unstamped `anas-<bucket>-*` snapshot (pre-0.4.2 take) is prunable only when exactly one enabled schedule targets or sweeps that dataset, else left with a per-dataset note; a bucket absent from the policy is left alone (no longer "keep 0") — the stamp makes the cross-schedule collision impossible and the absent-bucket rule stops a "minimal" preset from erasing a sibling schedule's hourlies; received snapshots carry the sender's stamp and are never this schedule's. (b) One helper in `safety/` binds a stable id into the confirm params and re-reads it in the job: pool destroy/export bind the pool guid (`zpool get guid`), dataset destroy binds the dataset guid and re-runs `ownedDescendant` + the LUN-held check in the job, rollback binds the target snapshot's createtxg AND the newest snapshot's createtxg (a newer snapshot since the challenge = refuse, re-challenge); mismatch = 409 with the reason, never a silent act on a namesake. (c) Pool-destroy disk cleanup: a leaf path that is not a kernel path is used as given (never the stripped `by-id` whole disk); non-ONLINE leaves are skipped; before any `wipefs`/`labelclear`/`zap` the leaf's `blkid -p -s UUID` must equal the pool guid captured before the destroy. (d) `DatasetPath` reserves the sub-resource segments `snapshots`, `access`, `permissions` at create and rename (400), and the datasets router treats a path that names an EXISTING dataset as a dataset, never as a snapshot sub-route. (e) Recursive chown/chmod/setfacl refuse when `ownedDescendant` is non-null. **Proof:** unit tests per rule; live: a schedule take shows the property, two schedules on one dataset keep each other's buckets, a renamed-away-and-recreated pool refuses the queued destroy with 409, a by-partuuid leaf pool destroy with cleanup wipes only its leaves (loop devices), a dataset named `tank/snapshots/x` is destroyed as a dataset. **Shipped:** `ident-zfs.spec.ts` 5/5 live (stamp with source local on target and child; standard + minimal schedules on one dataset keep each other's buckets; a pool swapped under the same name → 409 IDENTITY_MISMATCH naming both guids; `<pool>/snapshots/x` destroyed as a dataset, `<pool>@x` survives, new reserved name 400; by-partuuid leaf cleanup wipes only its partition); `schedules-exclude-api.spec.ts` 7/7 with stamped droppings and a `zfs send -p` copy read as `received` and left. Judgement calls recorded: own cadence bucket always planned (0 when absent); stamped snapshots still pruned under an incomplete schedule list; in-job schedule re-read deferred to the identity epic.

**ident.2** `[done 2026-10-06, b633ce4]` **iSCSI + image restore: the served device is the identity (audit #8, #9, #10, #14).** (a) Before any zfs verb on a LUN's backing (resize, destroyBacking, repair) the served backstore's major:minor (configfs) must equal `stat(/dev/zvol/<ds>).rdev`; mismatch = 409 "LUN serves a different device than its path names". (b) LUN delete and resize confirms bind `{target, serial, backstore}`; the job re-reads the LUN under `withIscsiLock` and refuses on mismatch (index reuse can never hit a new LUN). (c) Whole-image restore: under the lock the job re-reads the LUN and requires the same serial, backing path, rdev and size; the confirm binds `{serial, snapshot, archive}`. (d) PBS backup group: creating a task refuses a `(repo, namespace, backupId)` already used by another task (409 naming it); new tasks default `backupId` to `<host>-<task>`; existing tasks keep theirs. **Proof:** unit tests; live on the iSCSI fixture: rename-and-recreate a zvol under a live LUN → resize and delete refuse; delete+add within the confirm TTL → resend refuses; restore to a re-created LUN refuses. **Shipped:** `ident-iscsi.spec.ts` 3/3 live (rename-and-recreate under a live LUN → grow and destroyBacking delete 409 served-device-mismatch, plain delete allowed; delete+add within the TTL → stale code refused with a fresh challenge; stale restore code after re-create refused, restore after rename-and-recreate 409, served volume keeps its marker). Resize has no confirm by design (grow is non-destructive); repair can only refuse a path another live backstore serves.

**ident.3** `[done 2026-10-06, baa6353]` **AHR: destructive verbs act only on members whose on-disk identity is ours (audit #3, #4, #5, #6, #7).** (a) Create and spare-attach re-run `collectDisks`/`isComposableDisk` inside the job before the first destructive step; a disk enters `planned` only after its own wipe succeeds, so rollback never zaps a disk we never touched. (b) Destroy, cache detach and the boot recovery ladder act only on partitions whose `mdadm --examine --export` UUID is pinned in mdadm.conf (or live for this pool) and on cache slices that are PVs of this pool's VG UUID; the ladder never `--run`s an unpinned array; create refuses a pool name that already has arrays, labels or pins. (c) Spare removal acts only on members mdstat flags `(S)` at job time; an active member = 409. (d) Destroy clears the expansion intent and returns 409 while any `ahr.*` job or an intent run is in flight; the intent records the md/VG UUIDs and a mismatch reads as stale (never "halted"); resume re-checks each disk and goes through a confirm. **Proof:** unit tests; live on loop devices (the self-heal rig): a foreign `media-r1` array on the node survives a pool destroy and a reboot-ladder run; a spare that took over a rebuild cannot be removed; destroy-then-recreate under the same name shows no stale intent. **Shipped:** `test/stunt-node/ident3-gt.sh run` 39/39 live on loop devices + hot7–9 (a spare that took over a rebuild → 409 on removal and left in the array; a foreign unpinned `media-r1` ghost — built by INCREMENTAL assembly, the shape udev leaves at boot — stays inactive across an anasd restart with the scan logging `action=ignored reason=not-pinned-not-in-vg`; the same foreign array survives DELETE /v1/ahr/media with its UUID, superblocks and data byte-identical, reported as `foreignArrays`; create refuses the occupied name before any confirm; a halted intent is removed by destroy; after recreate under the same name no expansion, a planted stale record reads stale, resume 409). Rig lessons (test infra): an explicit `--assemble --no-degraded` of a lone member creates no array and a plain assemble starts a RAID1 member degraded; the ghost must be addressed by its kernel device (the md/ link is not created for an inactive array); holding the udev exec queue hangs the destroy (partition events). Also on the node: the leftover `lpahr` fixture pool from an earlier rig was removed through the gated destroy (arrays stopped, pins and labels cleared) as an unplanned live exercise.

**ident.4** `[done 2026-10-06, 0a32e87]` **Mounts, replication, cloud, recycle: act on what is actually there (audit #13, #15, #16, #17, #18, #19).** (a) Replication hold tag is per task (`anas-repl-<hash(location,target)>`); a run releases only its own tag; the existing global tag is migrated on the task's next run (release global, take own) so no base is ever unheld in between. (b) Cloud remote PUT that changes the destination (type, endpoint, bucket, path, host) is a 409 + confirm naming every task that references the remote, through wrappers. (c) The source guard realpaths the source and adds ZFS `mountpoint,canmount,mounted` facts: an unmounted dataset or a target whose live source differs from the spec is "not mounted" (refused), not an empty success. (d) Mount delete removes a credentials file only when it lives under the ANAS creds dir and no other fstab line references it; the generated name is hashed from the full mountpoint so two mountpoints can never share one. (e) Mount remove/unmount/disable/edit `umount` only when the live source and fstype at the target match the fstab spec; otherwise 409 naming what is mounted there. (f) Recycle purge `lstat`s the bin, refuses a symlink, requires a single-segment repository name and checks every file's realpath stays under the bin. **Proof:** unit tests; live: two replication tasks on one source keep both bases; a local fs mounted at a failed CIFS target survives Remove; a symlinked `#recycle` is skipped with a note. **Shipped:** `ident-mounts-repl.spec.ts` 4/4 live (two tasks on one source keep both bases under their own tags; a loop-backed ext4 at a failed CIFS target survives Remove with a 409 naming it; remote PUT changing the endpoint → 409 naming the task; symlinked `#recycle` skipped with the note). Spec helper fixed during the run: Run-now starts the unit `--no-block` and answers `{started:true}`, completion = the runner's result line in the unit journal.

## 0.4.3 shipped — moved verbatim from `docs/EPICS.md` §3 on 2026-10-14 (cloudproof.1–2 from 0.4.1, and release 0.4.3, 2026-10-12)

### After 0.4.0 — accepted 2026-09-26 (retro seed → test infrastructure, operator: "a spun-up S3-compatible endpoint would exercise that path; SMB/WebDAV are trivial")

**cloudproof.1** `[done 2026-09-27]` Cloud sync live proofs over real backends without accounts — the 0.4.0 proofs exercise every task mechanism against the node's own sshd (sftp) and Google Drive by the operator's hand; every other curated backend is covered only by form and catalogue tests, and the one defect class that got through this cycle (a per-backend credential shape rclone's Test cannot see) is exactly what a real endpoint would catch. **Build (fixture, `test/stunt-node/cloud-review-fixture.sh` or a sibling `cloud-backends-fixture.sh`):** four profiles, each `up`/`down`/`status`, loopback only, fixed credentials, nothing left behind on `down`: (1) `s3` — a single-binary S3 server on the node (MinIO preferred: rclone's `s3` provider `Minio`; Garage as fallback), installed under `/opt/anas-test/` from the vendor's static build, run as a transient unit (`systemd-run --unit anas-gt-minio …`) on 127.0.0.1:9000 with a fixed access/secret pair and a pre-created bucket; (2) `smb` — the node's own Samba: a fixture share on a plain directory with a fixture user, remote type `smb` host 127.0.0.1; (3) `webdav` — `rclone serve webdav` (1.60 has it) on 127.0.0.1 with `--user/--pass`, transient unit; (4) `ftp` — `rclone serve ftp` likewise. **Spec:** one `cloud-backends.spec.ts` that, for each profile, creates the remote through the API with that backend's curated fields (proving the guided form's field set is sufficient per backend), Test → `ok`, a copy task with ~12 files → files present at the destination (checked through the backend itself: `mc`/`rclone ls` for S3, `smbclient` for SMB, `curl` for WebDAV, `rclone ls` for FTP), a sync run with one source file deleted → gone at the destination, a wrong-password Test → `auth` verdict with the backend's sentence, and the remove-while-referenced refusal; then the remote's non-secret options as `GET /v1/cloud/remotes` reports them. Run ×2 per backend. **Acceptance:** 4 backends green ×2 on the node; each profile's `down` verified clean; the fixture never touches the operator's remotes/tasks or existing shares/users. Test infrastructure; slips freely. A real cloud S3 (AWS/B2/Wasabi) stays operator-gated.
**Result (2026-09-27):** built in one commit — `test/stunt-node/cloud-backends-fixture.sh` (s3/smb/webdav/ftp profiles, `up` idempotent teardown-first, `down` verifies unit + dirs + server process + smb user/share line by line, loopback only, fixed credentials) and `tests/integration/cloud-backends.spec.ts` (per backend: curated create → non-secret listing + `secretsSet` → Test `ok` → copy run of 12 files + a `*.tmp` exclude verified THROUGH the backend (rclone env-remote for s3/ftp, smbclient for SMB, PROPFIND for WebDAV) → sync run with one source file deleted → wrong-credential Test → the 409 remove-guard → task then remote gone); `up all` runs inside the spec's beforeAll, `down all` verifies in afterAll. S3 server: **versitygw 1.8.0** (posix backend, bucket = directory, provider `Other`; MinIO ruled out by the operator, the earlier attempt's fallback garage binary left on disk unused). rclone 1.60 has NO `serve ftp` (dlna/docker/http/restic/sftp/webdav only), so the ftp server is python3-pyftpdlib. **4 backends ×2 green** on the stunt node; every `down` verified clean; the operator's gtest remote, testoff task, existing shares/users and the gtbackup/gtiscsi pools untouched. Findings: the curated essential sets SUFFICED for all four backends (no `force_path_style` needed for versitygw on provider `Other`, no smb `domain`); the test-verdict classifier answers `auth` for s3 (403) and webdav (401) but only `error` for SMB's NT logon failure and FTP's 530 — real credential failures below the generic verdict (AUTH_RE widening is a 0.4.1 candidate); rclone's smb backend names the SHARE as the first path component, so a task's remote-side path under an SMB remote is `<share>/<subpath>` and the Test door (remote root = share list) cannot catch a bare-subpath mistake; against pyftpdlib rclone 1.60's ftp backend cannot create a missing top-level destination directory (`501 No such directory` instead of the standard 550 to a LIST of a missing path) — the fixture pre-creates the landing dir; a pyftpdlib quirk, not a product defect. 0.4.1 fixes: the two credential-failure lines were captured on the node (fixtures `probe-smb-auth-fail-1.60.1.log` / `probe-ftp-auth-fail-1.60.1.log`) and AUTH_RE now classifies them `auth`; the task wizard shows the SMB share-name path hint and the task create/update doors refuse an empty path under an smb remote.


**cloudproof.2** `[done 2026-09-27]` Cloud sync live proofs over emulated cloud stores and an encrypted wrapper (accepted 2026-09-27, operator: "do the cloud storage testing before this point release — azurite, fake-gcs, crypt"; follows cloudproof.1) — three more real protocol paths with no account: (1) **Azure Blob via Azurite** (Microsoft's emulator, an npm package; the node runs Node 22): `azurite-blob` on 127.0.0.1:10000 as a transient unit under `/opt/anas-test/azurite`, the well-known dev account `devstoreaccount1` and its published key, a container created through rclone; rclone's `azureblob` needs the emulator endpoint (`endpoint http://127.0.0.1:10000/devstoreaccount1` or `use_emulator true`) — if that option sits outside the curated essential set (account, key), that is a curation finding to record, not a product change here. (2) **Google Cloud Storage via fake-gcs-server** (single Go binary from its GitHub releases; filesystem backend under `/opt/anas-test/gcs-data`, http scheme on 127.0.0.1:4443, public host set to the same); rclone's `google cloud storage` backend with `anonymous true` and `endpoint http://127.0.0.1:4443/storage/v1/` — an UNCURATED backend, so the proof exercises rclone's own basic option list as the dialog renders it; the emulator takes anonymous access, so credentials are not proven here and the entry says so. (3) **crypt over the loopback sftp remote**: a `crypt` remote whose `remote` is `<sftp remote>:crypt-base` with a password (rclone types it as a password, so ANAS stores it obscured and never returns it — assert both), default name encryption; the proof must show the underlying sftp directory holds encrypted names and sizes, that `rclone cat` through an env-config crypt remote with the same password yields the original bytes, that a sync deletion propagates, and what Test answers for a wrong crypt password (rclone reports undecryptable names; record the verdict as a finding if it is not `auth`). **Fixture:** three profiles in `test/stunt-node/cloud-backends-fixture.sh` (`azure`, `gcs`, `crypt`, the last bringing up its own sftp user), each `up` idempotent and each `down` verified, binaries kept under `/opt/anas-test/`. **Spec:** `tests/integration/cloud-backends-2.spec.ts` running cloudproof.1's six steps per backend (create with the curated or rclone-basic fields as the dialog offers them, Test ok, copy with an exclude verified through the backend itself — Azurite and fake-gcs via `rclone ls` env-config, crypt via the underlying sftp AND through the crypt remote — sync deletion, wrong-credential Test, remove-while-referenced then remove) with the step runner shared with `cloud-backends.spec.ts` (one helper, not a copy). **Acceptance:** 3 backends ×2 green on the node; findings recorded honestly; 0.4.1 ships after this with any product fix it forces. **Result (2026-09-27):** built in one commit — three fixture profiles (`azure`/`gcs`/`crypt`, each `up` idempotent, every `down` line verified) and the six-step runner extracted into `tests/integration/fixtures/cloud-runner.ts`, now consumed by BOTH specs (cloud-backends.spec.ts re-run 4/4 after the extraction). Azurite under `/opt/anas-test/azurite` (npm, Node 22) as anas-gt-azure on :10000, container `gtcontainer` created through rclone; fake-gcs-server **v1.56.1** as anas-gt-gcs on :4443, bucket = directory under the filesystem root, listed live; crypt over the node's sshd (user `gtcryptsftp`, the cloud-review-fixture drop-in pattern, `crypt-base` pre-created OWNED BY THE USER — a root-created one is unwritable and the copy fails). **3 backends ×2 green** on the stunt node; `gtest`/`testoff` untouched; tsc + eslint clean. **rclone 1.60.1 facts (Debian 1.60.1+dfsg-4, captured live):** azureblob reaches an http emulator ONLY with `use_emulator=true` AND `endpoint=http://127.0.0.1:10000/devstoreaccount1` — `endpoint` alone builds `https://<account>.<endpoint>` (https only), and `use_emulator` alone fails because 1.60.1 defaults `endpoint` to `blob.core.windows.net` BEFORE the emulator branch reads it — and under `use_emulator` rclone IGNORES account/key (hardcoded dev credentials), so the curated essentials are not even used against Azurite; gcs needs `anonymous` + `project_number` (1.60 refuses a bucket listing without one) + `endpoint` — `endpoint` EXISTS on 1.60 but is ADVANCED. **Findings:** (1) azureblob curation gap — reaching Azurite needs `use_emulator` (standard) and `endpoint` (advanced), both outside the curated set {account, key}, and there is NO shape that answers `auth` against the emulator: a wrong key under use_emulator tests `ok` (ignored), without it `error` (base64 parse failure before any request); (2) gcs endpoint is advanced (dialog tucks it under More options) and credentials are unproven by design (anonymous); (3) a wrong CRYPT password tests `ok` — rclone logs undecryptable names at Debug level and the probe lists the remote root, so no error surfaces (a deeper `path` answers `not-found`: the encrypted directory name does not decrypt) — the Test door cannot catch a wrong crypt password; (4) the remote delete door checks TASK references only: DELETE `gtcryptsftp` while `gtcrypt` names it answers 202 and strands the crypt remote (next use: "didn't find section in config file") — remote-to-remote references unchecked. The crypt proof itself is green: the config file carries the obscured password (never the plain word), the API lists `secretsSet: [password]`, the underlying sftp tree holds encrypted directory AND file names (64-byte files vs 16-byte sources), and `rclone cat` through an env-config crypt remote returns byte-identical bytes (base64-compared). 0.4.1 fixes: the wrong crypt password is caught — a crypt remote is probed with debug logging (captured -vv lines `Skipping undecryptable dir/file name: bad PKCS#7 padding - too long`; the listing SUCCEEDS EMPTY, so the lines, not the emptiness, are the discriminator) and answers `auth` with a fixed sentence, an empty base staying `ok` with a nothing-stored caveat (fixtures `probe-crypt-{empty,good,wrong}-debug-1.60.1.*`); and the remote delete door refuses with 409 when another remote's `remote`/`upstreams`/`remotes` option names it (the option-NAME rule covers crypt, alias, cache, chunker, compress, hasher, union, combine on the 1.60 provider capture), wrapper removed first.

### 0.4.3 — bug release, slate ACCEPTED 2026-10-11 (GitHub #74, #75; pve5 incident of the same day)

Operator ruling 2026-10-11: #75 is a real bug on a shipped feature → same-day point release per §2; #74 is a support case whose only product item is visibility. Four stories, nothing else: *pools.r1* stays out (a boot-path change would pull the release into the bake-day matrix under ruling (e)); #73 is a feature; the installer manifest is a nicety. Gates: ruling (a) one fresh whole-diff review before the release commit, ruling (f) the build on one stable node before the tag, hand-written release notes. **Design rulings (operator, 2026-10-11):** the ZFS permissions editor (4.7.2) is THE permissions writer — one mechanism, one dialog, one outcome, with the two targets differing only where the filesystem differs and each difference stated in the dialog when it applies (parallel construction). The share-create grant is that editor with the form pre-filled, never a second writer. Recursive apply to existing files is always the user's explicit, confirm-gated choice: a default that rewrites a tree is a destructive action hidden inside "create share", and a default that does not must say so.

**access.1** `[done 2026-10-11, cb0f369 + 974a27a]` **Share creation grants the selected principals access on the share path (GitHub #75).** Today the SMB/NFS create routes write only the service config; the mountpoint of a fresh pool stays `root:root 0755`, so every share on it is read-only in practice for named users and for guest (`map to guest = Bad User` → nobody). **Build (daemon):** lift `getAccess`/`setAccess`/`setPermissions`/`resolveAccessTarget` out of `routes/datasets.ts` into a service with a target resolver (ZFS dataset or pool root → mountpoint; AHR pool → LV mountpoint; share → its path); behaviour byte-identical for the datasets routes (existing tests must pass unchanged — ruling (b): an assertion that has to change is a stop-and-report). The share create (and edit, when the principal list changes) composes a `SetAccessRequest` on the share path: each selected user/group → named entry at Read-Write, or Read when the share is read-only; guest access → Everyone at the same level; owner/group untouched; existing named entries preserved; access AND default entries so new files inherit; `applyToExisting` only from the explicit option, as the same confirm-gated recursive job the editor runs, with the same PVE-descendant refusal. On a PVE-owned or system-pool path the share is still created and the response carries the grant's refusal reason. The grant is never revoked by removing a principal or deleting the share (the filesystem carries no record of who wrote an ACL — identity-audit class); the dialog states this once. **Build (UI):** the share dialogs carry one checkbox "Grant the selected users access on the share path" (default on) with "apply to existing files" beneath it (default off, confirm) and one sentence: existing files keep their permissions unless applied. The share row gets a Permissions button opening the shared editor on the share path. **Proof:** a fresh ZFS pool and a fresh AHR pool, SMB share to a named user and a guest share on each, write from a client succeeds; the editor read-back on the share path shows exactly the rows the share granted; re-running the ZFS editor's test-suite unchanged; PVE-owned path → share created, grant refused with the 409 text. **Result (2026-10-11):** the writer moved verbatim into `services/access.ts` behind a target resolver (`zfs` dataset or pool root → its mountpoint; `ahr` pool → the mounted `@data`; `share` path → its home via the self-service resolver: ZFS dataset, AHR pool or neither); the datasets routes call it and every pre-existing access/datasets test passes unchanged (the job params and results keep their keys and order). `POST`/`PUT /v1/shares/smb` take `grantAccess: {applyToExisting?}`: after the `smb.conf` write and reload the job reads the target exactly as the dialog does and sends the editor's own request — each `valid users` principal (`@`/`+`/`&` = group; `%` macros and names `getent` cannot resolve are skipped and listed) at Read-Write, Read on a read-only share, guest → Everyone, owner/group untouched, every live named entry kept and NEVER lowered (a lowered level is a revocation), access + default ACL; on an edit it runs only when the principal set, guest, read-only or path changed, or `applyToExisting` asks. The share is saved whatever the grant answers: `ShareAccessGrantResult` (applied / refused with the gate's sentence / failed / nothing) rides on the job result as `grant` and the dialog alerts on refused/failed. PVE territory: share create already refuses a path on an owned dataset (pvepool.1), so the refusal-with-share-created cases are the recursive PVE-descendant refusal, a non-ZFS path inside a `dir` storage path (caveat-emptor shares), and an AHR home held by a LUN. Share rows (SMB and NFS) open the shared editor through `GET/PUT /v1/shares/smb/:name/access` and `/v1/shares/nfs/:path/access`. Recursive apply: the daemon has no confirm gate on `applyToExisting` (it never had one on the datasets route, and adding one would change three access.test.ts assertions — ruling (b)), so the confirm is the dialog's: the shared Permissions dialog and the share dialog both ask before sending it. Tests: 19 in `share-access-grant.test.ts`, the UI checks in `dialog-contracts.harness.mjs` §13. Live proof owed (separate job). **Rulings (operator, 2026-10-11)** `[done 2026-10-11]`: (1) the recursive apply gets the API confirm gate — `applyToExisting` on every door that can carry it (`PUT …/access` on a dataset, an AHR pool, an SMB share and an NFS export, and the SMB create/edit `grantAccess.applyToExisting`) answers `409` + `X-Anas-Confirm-Code` through the existing `confirmGate`, asked after every refusal, headline "Applying to existing files rewrites the permissions of every file and directory under '<path>'", warnings naming what the walk reaches; nothing is written before the resend (a share create with the flag is not created until confirmed). The dialogs' own confirm is gone: both doors submit through `ANAS.confirmAndRun` with a new `confirmHeadline` option that leads the prompt with the 409's headline verbatim; a create without the flag is a plain submit. The three `access.test.ts` recursive cases (and the AHR and share-grant recursive cases) now take the 409 and resend with the code. (2) No held-by-LUN refusal on a share-path grant — an AHR home holding a LUN now grants (test added). (3) A recursive named grant on a ZFS share path switches POSIX ACLs on for the child datasets mounted under it before the walk, and `ShareAccessGrantResult` gains `childAclEnabled`; the share dialog toasts the names.

**access.2** `[done 2026-10-11, cb0f369 + 974a27a]` **AHR pools get the same permissions editor (GitHub #75, parallel construction).** `GET/PUT /v1/ahr/:name/access` on the LV mountpoint through the access.1 service, same request/response schemas, same dialog (`60-datasets.js`'s Permissions dialog moves to the shared layer and is opened from Datasets, AHR and Shares). Where the targets differ, shown not hidden: btrfs has POSIX ACLs on by default so the `acltype=posixacl`/`xattr=sa` step and its note never appear for AHR (the resolver says so; never `zfs set` on a non-ZFS target); the recursive apply sweeps the subvolumes under `@data` as one tree (`@snapshots` is a sibling outside the mountpoint and is never touched), while on ZFS it crosses every child dataset mounted under the path, switching POSIX ACLs on first for each that has them off, and lists those in the result (corrected 2026-10-11: this sentence said the walk stops at child datasets — `setfacl -R` never did); the acl-package hint is identical on both. ~~The `heldByLun` hands-off gate applies as on every AHR mutation.~~ (withdrawn by the operator ruling of 2026-10-11 below). **Proof:** the same scenario matrix as the ZFS editor's tests run against an AHR fixture; the dialog opened from an AHR row and from a dataset row renders the same rows, levels and options with only the stated differences. **Result (2026-10-11):** `routes/ahr-access.ts` (`GET/PUT /v1/ahr/:name/access`, 404 for an unknown or unmounted pool, GET stamps `backing: 'ahr'`) through the access.1 service with the shared schemas; the target resolver is the only place the filesystems differ — no `acltype` read or `zfs set` for a non-ZFS home (`aclEnabled` is native), PVE territory for a non-ZFS directory is storage.cfg's `path`/`mountpoint` keys (at or above the mount → 400; inside the tree, recursive only → 400; unreadable storage.cfg → refuse), and the held-by-LUN gate is a hard 409 on every PUT (reads stay open). The recursive walk on ZFS does NOT stop at child datasets today — `setfacl -R`/`chown -R`/`chmod -R` cross into them and the PVE-descendant refusal guards the walk; that behaviour is kept unchanged and the Datasets help sentence is unchanged. `60-datasets.js`'s dialog moved to `14-permissions.js` (`ANAS.permissions.open`), opened from Datasets, the Hybrid RAID toolbar (Permissions, greyed unmounted or LUN-held) and the Shares toolbar; it states the AHR differences itself (no acltype note, the subvolume sentence under Apply to existing files too) and reads `backing` from a share path's GET. Tests: 19 in `ahr-access.test.ts` (the ZFS editor's scenario matrix plus the held, PVE-path and unmounted cases); the harness proves a dataset row and an AHR row render the same rows, levels, options and pre-fill and send the same body to their own paths. **Rulings (operator, 2026-10-11)** `[done 2026-10-11]`: (1) the recursive apply is confirm-gated at the daemon on `PUT /v1/ahr/:name/access` as on every access door (see access.1); the AHR confirm's warnings carry the subvolume sweep and any iSCSI LUN image under the mount. (2) The hard `heldByLun` 409 on the AHR access PUT is dropped: a permission change is not destructive to a LUN image, the ZFS editor never gated a dataset holding one, and as built an AHR pool holding a LUN could never get a writable share. The pool rows keep `heldByLun`; the Hybrid RAID toolbar's Permissions button no longer greys for a LUN-held pool (Destroy and Change mount still do). (3) The ZFS walk crosses child datasets, and the fix lands before the first write: before a recursive apply with named entries on a ZFS target (a dataset or a share path on one), after the PVE-descendant refusal, the job lists the descendants (`zfs list -r -H -o name,mountpoint,acltype`), keeps those mounted under the path, and runs the target's own first-grant rule (`zfs set acltype=posixacl xattr=sa`) on each with ACLs off; an unreadable list or a failed enable fails the job before any chown/chmod/setfacl and names the child; the result lists them as `childAclEnabled`, the confirm's warnings name the children crossed and those to be switched on, and the dialog toasts them. Tests: `access.test.ts` (the confirm, child off → enabled then applied, already posixacl → untouched, mounted elsewhere/legacy/none → ignored, no named entry → nothing switched, enable failure and unreadable list → nothing applied, PVE-owned descendant → refused before anything), `ahr-access.test.ts` (the confirm with the subvolume sentence, a wrong code re-challenged, the LUN disclosure, a LUN-held pool's change runs), `share-access-grant.test.ts` (every share door's 409, the LUN-held AHR grant, a child dataset under a ZFS share path); harness §13 and the real `confirmAndRun` flow.

**access.r1** `[done 2026-10-11, c466118 + 70606d3 + f0fcb15]` **Live-proof follow-up to access.1/access.2 (operator rulings 2026-10-11).** (F1) The recursive apply's spec carries `X` for every execute bit, the mask included (`rwX`, `r-X`, `m::rwX`); a literal `x` made setfacl resolve every `X` to `x`, so every plain file became executable. The walk writes the access ACL with `setfacl -R -b -m` (the same replacement as `--set`, but `X` is resolved against each file's own bits, so an executable keeps execute); the default ACL keeps the lowercase spec (directories only, where `X` is `x`); setgid goes on directories only (`find <dir> -type d -exec chmod g+s {} +`, crossing mounts like the setfacl walk); the clear path's `chmod -R g-s` stays as the repair of damaged trees; the release notes carry a repair command. (F2) ANAS-written SMB stanzas carry `inherit acls = yes`, `create mask = 0664` and `directory mask = 0775`: every create; an edit that runs a grant adds each one the stanza does not set under any spelling (`create mode`, `directory mode` are Samba's synonyms), never changing an operator's value; an edit with no grant leaves the stanza byte-identical. `inherit acls` alone did not fix F2: the re-proof (`docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.3-access-reproof.md`, RF1) showed Samba's default masks (0744/0755) clamp the inherited ACL to read on every SMB-created file and directory, `inherit acls` or not; the masks fix it (operator ruling 2026-10-11, the values OpenMediaVault and TrueNAS SCALE write), proven in that document's addendum. (F3) The share door asks the recursive confirm after every refusal: the grant is prepared (gates, composition, plan) before the 409, a refused or empty grant asks nothing and the share is saved with the refusal in `grant`, and a job never runs a recursive apply its request did not confirm. (F5) `grant.granted` reports the level each principal ends with (the kept one under the never-lower rule), Everyone included; progress lines echo the argv that ran (`-R` included). Tests: `getfacl.test.ts`, `access.test.ts` (the recursive spec has no lowercase x; the setgid argv run against a real tree leaves regular files untouched), `ahr-access.test.ts`, `share-access-grant.test.ts` (kept levels, no 409 on a refused grant on create and edit, the unconfirmed job, the three SMB parameters on create, on an edit with a grant, an operator's value kept, no grant), `smb-conf.test.ts` (the three parameters on create; an edit with a grant adds only the missing ones, any spelling; `inherit acls = no` and `create mode = 0700` stand). Re-proven in `docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.3-access-reproof.md` (RF1 re-run in its addendum). (guest) Everyone is granted only when `valid users` is empty; otherwise the result carries `guestNote`.

**installer.1** `[done 2026-10-11, b8e93e5 + 9da6613]` **The apt re-apply hook and the preflight refusal are visible (GitHub #74).** (a) `packages/pve-integration/install.sh`'s `DPkg::Post-Invoke` line runs the integration installer with `|| true` and no logging: a failed re-apply after a pve-manager or libpve-http-server-perl upgrade leaves no trace anywhere (not even in apt's term log), and the first symptom is a user filing a bug. The hook logs start and result to journald under a stable tag (`anas-pve-integration`) and, on failure, sends a PVE notification through the existing notify path (journald + PVE notifications per the Proxmox-integration rule; no new alerting). (b) `packaging/install.sh`'s "release incomplete" refusal names the directory it checked (`SCRIPT_DIR`), lists every missing sibling, and prints the fix (re-extract the asset, run install.sh from inside it). (c) A bash test beside the four in `packaging/test/` for both. **Proof:** on the stunt node, `apt-get install --reinstall pve-manager` → journald shows the hook's re-apply, tpl line present; a hook forced to fail (installer renamed) → journald failure line + a PVE notification; an extraction with VERSION removed → the refusal names the directory and the file.
*Result:* the hook line now runs `packages/pve-integration/apt-reapply.sh` (`if [ -x …/apt-reapply.sh ]; then …/apt-reapply.sh || true; fi`), a wrapper shipped beside `install.sh` in the same tree (so `packaging/uninstall.sh`'s removal of the prefix removes it; `install.sh` refuses to write a hook when it is missing) because an apt.conf string cannot carry the quoting the notify call needs. It logs `EVENT=ReapplyStart`, the installer's own output, and `EVENT=ReapplyResult RESULT=ok|failed STATUS=<n>` under `anas-pve-integration`; a failure (including a missing installer, `STATUS=127`) sends one `PVE::Notify` warning through a new `anas-integration` template pair (the anas-md-event mechanism; the pair joins `NOTIFY_TEMPLATES` and the uninstall list) naming the installer to run as root and carrying the installer's ERROR lines; the wrapper always exits 0. The preflight collects every missing release path (`VERSION` is now one; a missing top-level directory is named once) into one refusal with `checked:` (`SCRIPT_DIR`), `missing:`, `expected:` (the layout) and `fix:`. Tests: `packaging/test/preflight-refusal.test.sh` (39), `packages/pve-integration/test/apt-reapply.test.sh` (35), `proxy-install.test.sh` §12–13 (hook line shape, run through `sh -c` against a failing and an absent wrapper; no wrapper → refused before anything is touched); `recycle-units.test.sh`'s two preflight source-greps re-pointed at `release_need` (same requirement, now also proven behaviourally). Live proof on the stunt node: `docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.3-installer.md` (reinstall → start + `STATUS=0` + tpl 1; installer renamed → `STATUS=127` + notification; installer failing → its ERROR line in journald and in the delivered mail; VERSION removed from an extracted dev tarball → refusal names the directory and the file, node unchanged).

**installer.2** `[done 2026-10-11, 43b50a5]` **The installed tree is root's.** Found on a real node (2026-10-11): the release tarball was built with plain `tar czf`, so every entry carried the build host user's uid/gid, and `packaging/install.sh`'s `cp -a` of the app tree preserved them onto the node — everything under the prefix, including the daemon and gateway code root runs as services and the apt-hook script root executes, owned by an ordinary account (uid 1000 on most Linux builds, and the first regular user on Debian gets 1000). True since the first release. **Fix (two layers, at the source):** `packaging/make-release.sh` builds the tarball with `--owner=0 --group=0 --numeric-owner` (the staged tree may stay owned by the build user; the archive records root; modes untouched), and `install.sh` runs `chown -R root:root "${PREFIX}"` after the copy — the tarball fix is the source, the chown is the belt that also repairs an existing install on the next upgrade. Everything else the installer places out of the release tree already goes through `install -m`, which creates fresh root-owned files, and the two `cp -a` calls that remain copy pre-existing system files being backed up, whose ownership is already correct. **Tests:** `packaging/test/root-ownership.test.sh` (12) — the tar line's flags, the flags proven to record uid/gid 0 in a real archive built by an unprivileged user, and `phase1_install` exercised against a fake root with a stubbed `chown` recording argv: called exactly once, `-R root:root <prefix>`, after the copy populated the prefix, unconditional so every run repairs.

**disks.2** `[done 2026-10-11, 7b87bd6]` **The disk health badge honours AHR member state (pve5, 2026-10-11).** `computeHealth` in `routes/disks.ts` fuses SMART pass/fail with ZFS vdev state only; an AHR member md has marked `faulty` reads `healthy` in the Disks tab and on the dashboard while the pool view says degraded (observed live: sdj faulty in chiaahr2-r2, badge healthy). **Build:** the AHR member state the read layer already derives per disk (`in_sync | faulty | spare | rebuilding | missing`, DESIGN "AHR" shape) feeds the same fusion with the ZFS mapping mirrored: `faulty`/`missing` → critical, `rebuilding` → warning, `in_sync`/`spare` → no change; SMART failed still wins; a disk in several bands takes the worst band. No new SMART-attribute interpretation (smartd already does that — no-undifferentiated-code; grooming question, not this story). **Proof:** fixture with a multi-band disk faulty in one band → critical; rebuilding → warning; dashboard counts follow; the existing ZFS cases unchanged. **Result:** `collectDisks` now carries each disk's per-band md member states (from the same `readAhrPools` payload the Hybrid RAID view renders) into `computeHealth`, whose AHR branch mirrors the ZFS mapping — `faulty`/`missing` critical, `rebuilding` warning, `in_sync`/`spare` unchanged, SMART-failed still loudest — with a multi-band disk taking its worst band; a cache disk (no array membership) stays SMART-only. The Disks badge and the dashboard's health counts both read the fused `healthStatus`, so an AHR member md marked faulty now shows critical everywhere; fixture tests cover the multi-band faulty/rebuilding cases and the dashboard count, and every existing ZFS case passes unchanged.

## 0.4.4 shipped — moved verbatim from `docs/EPICS.md` §3 on 2026-10-28 (the code-review release: cr.1–cr.71, nf.1–3b, waves 1–5; ACCEPTED 2026-10-14, RELEASED 2026-10-18, tag v0.4.4 at be10d775b)

### 0.4.4 — code-review release, ACCEPTED 2026-10-14 (operator: "a new point release") — **RELEASED 2026-10-18** (tag v0.4.4 at be10d775b; the whole ANAS roster pve1/5/7/10/14 on the published tarball the same hour, smoke green on every node; the tag moved twice before publishing, both times for a TEST that the Fedora dev box could not falsify — acl 2.3.2 skips a `setfacl -P` symlink operand where 2.4.0 refuses it, and the 20261012 runner image dropped `/usr/bin/node` — retro items)

Scope is the findings of `docs/CODE-REVIEW-2026-10-14.md`, which is the evidence each story cites; findings marked [R] are confirmed at the line before they are fixed. Fixes and hardening of shipped features, no new workflows (point-release ruling). Rulings that shape it: authorization follows PVE (§2 Cross-cutting), user delete IN with the uid-reuse guard (§2 Shares & identities), fstab disable via `noauto` going forward (§2 Mounts), and the 0.4.3 retrospective's (i)–(l). Bake-day matrix applies (ruling (e): destructive and boot paths are touched); the scoped review of the changed paths is gated on the operator per amended ruling (g). *(Operator 2026-10-16: the gate is DECIDED — no separate scoped review; the release IS the review's fixes, its scope was set when the work was authorized, and every story is hardening or a bug fix found in that review, each cross-model reviewed at merge.)* Stories are cut in waves: (1) C1, C2, C3, H2, H5 (remove the uncalled legacy permissions route), H7, W4; (2) authorization + CSRF (H1, one check in the proxy path), H3, H4, H6, uid-reuse guard; (3) H8 + M3 (the `safety/` helper), M1 (executor timeouts, per-verb opt-outs), M5; then M2, M4, M6, M7, §4, W1–W3 as the release allows. Lanes per the local-delegation hints; every merge with a request-path change gets a live smoke.

**Wave 1 stories (cut 2026-10-14).** Each cites the review finding; "confirm at the line before fixing" applies to every [R] item.

**cr.1** `[done 2026-10-14, 73ca9ac]` **Replication remotes cannot inject ssh options (review C1).** `buildSshArgv` (`services/replication-transport.ts`) puts `--` before the destination in both branches (`ssh-keyscan` already does, `services/replication-remotes.ts:266`). Shared schemas `RemoteUser` (a POSIX-style login name: letters, digits, `_ . -`, never a leading `-`, ≤ 32) and `RemoteHost` (DNS name, IPv4 or bare IPv6, never a leading `-`, no whitespace, `[`, `]`, `,` or `@`) are used by `UpsertRemoteRequest` and by a new `TestRemoteRequest` that `POST /replication/remotes/test` parses instead of its hand-rolled checks; the registry READ schema stays lenient (a stored value is made harmless by `--`, and a stricter read would break existing installs). Every element of every remote command (`sshExec`, the recv argv in `routes/replication.ts`) is a constant or a value validated as a ZFS name — the remote shell re-parses them. Tests: argv pins with `--`; the hostile user/host from the review refused at both doors; a stored hostile user reaching `buildSshArgv` lands after `--`.

**cr.2** `[done 2026-10-14]` **fstab fields are escaped and options are tokens (review C2; ruling (i)).** Writing a line octal-escapes space, tab, newline, `\` and `#` in the spec and mountpoint (`\040`, `\011`, `\012`, `\134`, `\043`, as `getmntent` reads them); reading unescapes, so a hand-written `\040` line parses to the real path and an untouched line still round-trips byte-identically. `extraOptions` becomes a list of option tokens validated in `shared/` (one token: a name of letters, digits, `_ . -`, optionally `=` and a value with no whitespace, `,`, `#`, `"` or `\`); credential-carrying tokens (`credentials`, `password`, `pass`, `username`, `user`, `domain`) are refused because secrets live in the creds file (§2 Secrets). The first file of the per-format layer from ruling (i) lives in `packages/shared/src/formats/`. Tests from a real fstab capture plus the review's hostile mountpoint (`/usr nfs4 rw 0 0 #`) and a newline in `extraOptions`.

**cr.3** `[done 2026-10-14, 022fab2]` **System accounts are not mutable through the identity API (review C3).** Every identity mutation (user enable/disable, SMB password, user delete, group create/members/delete, and each group named in `CreateShareUserRequest.groups`) is refused unless the target's uid/gid is in the share band [1000, 60000) — the same band `isShareRelevant` draws, without its uid-0 exception. Refusal = 409 with no confirm code (Principle 14 "blocked"), checked at the route and again in the job. `groups` becomes an array of `IdentityName`; `fullName` becomes `SingleLine` without `:`. Root stays LISTED (ownership pickers need it); only mutations are refused.

**cr.4** `[done 2026-10-14, bf65aec]` **The legacy permissions route is removed (review H5).** `PUT …/permissions` (`routes/datasets.ts` `setPermissions`, job `fs.setPermissions`) has no caller in the UI or the integration specs; the `…/access` editor replaced it. Remove the route branch, `SetPermissionsRequest` (if nothing else uses it) and its DESIGN row; the ownership-guard tests that use it (`datasets.test.ts` ~674, ~690) are re-pointed at `…/access`, not deleted.

**cr.5** `[done 2026-10-14, bf65aec]` **Restore probes cannot be steered by a planted symlink (review H7).** The write-test probe name in `services/backup-restore-files.ts` (`writeTestDirectory`) is random (crypto), not `-${pid}`; `writePartialMarker` writes a random temp name with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` and `rename`s it over `.anas-restore-partial`, so a symlink or hard link at the marker name is replaced, never followed.

**cr.7** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Uninstall never rolls PVE's HTTP server module back (review H2).** Removal of the proxy hook is always marker excision plus the `perl -c` gate; the whole-file `AnyEvent.pm.anas-orig` backup is no longer taken, and one left by an older install is removed (unused) by both install and uninstall. Proven in `proxy-install.test.sh`: install on v1, upgrade to v2, re-splice, uninstall → byte-identical to v2. Follow-up noted: `packaging/install.sh` writes an `index.html.tpl.anas-orig` nothing reads.

**cr.8** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Shell and perl suites run in `npm test`, CI and the release (ruling (k), review W3).** `test:unit` now ends with `test:unit:shell` (every `packaging/test/*.test.sh` and `packages/pve-integration/test/*.test.sh`, found by glob, stdin closed) and `test:unit:perl` (`prove` over `AnasProxy.t`); CI installs `libwww-perl`/`liburi-perl` and reads with `contents: read`; `release.yml` runs build, `test:unit`, the second-TZ daemon run and lint before `make-release.sh`, so a tag cannot publish a red commit.

**cr.9** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Three small input fixes (review M7, §4).** A malformed `PVEAuthCookie` percent-escape answers 401, not 500; a backup task's `schedule` is `SingleLine` (no newline into `OnCalendar=`); `packaging/anas-cache-event.sh` refuses a GPT label whose pool part is not a `PoolName` (checked character by character) or whose suffix is not `-cache<1–2 digits>`, before it posts with root identity (`packaging/test/cache-event.test.sh`, 22 checks).

**cr.6 automount defects (node pass 2026-10-14, docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1.md R1) — ANSWERED by the §2 Mounts automount ruling (2026-10-14) and cr.19, PROVEN docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R1 (this header still read "needs an operator design call before 0.4.4 tags" until the 2026-10-17 zero-work sweep — nothing was open):** (D1) systemd-fstab-generator applies `noauto` to the `.mount` unit only and still wires the `.automount` into `remote-fs.target.wants`, so a disabled `x-systemd.automount` line is armed at every boot and mounts on first access; (D2) disabling an ARMED automount leaves it armed (the daemon only daemon-reloads); (D3) removing an armed automount line leaves an orphaned autofs placeholder (`not-found active waiting`, "Host is down" on access) until `systemctl stop <unit>.automount`. Plain (non-automount) lines behave as the ruling says, including across a reboot.

**cr.6 node proof (was "owed before 0.4.4 tags"; status at the 2026-10-17 sweep: (a) PROVEN wave-1b R1 — disable stops the armed unit, nothing in `remote-fs.target.wants`, access and a reboot do not mount; (b) PROVEN wave-1 R1 — `mount -a` rc=0 mounted nothing, `findmnt --fstab` lists the line with `noauto,x-anas.disabled`; (c) NOT proven live → one row in the next node pass, low (PVE reads no fstab option); the UI follow-ups → cr.69):** on a PVE 9 node, (a) after `systemctl daemon-reload` a `noauto,x-anas.disabled` line with `x-systemd.automount` does not arm its automount at boot or on reload, and whether disabling an ALREADY-armed automount leaves the live placeholder (the daemon only daemon-reloads); (b) `findmnt --fstab` / `mount -a` skip it; (c) PVE tooling ignores the `x-anas.disabled` option. UI follow-ups: `67-mounts.js` still describes the `#ANAS` marker in a pill title and comments, and its fstab-line preview will now show `x-anas.disabled`. Known corner: a legacy `#ANAS` line carrying inline plaintext credentials loses them on a disable-again conversion (the edit path migrates them first).

**cr.11** `[built 2026-10-14; browser probe proven 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1.md R6]` **Panels encode text by default (review M5, first bullet).** `ANAS.alertMsg` and `ANAS.toast` encode their body; `ANAS.alertHtml`/`ANAS.toastHtml` take deliberate markup (caller encodes every value); the job-failure and rejected alerts in `10-api.js` encode the daemon's text; the review's unencoded grid columns, combo templates and the pool-export intro encode; six callers that pre-encoded lost their inner `enc()` so nothing double-encodes. `alert-encoding.harness.mjs` proves the contract against the real `00-core.js`. Owed: a browser probe that a failing job whose stderr carries `<b>` shows the tags as text, and a cloud verdict toast reads normally. Proven: wave1 R6 — the Playwright spec drives both alert branches (`jobRejected` 400 and `pollJob` failed) with `<b>x</b>` shown literally, mutation-checked (identity `ANAS.enc` fails the spec); the LUN/SMART grids were confirmed only in the served bundle, and the cloud verdict toast is not in that row.

**cr.10** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Governing documents match the code (review W1, W2).** Nuxt → the anas gateway in PRINCIPLES §3/§6 and CLAUDE.md; §8's install commands are the tarball and `./install.sh`; DESIGN: shipped scope un-listed from "out of scope", config row = environment (`/etc/default/anas`), retired markers on the `anas setup` and YAML plans, Users & Groups rows at `/v1/identity/…`, a route index for 59 untabled registrations; comments in `20-notinstalled.js` and `anas.service` tell the truth; the identity audit carries per-finding status; §4's shipped struck entries and stray row removed; CONTRIBUTING and the stunt-node README name what exists. **Ruled 2026-10-14:** PRINCIPLES §8's "no external binaries" meant ANAS ships none of its own and builds no undifferentiated code; it relies on the standard Linux/PVE tooling `install.sh` installs, exceptions are scrutinized (Node.js from NodeSource), and the system keeps working if ANAS is removed — §8 reworded. Noted: DESIGN lists `GET /v1/iscsi/targets/:iqn/luns/:lun`, which no route registers.

**cr.13** `[done 2026-10-14]` **smb.conf, /etc/exports and rclone.conf values are safe for their file (review H3, ruling (i)).** `shared/formats/smb-conf.ts` (no control characters, no trailing `\`; list entries carry no separator), `formats/exports.ts` (client specs, option tokens, export paths without `"`, `\`, `#`), `formats/rclone-conf.ts` (option keys `[A-Za-z0-9_]+`, values without control characters; the writer refuses a newline as a backstop; a pretty-printed OAuth token paste is collapsed to one line). Write doors only; reading stays lenient. A failed `exportfs -ra` restores the previous `/etc/exports` under the config lock (a concurrent hand-edit is left alone) and reloads it. Noted: cloud and replication task `schedule` are still bare strings (gated by `systemd-analyze` at save; align to `SingleLine` later); the PBS repo secret schema has no control-character check.

**cr.14** `[done 2026-10-14; pmxcfs node check proven 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1.md R4]` **One durable atomic-write helper (review M4).** `writeFileAtomic` in `services/config-writer.ts`: an exclusive, unpredictable temp in the target's directory, fsync, the target's mode and owner copied (not under `/etc/pve`, where pmxcfs fixes them), rename, best-effort directory fsync; `editConfig` and six duplicate writers use it (rclone.conf, both backup-repo files, mount credentials, the replication registry and known_hosts, the AHR intent file — the last one the review missed); the three secret writers still force 0600. The installers replace `cat tmp > live` with temp + `mv -f` keeping mode and owner, and clean their temp files on exit. Owed on a node: `O_EXCL` create, fsync and same-directory rename on pmxcfs. Proven: wave1 R4 — raw `wx` second open EEXISTs, file fsync/datasync and directory fsync all succeed, rename is atomic and immediately visible, and the deployed helper's create/replace land under both `/etc/pve` directories (priv/ refuses the chmod — the helper's no-chmod branch is what keeps it working). Next story candidate from its investigation: rclone.conf writes have no change check, and rclone itself rewrites the file on OAuth refresh, so an ANAS write can revert a fresh token — hash on read, re-hash before rename, 409 on mismatch.

**cr.12** `[done 2026-10-14; node `--` check in the wave-1 node pass]` **ZFS names are validated, operands follow `--`, force is confirm-gated (review H4).** `ImportPoolRequest.name` is a `PoolName`, `.guid` decimal digits, every disk field a `DiskId`; create/add/attach refuse a disk the inventory does not know and fail closed when it cannot be read; every `zpool`/`zfs` argv in pools.ts and datasets.ts carrying a user operand has `--` before it except `zpool set`, which rejects the terminator (proven on OpenZFS 2.4.4, comment at the call site); `force` on import and create answers 409 + confirm code; `GET /pools/:name/datasets/*` validates the pool name first. Residual: `parsers/zfs-list.ts` list helpers and `services/backup-restore.ts` `zfs destroy` still pass operands without `--` (inputs now validated upstream). Built in two sessions (the first capped at 250 turns in its test tail).

**cr.17** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Loose ends from the wave-1 reviews.** `--` in `parsers/zfs-list.ts`'s three list helpers and backup-restore's `zfs destroy`; cloud and replication `schedule` are `SingleLine` like backup's (both schemas also read stored units: a bad replication unit reads `invalid`, a bad cloud unit is skipped with a warning); group member add/remove are `IdentityName[]`; the shared `ErrorCode` enum carries all 12 codes the daemon and gateway emitted outside it (`CONFLICT`, `UNAVAILABLE`, `IN_USE`, `PVE_MANAGED`, `IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, `NOT_CANCELLABLE`, `UPSTREAM_ERROR`, and five gateway codes). Still without `--` (internal paths, inputs validated upstream): `services/iscsi-mutate.ts` ~1487, `services/zfs-snapshot.ts` ~141, `services/snapshot-schedules.ts` ~148/335/357/509, `services/backup-snapshots.ts` ~223, `safety/stable-id.ts` ~103.

**cr.16** `[done 2026-10-14]` **An ANAS write to rclone.conf never reverts rclone's own change.** `writeRemote`/`removeRemote` pass the sha256 of the file bytes they read; `writeConfigFileAtomic` re-hashes the file just before writing and throws `ConfigConflictError` on a mismatch, and the cloud job fails with "rclone changed its config file while ANAS was editing it — reload and retry" (the remote doors are 202 jobs, so the conflict is a failed job, not a 409). The repair write after a failed post-write check stays unconditional; it can still eat a token refresh that lands in that window (noted, by design).

**cr.15** `[done 2026-10-14; legacy-pool node check proven 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R3]` **AHR LVM teardown acts only on a VG whose every PV is the pool's own (review H6).** `requireOwnedVgPvs` (services/ahr-identity.ts) re-reads `pvs`/`vgs` before any LVM teardown in `destroyAhrPool` — which is also create's rollback — and refuses, before any md stop, unless every PV is an owned md array (ident.3) or the pool's cache slice (or `[unknown]`, a missing device that was the pool's); unreadable `pvs`/`vgs` refuse. The request-time `vgs` check answers 503 when `vgs` fails; `ahrNameOccupancy` adds VG names and refuses on unreadable sources. `isOwnedArray`'s unpinned fallback now adopts only when the PV list gives no named membership answer (capture-backed tests keep pinned, legacy-adopted, unreadable-`pvs` and bands-down pools). No VG UUID is stored (Principle 11). Owed on a node: a real pre-pin adopted pool still destroys end to end. Proven: wave1b R3 — pins removed, anasd restarted, the pool still listed as adopted, Destroy completes end to end (strace: no teardown command reaches anything else). Noted: destroy's `pvremove` pass would also take a foreign PV hand-added to the VG — the gate now refuses that VG first; narrowing the pass to owned PVs is a follow-up.

**cr.18** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Mount and AHR guards (review M7).** The "fstab entry already exists" refusal applies to every mount create (a non-persistent CIFS mount used to overwrite the existing entry's credentials file), and credentials are written only after the fstab edit (persistent) or right before the mount; a mountpoint equal to a system path (`/`, `/bin`, `/boot`, `/dev`, `/etc`, `/home`, `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/opt`, `/proc`, `/root`, `/run`, `/sbin`, `/srv`, `/sys`, `/tmp`, `/usr`, `/var`) or inside `/usr`, `/etc`, `/boot`, `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/run`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/lib*` is refused (`SYSTEM_MOUNT_TARGETS` in services/mounts.ts); an AHR pool name is refused at the create schema when its longest derived identifier would not fit (md name 32 bytes, GPT label 36 characters — 29 characters for up to 9 disks, 28 up to 99), before any wipe. Open: AHR expand to ≥10 disks and cache attach with ≥10 slices are not length-guarded (a 29-character pool expanding to a two-digit band).

**cr.20** `[done 2026-10-14]` **Three identity items from the 2026-10-06 audit (review M6).** #23: a new SMB share may not be named `global`, `homes` or `printers` in any case (`NewShareName`; Samba section names are matched case-insensitively, and the parser already folds them), while existing stanzas stay listed and manageable. The `anas-` prefix is refused on a manual ZFS snapshot create/rename and an AHR snapshot create (`NewSnapshotName`, `NewAhrSnapshotName`; `SCHEDULED_SNAPSHOT_PREFIX` now lives in shared). #46: a scheduled snapshot that collides in the same second retries once under `-2` (the retention grammar accepts `-<digits>` at the same instant, so it stays owned and pruned). Retention today prunes a hand-made `anas-*` snapshot only when it matches the exact schedule grammar and (ZFS) exactly one enabled schedule covers the dataset, or (AHR) any enabled schedule covers the pool. Noted: `hasShare(text, 'global')` is false although `[global]` is present, so the create route's existence check can never fire for it; the schema gate closes the only door.

**cr.19 follow-ups (Opus rebuild report):** the panel's Disable/Enable (`67-mounts.js` `mountEnableDisable`) ignores `job.result.warnings`, so a failed `.automount` stop on Disable reaches only the job record (Remove shows them); the legacy `comment=systemd.automount` spelling is not parked (from memory: the generator still honours it); node checks owed — `systemctl stop` of an automount unit the reload removed exits 5 on PVE 9 (no warning), no journal complaint about `x-anas.disabled-automount`. Both node checks proven 2026-10-15 (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R1: stop-exit=5, the product took the same path with no warning; `journalctl -b 0 | grep -ci 'x-anas'` → 0); the panel-warnings and legacy-`comment=` follow-ups stay open → cut 2026-10-17 as cr.69 (b) and cr.70 (a); the stunt node confirmed the same day that the PVE 9 generator binary still carries the `comment=systemd.automount` spelling.

**cr.21** `[done 2026-10-14]` **`--` before every derived zfs/zpool operand (ruling (i)).** Finishes cr.12 and cr.17: replication send/hold/release/holds (the local `send` and the remote `recv` both carry `--`), snapshot/schedule/scrub argv, access, iSCSI, backup zvol/restore/snapshots, share self-service, `zpool iostat`; `zpool set` alone stays without it (it rejects `--`). `services/__tests__/cr21-argv-separator.test.ts` checks 19 pure argv builders; inline argv is guarded by each suite's exact pins. Built in two sessions (the first capped in its test tail); all 107 failures at the handover were stale pins.

**cr.22** `[done 2026-10-14; LVM missing-PV node checks proven 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R2 (and R3 for the legacy-pool half)]` **cr.15's LVM gate closes the review's gaps (Opus).** A missing (`[unknown]`) PV counts as the pool's only when the same VG also shows a NAMED PV proven the pool's (an owned band array or its labelled cache slice) — an all-`[unknown]` VG is refused, pinned or not (a pin is writable by `mdadm --detail --scan` for any array); `pvs` returning fewer rows than the VG's `pv_count` (or none) refuses; cache detach and the unattended cache recovery ask a new `requirePoolVg` gate (same anchor, hand-added PVs left alone as before); destroy's `pvremove` names only proven PVs, runs plain `vgreduce --removemissing` (never `--force`) before `vgremove` when a missing row remains, and stops if `vgs` fails after the LVs are gone. Behaviour change to check: the one-band all-`[unknown]` "bands down" cache-event shape is now refused (real LVM cannot list a VG with no readable PV). Owed on a node: `vgremove`/`lvremove` with a missing PV, plain `--removemissing` with no LVs, a real legacy adopted pool destroying end to end. Proven: wave1b R2(a) — with a live-pulled PV, `lvconvert --uncache`, `lvremove`, plain `vgreduce --removemissing` (with and without LVs left) and `vgremove` all succeed and `pvremove` names only proven PVs, never `[unknown]`; R2(b) — a foreign VG named like a pool is refused with zero LVM/md argv reaching it in both shapes; R3 — the legacy adopted pool. The row's defects R2-D1/D2 became cr.31/cr.31b.

**Release-note line owed for cr.31 D1 (shipped 0.4.0–0.4.3):** "Destroying an AHR pool with two or more bands, or one grown by an expansion, failed partway with \"Failed to find logical volume\" after the pool had been unmounted, and a second Destroy was needed to finish. Destroy now removes these pools completely in one job." **cr.31b** `[done 2026-10-15]`: an unreadable `lvs` stops destroy before any act (and a failed create's rollback now refuses instead of half-tearing-down); destroying the last AHR-pinned pool removes ANAS's own `PROGRAM /usr/local/bin/anas-md-event` line (an operator's PROGRAM line is never touched; create re-adds it). Still open: the ZFS legacy-dataset reference exclusion has a unit test only.

**Wave-1 review notes (grooming, not stories yet):** pools.r1's boot-scan half: read `/etc/systemd/system/zfs-import.target.wants/`, match each instance by `zfsImportUnitName(<pool>)` against imported and importable pools, journald-warn the rest, at the end of the boot-scan chain in `index.ts` (needs a PVE 9 capture first); export has no name-list root-pool backstop beside the boot-facts guard (`pools.ts` ~1932 vs destroy's `isRootPool`), so a DATA pool named `rpool` on a node with readable boot facts is exportable — a ruling, not a bug; a leniently-read stored replication task can carry shell metacharacters into a remote `zfs list` (root-only write, runs on the target; `routes/replication-tasks.ts:73`); IPv6 zone ids (`fe80::1%eth0`) are refused by `RemoteHost`; existing 409s use `CONFLICT`, which the shared `ErrorCode` enum lacks; `UpdateGroupMembersRequest.add/remove` are plain strings.

**cr.6** `[plain lines PROVEN on the node 2026-10-14; automount lines FAILED → ruled (§2 Mounts automount ruling) and fixed by cr.19, PROVEN 2026-10-15 wave-1b R1; this status read "design call open" until 2026-10-17]` **Disable writes `noauto` + `x-anas.disabled` (§2 Mounts ruling, 2026-10-14).** `disableMount`/`enableMount` in `parsers/fstab.ts` move to the option form; the reader accepts both forms (`#ANAS` prefix and the `x-anas.disabled` token) as disabled; an `#ANAS` line is converted only when ANAS writes that line; `x-anas.disabled` is never shown in the passthrough/extra options the UI edits and is refused in `extraOptions`. Every consumer that skips disabled mounts (inventory, backup source guard, boot ordering, liveness) is checked against a `noauto,x-anas.disabled` line — a `noauto` line still gets a systemd mount unit, unlike a commented one. Tests from the mounts fixtures: disable→enable round trip byte-identical with and without a pre-existing `noauto`; an `#ANAS` line read, left untouched by an unrelated edit, converted on re-disable. Builds on cr.2 (same files).

**cr.19** `[rebuilt by Opus 2026-10-14 (replacing the local build, whose session saw injected-looking tool text); node re-run of R1 PROVEN 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R1]` **Disable and Remove neutralise automount (§2 Mounts automount ruling; node pass R1-D1..D3).** `disableMount` swaps `x-systemd.automount` in place to `x-anas.disabled-automount` (alongside the existing `noauto` / `x-anas.disabled` tokens); `enableMount` swaps it back; the reader treats `x-anas.disabled-automount` as the operator's automount=true while disabled and never shows it in the passthrough; `extraOptions` refuses it. The disable and remove routes run `systemctl stop <unit>.automount` (unit name via `systemd-escape --path --suffix=automount`, or the file's existing helper) for an automount line BEFORE the daemon-reload, and a failure to stop is reported, not swallowed. Tests from the mounts fixtures; then the node pass re-runs R1 (disable an armed automount; reboot; neither arms; enable byte-identical; remove leaves no placeholder). Builds after cr.18 (same route file).

**cr.23** `[done 2026-10-15; node check proven 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R4]` **The system-pool refusal decides on facts, never names (operator ruling 2026-10-14).** A pool (ZFS or AHR) is a system pool when the LIVE mount table shows any of its filesystems mounted at a system path — the `SYSTEM_MOUNT_TARGETS` list from cr.18, as the single source — or when the ZFS boot facts (`bootfs`, root dataset) name it. On the PVE default ext4/LVM root no pool is flagged for `/`, but a dataset or AHR volume mounted at `/var`, `/home`, `/boot`… is; on a ZFS root `/` and `/boot` resolve to their pools by fact. Destroy, export and remount (ZFS) and destroy/remount (AHR) all ask the one rule. The well-known-name list (`rpool`/`bpool`, `safety/root-pool.ts`) is removed. When neither the mount table nor the boot facts can be read, the verb is refused (fail closed); a missing fact may only tighten the gate.

**cr.24** `[done 2026-10-15]` **Pool export and destroy name what still references the pool (operator 2026-10-14).** ZFS and the kernel refuse a BUSY pool (open files), and a served LUN is a hard 409; what nobody checks is REFERENCES: an SMB share, an NFS export, a backup task source, a replication task (source or target), a cloud-sync task path, a snapshot/scrub schedule, or a mount whose path lives on the pool. Export and destroy (ZFS) and destroy (AHR) list each one in the confirm warnings — named, with its kind — so the operator decides with the facts; they do not refuse (state the failure mode once). The same list is re-read in the job and a reference that appeared after the confirm is reported in the result (ruling (j)). Reuse whatever cross-check already exists (the backup source guard, the mount unmount's share/backup-task check, the identity delete's share references) instead of writing a third; one helper answers "what references this path". The stale "no cheap signal for active shares yet" comment in the export route goes.

**Wave 2 stories (cut 2026-10-15 from docs/WAVE2-DESIGN.md §9 — the design is the spec; its §10 open questions are the operator's).** Order: nothing may require what an earlier merge does not yet send — cr.25a deployed before cr.27; cr.27 is one merge across Perl, gateway and UI; cr.28 after cr.27; cr.26, cr.29, cr.30 independent of the auth chain.
- **cr.25a** `[done 2026-10-15; browser-trace check proven 2026-10-15: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave3.md row N3/cr.25a]` The UI and the gateway send PVE's CSRF token (no enforcement). Opus. Proven: the Playwright trace through the real PVE UI shows every mutation sent through `/anas` carried PVE's `CSRFPreventionToken`.
- **cr.25b** `[done 2026-10-15; node suite run owed]` The integration specs send the token through one helper. After cr.25a. Local lane.
- **cr.26** `[done 2026-10-15]` The daemon accepts JSON bodies only (415 otherwise). Local lane.
- **cr.27** `[done 2026-10-15; node smoke PROVEN 2026-10-15 — docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave2.md rows 1–15, 17 and review rows 18–21, `prove` on the node against the real PVE modules (57 new checks in AnasProxy-node.t)]` *(AnasProxy.pm asks PVE's `auth_handler` and `$rpcenv->check` inside pveproxy and signs an `X-Anas-Auth` HMAC with the key the gateway writes to `/run/anas/proxy.key` at startup; the gateway refuses anything unsigned or older than 30 s, answers 403 naming Sys.Audit or Sys.Modify, forwards a body-less non-JSON Content-Type so the daemon's 415 holds through `/anas` too, and the installer reloads pveproxy when the module changed — WAVE2-DESIGN §8 rows 1-17 are the node pass's.)* (cr.26 note: a body-less POST that carries `Content-Type: text/plain` loses the header in the gateway's forward, so the daemon's 415 rule is stricter at the socket than through `/anas` — make it uniform here.) PVE decides who and what inside pveproxy; the gateway enforces Sys.Audit/Sys.Modify; refuses outside pveproxy (the hook also runs in pvedaemon on :85); disabled users and realms honoured. **Q2 ruled 2026-10-15: option A** — an HMAC-signed hand-off header over the existing loopback TCP (key readable by root and www-data only). Opus + node smoke. **Cross-model security review 2026-10-15 (Qwen, read-only, 47 live attacks against the real gateway with a stub daemon, known-answer vectors recomputed independently): 0 defects.** Folded: the one silent answer (`_respond_502`) now logs method, path, peer and the transport reason; the forward docstring says the PEER's pveproxy verifies. Node pass adds to §8: a signed target with `%2F` and a query through real pveproxy (row 14 extended), www-data actually reads `/run/anas/proxy.key`, the real `:85` refusal, the key-rotation 401 window. Possibles recorded, not changed: a ticket+token request is served on the ticket (PVE's own precedence, deliberate); an expired ticket renders the "not installed" panel (401 is row 15's answer). *(The coordinator's follow-up commit 455ff821 and the cr.47b schedule-door fold 1cfe4a14 were cross-model reviewed by the new ai1 lane 2026-10-16: clean — no silent answer path remains in AnasProxy.pm; the panel's supplementary picker equals the daemon's band exactly and `users` (gid 100) is rightly not offered as a supplementary group; the schedule door's three cases constructed.)*
- **cr.28** `[done 2026-10-15; node smoke PROVEN 2026-10-15 (wave-2 record row 2; the udev cache-event job itself not run — needs an AHR pool the stale mdadm.conf blocks)]` The job queue refuses work without Sys.Modify. After cr.27. **RULED YES 2026-10-17 (proposed 2026-10-15; the coordinator's premature 'ruling' corrected 2026-10-16):** a job that only reads (cr.48's `read.*` jobs) needs Sys.Audit, not Sys.Modify. The daemon backstop implements it; the gateway still asks Sys.Modify for every POST, so an Audit-only user cannot run these through /anas until the operator rules on a per-route gateway exception. `submit` refuses with the gateway's 403 sentence, logged, a request whose `x-anas-privileges` (now a required identity header, read into an async-local store by the daemon's hooks rather than taken from the submitter a route builds) lacks Sys.Modify, or Sys.Audit for a `read.*` job or its cancel, so the task runners and the udev cache-event script name both privileges and only work no request started (boot work, job bodies) goes unchecked (`jobs/authority.ts`).
