Discrepancy recorded 2026-10-17 (review W2): the daemon's comments still call the read-job privilege an "operator ruling 2026-10-15" (`jobs/read-jobs.ts:5`, `jobs/authority.ts:16`) while this line says NOT yet ruled — the operator has not ruled, and the branch is unreachable today either way (the gateway asks `Sys.Modify` for every non-GET method, `packages/gateway/src/auth/policy.ts` `requiredPrivilege`), so an Audit-only user cannot start a read job through /anas; the question stands for the operator: rule it, then either a per-route gateway exception or delete the dead branch. → Ruled YES 2026-10-17: the gateway's per-route exception is cr.67, after which the two comments are true (their date corrected to the ruling's).
- **cr.29** `[done 2026-10-15]` Create user/group always passes `-u`/`-g`: an id the operator gives is refused only when an account holds it (the band is the schema's 400) and never checked for files, a picked one is the next above the highest in-band account that owns nothing on any ZFS filesystem (`zfs userspace|groupspace -Hpn -o name`) or under a non-ZFS share root (nf.1-bounded `find -uid|-gid -print -quit`, one 10 s budget) and is promised to its create so two creates never share it, an unreadable source refuses 409 `id-check-unreadable`, and the delete confirm says once that the number stays on the files. A deleted user's or group's number is not handed out again by accident. **Ruled 2026-10-15 (W2 Q1):** no tombstone accounts (they fight the standard tooling). Create passes `useradd`/`groupadd` an explicit `-u`/`-g`: when the operator gives none, ANAS picks the next free id in the share band and checks it owns nothing under ANAS-managed paths (`zfs userspace`/`groupspace` per dataset; a bounded `find -uid/-gid` on non-ZFS share roots), moving on to the next if it does; the operator may also SET the uid/gid explicitly on Add (the out for re-creating an erroneously deleted account), refused only when an existing account already has it (and outside the share band). The delete confirm says once that the number stays on the files and that ids seen only in exported pools or backups cannot be checked. Opus. Follow-up owed (cr.29b): a share or export root that no longer exists blocks every automatic pick (find exits 1 → unreadable) — an absent root owns nothing and should count as such; DESIGN.md's identity POST rows still omit `uid`/`gid` and its DELETE rows still promise an ACL-reference scan cr.29 does not do. **cr.29b `[done 2026-10-15]`:** a share or export root whose find fails is stat'ed with the share list's own probe and owns nothing when it is ENOENT/ENOTDIR (any other failure still refuses), DESIGN.md's identity rows now carry `uid`/`gid`/`primaryGroup` and say ACLs are not read, and the Add dialog's primary-group list offers only `users` (the default, shown and not sent) and the band groups.
- **cr.29c** `[done 2026-10-15]` **Explicit ids, designed (operator 2026-10-15).** (1) No ownership confirm on an explicit id (too expensive for what it buys). (2) An explicit uid also checks the user's private group: with PVE's `USERGROUPS_ENAB`, `useradd -u N` asks for gid N and silently takes another when a group holds it — so when gid N is taken the create answers a 409 confirm naming the group that holds it (resend creates with the next free gid, said in the warning); the same path opens an explicit PRIMARY group on Add (an existing group instead of a private one, `useradd -g <group> -N`) if the dialog has none today. (3) The uid/gid fields (and the primary-group choice) live in a collapsed "Advanced" section of the Add dialogs. (4) Groups get the same treatment: an explicit gid held by an existing group is refused, a gid equal to an existing user's uid (a would-be private group) is a confirm. (5) The journald audit line of a create carries the uid/gid used and whether it was explicit. (6) Fencepost: the share band is [1000, 60000] inclusive, matching login.defs `UID_MAX`/`GID_MAX` (the reader, the schema and the panel limits move together). Opus. Shipped with one correction: ANAS keeps `useradd -N` (identity.1c), so no private group is ever made and `USERGROUPS_ENAB` never applies — (2) and (4) are one bound 409 confirm whenever uid N and gid N would belong to two unrelated accounts (re-checked in the job, the pick steps around the other kind's numbers), beside an optional `primaryGroup` (`useradd -g <gid> -N`, band or the default gid 100), the collapsed Advanced sections, `explicit: true|false` on the create audit line and the band at [1000, 60000]. Follow-up owed (cr.29d): with `USERGROUPS_ENAB yes` a plain `userdel` silently removes a group named like the user when it is that user's primary group with no members — now reachable from an ANAS user created with `primaryGroup` = its own name, and already true of pre-existing private-group accounts; the user delete must not remove a group behind the group-delete gates (verify userdel's options against the man page, or refuse/warn); and the panel's primary-group list offers groups the daemon refuses (root) while omitting `users`. **cr.29d `[done 2026-10-15]`:** userdel has no way to keep the group (no `-K`: its getopt string is `fhrR:P:Z` in shadow 4.17.4 and 4.19.0), so the user delete mirrors remove_usergroup's rule (with login.defs USERGROUPS_ENAB), names the group in its confirm, binds it as the confirm's scope with its gid key, and refuses as the group delete would (`referenced-by-share`, `SYSTEM_ACCOUNT`); the panel half shipped in cr.29b. *(2026-10-15: the panel's supplementary-group picker offers the share band only — root and other system groups, which the daemon refuses with SYSTEM_ACCOUNT, are no longer listed; same rule as the primary-group list.)* **cr.29e `[done 2026-10-15]`** two verified defects in the cr.29d mirror are fixed: its "another account's primary group" check reads the LOCAL passwd file (`getent -s files passwd`), the only source userdel consults, and the user delete now runs the going group through the group delete's primary-group gate over ALL of NSS (minus the user being deleted) — so a directory account pinned to that gid makes the delete refuse (`primary-group-in-use`) where the real userdel would have removed the group and orphaned it — and a login.defs line with a trailing comment (`USERGROUPS_ENAB yes # note`) no longer reads as not-yes, so its gates are no longer skipped; Debian's shadow 4.17.4 behaviour is owed a node check (the proofs ran on 4.19.0). *(Cross-model review of cr.29e, DeepSeek 2026-10-16, against real `userdel -P` + a node probe on the stunt node: 1 defect fixed by the coordinator — the going-group gate excepted the deleted user by NAME, so a directory account under the same name (another uid) was excepted too and its primary group would have been orphaned; the except is now name AND uid, pinned. Node fact that overturns cr.29e's premise: Debian's shadow 4.17.4 (PVE; no libeconf) does NOT strip inline comments — `yes # note` and `yes#x` keep the group, `"yes" # note` removes it, an absent key keeps it (docs/probes/wave2-node/out/ldefs-debian-shadow.txt); the earlier proofs ran on Fedora's libeconf parser. The mirror now follows Debian's rule (unquoted value to end of line; quoted value to its closing quote); the absent-key over-estimate stays documented (PVE ships the key). Two-door flips both caught (SCOPE_CHANGED, STATE_CHANGED); `getent -s files` unreadable → 503, an nsswitch without a files source reads as empty with the all-NSS gate behind it.)*
- **cr.30** `[done 2026-10-15]` Delete refuses every smb.conf name reference (all user-list parameters, `@`/`+`/`&` forms). Local lane. ACL references are not read: a user/group delete neither reports nor refuses on an ACL entry (cr.29 checks file ownership for id reuse, not ACLs). The user-list scan reads the way Samba does: a double-quoted entry keeps its spaces (`valid users = "@DOMAIN\Domain Users", "vuser"`), `[global]` user-list values count for every share as the section `global`, a bare `%S` is the share's own name (any other `%` token never matches), and a group sigil is stripped whole (`+&`/`&+` included).

**cr.31** `[done 2026-10-15; proven on the stunt node]` **AHR destroy fixes from node pass 1b (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave1b.md R2/R5).** (D1, SHIPPED since ahrcache.1 `a9e0e2f`, 0.4.0) `LVS_ARGS` carries segment fields (`cache_mode`, `cache_policy`), so `lvs` returns one row per segment and destroy's per-row `lvremove` fails on the second row of any multi-segment LV — every uncached pool with ≥2 bands and every expanded pool; the job stops after unmount, fstab edit and the first lvremove (a second Destroy finishes). Fix at the source (the report and/or one-row-per-LV parsing) and check every reader of the same args (topology, cache, expand). (D2) `destroyAhrPool` unmounts, edits fstab and daemon-reloads BEFORE `requireOwnedVgPvs`, so a refused destroy has already unmounted a foreign LV and deleted the operator's fstab line — the gate must run before ANY change. (D3) the cr.24 reference list names the pool's own mountpoint as a reference to itself. Opus; node re-run of a multi-band destroy after. A point-release note is owed for D1 (shipped since 0.4.0).

**Wave 3 stories (cut 2026-10-15 from docs/WAVE3-DESIGN.md §9; the design is the spec, its §2 inventory row is each story's acceptance list).** Order: cr.32 → cr.33 → cr.36…cr.43 (any order, one merge each); cr.34 → cr.35 independent of the guard chain; cr.44 (UI) merges BEFORE cr.36/cr.37 bind the destroy flags; cr.45, cr.46 independent. Design §10 questions: **Q2** (export/destroy of a PVE-owned pool) is answered by the §2 ruling "PVE territory is read-only and hands-off" and DESIGN pvepool.1 ("pool-level actions on a PVE pool stay hands-off") — the API refuses with 409 `PVE_MANAGED` as the panel already does; **Q4** (unmount references) follows cr.24 and the no-babysitting rule — warn, never refuse; **Q1** (delete of a task during its run) and **Q3** (request-path reads past 30 s become jobs?) are the operator's and block cr.41 and that part of cr.35.
- **cr.32** `[done 2026-10-15]` The guard (`safety/guard.ts`) and bound confirms; pool export and destroy as the reference specs (incl. the `PVE_MANAGED` gate). Opus. Pool export and destroy run one gate list (exists, system pool, PVE ownership, held by a LUN) at the route and again in the job against facts read then, their confirm signs the whole request (`force`, `cleanup`) plus the guid and, with cleanup, the leaves to wipe, and a resend with a different option answers 409 `CONFIRM_SCOPE_CHANGED` naming it. cr.32r: the job door's guid re-check (swap and vanish), the nested-PVE-storage race, the resend scope move, flag-spelling equivalence and per-object single-use codes are pinned by route-level tests, and mock mode resolves ONE storage.cfg (env, then the fixture) for the pool, iSCSI and access readers, so no test reads the host's /etc/pve/storage.cfg.
- **cr.33** `[done 2026-10-15]` Per-object locks and the long lane in the job queue. After cr.32. Opus. The queue starts a job only when it can grant all of its lock claims at once (shared/exclusive, per-key FIFO), a lock-waiting job holds no slot and its progress names the job it waits for, locks are released in `finally` or, after an abandoned child, when that child exits (`holdsLocks`), long-lane jobs fill at most 3 of the 4 slots, and pool export and destroy now hold their pool's guid and name keys (plus each by-id disk a cleanup wipes) while the replication, backup, cloud and restore runs ride the long lane.
- **cr.34** `[done 2026-10-15; node proofs N4/N5 run 2026-10-15 — N5 PROVEN, N4 PARTLY: docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave3.md rows N4/N5 (the WAVE3-DESIGN §10 node-check rows of the same titles)]` Executor deadlines (process-group kill, `CommandTimeoutError`, lanes, abandoned registry, 503 `TIMEOUT`). Opus. Every command now runs under one spawn-based executor whose deadline (request 10 s, job and background 300 s, a `/usr/bin/timeout` wrapper's own plus 5 s, or a per-call budget) signals the child's process group TERM → 5 s → KILL → 2 s and then rejects with `CommandTimeoutError`, listing a child that outlived its SIGKILL on GET /v1/health and as a dashboard `command` warning; the data movers opt out by name (`null-sites.test.ts`) and `zpool create`, recursive `zfs destroy`, `mkfs.btrfs` and `update-initramfs` carry their §6.3 budgets. cr.34r (review fixes): a child over its `maxBuffer` now takes the same group TERM → KILL → abandon ladder before the call rejects with its overflow error (so a grandchild still writing can no longer keep the daemon reading for good), and GET /v1/health and the dashboard name an abandoned command by its label and cause, never its argv.
- **cr.35** `[done 2026-10-15; N4/N5 run 2026-10-15 — N5 PROVEN, N4 PARTLY (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave3.md rows N4/N5: the gateway 504 ladder and the job-lane deadline/abandoned registry proven, but no request-path read blocks on a stuck pool device on that node, so 503 TIMEOUT and the per-section `unavailable` were never seen live); Q3 sites at 12 s (cr.35b, cr.35r), the §10 ruling still owed]` Request-path budgets and the gateway ladder (§6.3). Opus. The §6.3 per-command budgets ride one table in front of the daemon's executor (smartctl, ssh-keyscan, lsof, fuser in every lane; ssh, pbc reads, the import scan, snapshot lists and `send -nP` on the request path), every ssh carries ConnectTimeout/ServerAlive keepalives, `udevadm settle` and PVE notify are bounded, share paths and the ZFS mountpoint read run through the executor (a path that does not answer is `not-answering`), GET /v1/status reads each section under its own 12 s budget and lists the ones it could not read, and the gateway answers 504 at 20 s on the local socket and splits the forward into 5 s connect and 25 s response. cr.35b: every request-path budget is now at most 15 s — the §6.3 rows, the three Q3 sites (the PBS catalog shell, the rclone remote probe and the cloud preview, whose `--timeout` is 12 s so rclone's own error wins over the wrapper's 124) and the dashboard's section budget — so the daemon answers a hung read with its own verdict before the gateway's 20 s socket hang-up replaces it with a 504. cr.35r: the rule is the WHOLE ladder — every request-path row and Q3 wrapper, budget plus the 5 s TERM and 2 s KILL graces, settles inside the gateway's 20 s (rows and wrappers 12 s, rclone's own `--timeout` 9 s), and a hung AHR membership read on GET /v1/disks answers 503 TIMEOUT instead of rendering member disks free.
- **cr.36** `[done 2026-10-15]` ZFS pool verbs on the guard. After cr.33. Opus. Every pool verb now runs its gates at the route and again in the job under its lock keys (upgrade and mountpoint bound to the guid, create/add/attach/replace/raidz-expand re-check every disk and hold its `disk:` key, the attach family behind the system-pool and PVE gates, the leaf and vdev token re-resolved in the job, forced create and import signing the whole request), and a pool PVE uses is refused 409 `PVE_MANAGED` by every pool verb. cr.36r: the pool mountpoint (and the NFS export create path and the restore target) is canonicalised once at the door with the lock key's normaliser, so `/mnt//pve` meets the reserved refusal and `//` spellings meet every collision gate; an unreadable `zpool status` in remove-vdev and raidz-expand answers 409 "could not be read", not 404.
- **cr.37** `[done 2026-10-15]` ZFS dataset/snapshot verbs and `fs.setAccess` on the guard. After cr.33. Opus. Every dataset and snapshot verb and `fs.setAccess` (datasets, AHR and share doors) runs one gate list at the route and again in the job under its `zfs:`/`ahr:` lock claims, the dataset destroy confirm binds `recursive` and its child datasets (name + createtxg), the rollback confirm binds `force` and the newer snapshots and bookmarks it destroys, and a set-access plan is bound to the revision of the ACL state it was read from, so a child, bookmark or ACL entry that appears after the confirm answers 409 `CONFIRM_SCOPE_CHANGED`/`STATE_CHANGED` and nothing runs; the review follow-up adds that a set-access plan refuses 409 when the ACLs are enabled but unreadable — at every door — instead of planning a write from the mode bits, and the LIO-unreadable disclosure now surfaces on the rollback confirm and the property-set job result as it does on destroy.
- **cr.38** `[done 2026-10-15]` AHR verbs on the guard (two sessions: short verbs, long verbs). After cr.33. Opus. Session A: create, mountpoint, remount, destroy, snapshot create/delete/rollback, cache attach/detach, spare add/remove and abandon run one gate list at the route and again in the job under `ahr:<vg uuid>` (the name key when the VG UUID is unreadable) plus the disks and path each writes — destroy re-runs in-flight, intent, system pool and LUN-held, mountpoint/remount/rollback gain the system-pool gate, cache attach refuses a disk `blkid -p` finds a content signature on and proves the VG the pool's before `vgextend`, create signs disks, tier and mountpoint, abandon binds the intent id — and the udev cache event and the boot cache rung run as jobs with the pool's key. Session B: the queue gains `ctx.release()`/`ctx.reacquire()` (a long job gives its keys back for an md wait it only watches, keeps its slot, and takes them back through the same FIFO; an abandoned child still holds them) and `reguard()` re-acquires before it re-reads, so expand, resume, replace, readd, parity rewrite and mirror reconcile release around every md wait and re-gate before every act step against the pool as it is now (expansions bind the VG UUID, the submitted band arrays and the intent's id and state, re-run composability and, for expand, the degraded refusal, halt their intent when refused, and prove the VG with `requirePoolVg` before `vgextend`; the band verbs bind the band's array UUID), scrub and repair run on the guard under the pool's key shared with the job conflict (an older job only), the node-wide md check, repair's path confinement and LUN session re-run in the job, and the new-LUN image restore holds its `iscsi-target:` key until the LUN is mapped.
- **cr.39** `[done 2026-10-15]` Mount verbs on the guard, and the unmount reference check §2 Mounts already describes (warn, Q4). After cr.33. Opus. Every mount verb runs one gate list at the route and again in the job under its `mount:<path>` lock (held by a LUN, the verb's state, the fstab entry for every create), an edit is bound to its fstab line's revision, a remove always confirms and binds `removeMountpointDir` and the credentials file it deletes, a forced unmount detaches only the holders it confirmed, and unmount, disable and remove name the shares, exports, backup and cloud tasks and nested mounts riding the path in the confirm and report new ones on the job.
- **cr.40** `[done 2026-10-15]` iSCSI and image restore on the guard. After cr.33. Opus. Target update, enable/disable and delete are bound to the target's revision (ACLs, portals, LUNs, enabled, auth) and re-run their gates in the job under the iSCSI mutex, so an update never deletes an ACL added since or trips on one already removed; LUN add and the restore-as-a-new-LUN door take index and ACL grants from the target read in the job; LUN update and delete refuse a session that logged in after the request; LUN delete binds `destroyBacking` (re-challenged in the panel) and re-runs the snapshot check; the in-place restore reads `enabled` fresh; every verb holds its `iscsi-target:`/`iscsi-lun:` keys (the in-place restore for its whole run; the new-LUN restore until its LUN is mapped (cr.38b release)).
- **cr.41** `[done 2026-10-15]` Tasks, schedules and registries on the guard. After cr.33. **Q1 ruled 2026-10-15:** deleting a task or schedule while its run is active is refused with 409 until the run ends or the operator cancels it (the sentence names the run and the cancel). Local (DeepSeek) + cross-model review. Every task, schedule, repo and remote verb runs one gate list at the route and again in the job under its `task:`/`schedule:` name key — a create re-checks existence so two creates of one name never both write (the second waits under per-key FIFO and refuses), an update binds the stored unit's revision so a deleted task/schedule reads gone and never resurrects and a hand edit fails `STATE_CHANGED`, a delete refuses an active run at both doors naming the run and how to cancel, a schedule fire re-reads the schedule in the job instead of acting on the route's copy, the backup repo secret is written after the CAS (a lost CAS leaves no secret behind) with the repo IN_USE and the replication-remote and cloud-remote reference checks re-run in the job, and the direct backup/cloud runs hold the task key shared while their Run-Now supervisors hold no key at all. *(Cross-model review of cr.41/41b/47e, Qwen 2026-10-15, 14 constructed probes + one mutation re-run per commit: 0 defects. Possibles recorded: a replication delete can land in the trigger's `--no-block` window before the unit is active (not a Q1 case — the run is not active yet; bounded: the run then fails task-not-found); a 0-byte `.service` with no `.timer` reads absent to DELETE (writeTaskUnitFiles is not tmp+rename — grooming); a `.timer`-only hand edit is rewritten by a waiting update (the revision binds the service text, §3.3 as stated; unpinned). Node rows owed: whether `zfs destroy -r` is partial on a clone-origin refusal as it is on a hold; zvol snapshots in the recursive sweep.)*
- **cr.41b** `[done 2026-10-15]` The four delete verbs' guard object is the unit AS STORED — a `.service`/`.timer` that exists reads deletable, so a unit whose `X-ANAS-Task`/`X-ANAS-Schedule` line no longer parses (a hand edit, or a task an older ANAS wrote in a shape the current schema refuses) is skipped by the list yet still removable through the API — and their active-run gate fails closed: a `systemctl show` that cannot answer refuses 409 saying the run state could not be read and nothing was deleted (the status/dashboard readers keep their fail-open reads). *(Node-proven 2026-10-16, wave-2b row 2, systemd 257: `systemctl show -p ActiveState` exits 0 with inactive for a loaded, bad-setting, load-error, masked AND not-found unit — only a template name with no instance exits 1, which BackupName cannot produce — so a stored unit is never refused `run-state-unreadable` on a healthy node; the refusal itself proven with a wrapped systemctl (now carries reason `run-state-unreadable`, N2); a corrupted X-ANAS-Task unit is omitted by the list and DELETE 202 removes both files. N3: a bad-setting unit lists as healthy.)*
- **cr.48** `[done 2026-10-15; node PROVEN (wave-2 record row 3: 202 + poll + Cancel in the API and all three dialogs; note: only the preview dialog shows a progress line — the remote test and the catalog listing show none)]` **Ruled 2026-10-15 (W3 Q3): the slow interactive reads run without a short timeout and can be cancelled.** The cloud sync preview, the rclone remote test and the PBS catalog listing become jobs (202 + poll, the result in `job.result`) with a generous ceiling (minutes, not seconds) and a Cancel button in the dialog that cancels the job (they only read, so a cancel has no consequence); the 12 s synchronous budgets and their Q3 markers go. Read-only jobs need Sys.Audit (cr.28 note). Opus. The preview (600 s), the remote test (120 s) and the catalog listing (300 s) answer 202 with a `read.*` job (`jobs/read-jobs.ts`, the marker cr.28 keys on) that the dialog polls with a progress line and a Cancel, which stops the child's whole process group through the job-cancel door without a confirm. *(Wave-2b 2026-10-16: **D13 fixed** — the cloud wizard's destroy handler called `win.down()` outside its try and threw on an emptied window, so after every cloud task Create/Edit the grid never reloaded or selected the row (72-cloud.js, one line; cloud-tasks-ui 0/4 → 8/8 ×3). **D14 recorded, low**: `jobRejected` runs `onFailed` (the reload) before `Ext.Msg.alert('Save failed')`, and the reload's own failure alert replaces it ~0.1 s later in the singleton — the operator never sees the save's refusal (10-api.js:235-239 + 72-cloud.js:390; cloud-remotes-ui test 7 flaky on it). → cr.48g.)*
- **cr.48b** `[done 2026-10-15]` A secret never rides in a reply even when a backend echoes one — the three read jobs mask every secret value they handed their tool (`maskSecretValues`, the output-side mirror of the argv guard beside `assertNoSecretValues` in `services/secret-argv.ts`), so rclone/pbc echoing the token or repo password in their own stderr word (the remote test's `message`, the preview's `errors`, the pbc reads' `detail`) leaves `[secret]` in its place, both the plain value and its `encodeURIComponent` form; the verdict classification still runs on the raw text, and a hung child's `TIMEOUT` failure names the command, never its stderr — no secret reaches `job.error`.

- **cr.48e** `[done 2026-10-15]` True now: the four carriers cr.48b–d's review found outside their own enumeration are masked at one boundary each against the secret values the run handed pbc, classification always on the raw text — the repository **Test** verdict's `detail` (its datastore/namespace/generic-auth branches quote pbc's `Error:` line verbatim into the reply), the **prune** verdict's `detail` (ONE pass, upstream in `services/backup-prune.ts`, because the prune-preview reply and the run notification's Warnings block are both built from that same field — masking at the notification builder would leave the preview unmasked), the backup **run** failure detail that becomes `job.error`, and the forwarded `restore …` **progress line** at both image-restore doors (a line matching the progress shape can carry an error fragment quoting the credential; the percentage parse keeps reading the raw chunk). `maskSecretValues` now also masks the `+`-for-space form of a value's URL encoding and both Unicode normalisations when they differ from the stored value; BASE64 is recorded out of scope (a tool does not echo a credential base64-encoded in an error line), and the 4-character floor now applies per form — with the secret schemas setting no minimum length, a secret under four characters rides through, the operator's call, recorded. *(Cross-model review, DeepSeek 2026-10-16, construct-it at the real routes: 0 defects — the prune detail has no third consumer (audit carries only the verdict), the repo-Test route feeds a saved repo's STORED secret into both env and mask so cr.48b's saved-remote blind spot is not replicated there, the restore progress line carries the percentage and `[secret]`, the per-form floor and longest-first hold; the success-path strings from `parseBackupProgress` reach `job.result` unmasked — confirmed by running it — and are cr.48f.)*
- **cr.48f** `[done 2026-10-16]` True now: the SUCCESS-path strings `parseBackupProgress` extracts are masked in ONE seam at the parser against the secret the run handed pbc — the archive stats, target line, nofile warning, duration, image sources, skip paths and change-detection summary — so `job.result`, the job's progress line, the skipped-mount notices and the 16.12 notification body all carry `[secret]` wherever a backend echoed the credential and the whole job record holds no value (proven at the route and by the mutation check); the taxonomy and the parse read the RAW stderr. **Rule 2:** the supervisor/journal door needs no mask — the backup unit's journal (and the Run-Now supervisor's input) is written only by the `backup-task` runner, which prints the already-masked result JSON and the already-masked error line, and the unit carries NO secret in its environment (the daemon resolves the credential at run time, `readRepoSecret`), so `readRecentJournal`/`failureDetailFromJournal`/`parseHelperResult` take the journal verbatim; the one residual is precisely that a FUTURE journal writer that ever printed RAW pbc stderr would surface it (the readers hold no secret at read time and are not a mask door) — pinned. **Rule 3:** the cr.48b saved-rclone-remote blind spot is closed — a saved remote's probe now masks (and guard-checks) against the remote's OWN stored credentials, recovered at probe time by reading its section: name-rule secrets are stored plain by the write path and IsPassword values are reversed IN PROCESS through `revealObscured` (rclone's `Reveal` reimplemented beside the write path — raw-URL-safe unpadded base64 + AES-256-CTR under rclone's fixed public `cryptKey`, KAT-pinned against the real binary's output and rclone's own test vectors), so NO credential — stored or recovered — ever touches an argv (a subprocess `rclone reveal` would put the value it decodes in `ps` for the call's lifetime, and `reveal -` does not read stdin — checked on the local rclone and the node's), bool options excluded so ordinary probe text is never shredded; the residual is a stored credential that fails to decode in process (a hand-edited/foreign obscured value, malformed base64, a value whose plaintext is outside the 16-byte/UTF-8 bars) or one under the 4-character floor — its plain text would ride unmasked, all bounded. *(Two cross-model reviews of 9f47134d in parallel, 2026-10-16 — Qwen (dgx3) and the new ai1 lane, same brief, both construct-it against real rclone: 0 defects from either; ten fresh obscurings round-trip, every malformed shape decodes to nothing, a one-byte key change fails the KATs, no executor argv carries a stored or decoded value, a backend echo of the decoded password is masked. ai1 alone found and ran one real possible — the skipped path was joined from two already-masked halves, so a secret straddling the join survived — fixed: the join is masked after it is made, test pinned. Recorded: an IsPassword value that decodes to a dictionary word shreds that word in the probe's message (the 4-character floor's converse, by design); the parser call-site wiring is pinned by the route test only.)*

- **cr.49** `[done 2026-10-15]` **Ruled 2026-10-15: watch-only jobs get their own reserved slot.** A Run-Now supervisor only watches the unit it started, yet it takes a long-lane slot, so three Run Nows fill the long lane and their own direct runs wait until the supervisors give up (600 s). The queue gains a `watch` lane with 8 slots of its own beyond the 4 (counted apart from the regular slots and the long cap, refused at submit when it carries lock claims), and the backup and cloud Run-Now supervisors ride it while their direct runs stay long, so three Run Nows and their three direct runs all run at once.
- **cr.47** `[done (a)(b) 2026-10-15; (c) node-proven 2026-10-15: recursive SNAPSHOT destroy is atomic (a held or send-busy child destroys nothing) → the destroy may take its key SHARED; `zfs rename -r` is NOT stopped by a hold or by a running send (F2: the send completes and the receiver gets the old name), so the rename has no ZFS busy refusal to lean on — stays EXCLUSIVE unless the operator rules otherwise; (e) node-proven PARTIAL: `zfs destroy -r` over a held child snapshot destroys the siblings, the held child's children and the parent's own snapshot before failing, and `-nv` does not see the hold → BUILD (e): read `zfs holds -r` across the sweep ahead and refuse naming each hold (nf.3 D11 pattern) — story cr.47e]` **Operator rulings 2026-10-15 on wave-3 review items.** (a) A verb the API allowed before wave 3 is not newly restricted on a system pool: attach, replace and raidz-expand drop cr.36's system-pool gate (the PVE-ownership gate stays — standing hands-off ruling); `zpool upgrade` stays unrestricted. (b) One fact, one answer: a PVE-owned dataset is refused 409 `PVE_MANAGED` at every dataset/snapshot/access door, as pools are (was 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`); the panel's handling checked. (c) Ruled 2026-10-15: OK provided no mid-state abort is possible — verify that a recursive snapshot destroy/rename is atomic in OpenZFS (all-or-none) before switching to SHARED; a variant that can partially complete stays EXCLUSIVE — lock granularity: snapshot destroy and rename take their dataset's key SHARED (as the schedule's own prune already does) — a send in flight is protected by ZFS's own busy refusal on the snapshot it reads, so deleting an unrelated old snapshot no longer waits hours behind a mover; rollback and dataset destroy stay exclusive. (d) Ruled 2026-10-15: no partially performed operation — a LUN delete with `destroyBacking` checks the zvol's snapshots BEFORE anything is unmapped and refuses when they cannot be read (fail closed for this case), so the delete either does both halves or neither. (e) Same no-partial-op rule, found by nf.3: a recursive dataset destroy (`zfs destroy -r`) over a tree where a child's snapshot carries a hold may destroy some children before failing on the held one — prove it on the node; if it can, the destroy reads `zfs holds` across the sweep ahead of time and refuses naming each hold, as nf.3 D11 does for a forced rollback. True now (a)(b): the attach family (attach, replace, raidz-expand) asks only the PVE-ownership question — 409 `PVE_MANAGED` when PVE uses the pool, no system-pool refusal — and every dataset, snapshot, access and share door answers a real PVE-territory fact with 409 `PVE_MANAGED` reason `pve-managed` (an unreadable storage.cfg, a different fact, keeps its 400). *(merge 2026-10-15: the replication snapshot-first door joined (b) — 409 PVE_MANAGED at the request and `PVE_MANAGED` as the job's error code when PVE claims the source while the job waits; 16 job-re-gate expectations in access-guard, ahr-access, datasets-guard, mover-source-locks and replication tests flipped with it.)* *(Cross-model review of (a)(b) + cr.28, DeepSeek 2026-10-15, construct-it: 0 defects; its one possible — the snapshot-schedule target door still answered 400 for an owned dataset — folded into (b): 409 PVE_MANAGED there too, config-unreadable keeps 400. cr.28 clean: every header variant fails closed, a duplicate header line through a raw socket → 401, only the three `read.*` jobs exist and none mutates, boot work carries no privileges by design.)*
- **cr.47e** `[done 2026-10-15]` True now: a recursive dataset destroy reads ONE `zfs list -H -t snapshot -r` sweep and ONE `zfs holds -H` across every snapshot it would destroy — the dataset's own and each descendant's — and refuses 409 `snapshot-held` naming each hold and holder (and `snapshot-holds-unreadable` when the sweep or the holds cannot be read) BEFORE anything runs, so nothing unheld is ever destroyed first, the gate running at the route and again in the job like the rest of the list, and the (c) lock split shipped with it: the snapshot destroy takes its dataset key SHARED (the node's c4/c5 "dataset is busy" refusal protects the snapshot a send reads, and a recursive snapshot destroy is atomic), the rename keeps EXCLUSIVE (c2/c3: a running send does not stop a rename — F2). *(Node-proven 2026-10-16, wave-2b row 1: a held ZVOL snapshot in the sweep → 409 `snapshot-held` naming it, nothing destroyed; the zvol's snapshot IS in the `zfs list -t snapshot -r` sweep; a clone origin outside the tree is ATOMIC in ZFS (`filesystem has dependent clones`, nothing destroyed, `-rnv` sees it) so through ANAS the job fails with the raw text and nothing is partial — F3: the confirm does not name the clone; `-o name,clones` could ride the same sweep.)*

Every user and group verb runs one gate list at the route and again in the job under its `uid:`/`gid:` key (deletes and creates also take the case-folded `idname:` key, a create into groups their `gid:` keys), the job compares the uid/gid it reads with the one the request was computed from (a namesake re-created in between fails `IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, and the delete confirms bind the uid/gid), and the share references, the primary-group use and the SMB password's passdb-owner check re-run in the job; uid reuse by a re-created account stays cr.29's.
- **cr.43** `[done 2026-10-15]` Share verbs on the guard (vanished share → `NOT_FOUND`, no duplicate stanza). After cr.33. Local (Qwen). SMB and NFS update and remove run their gates at the route and again in the job under the share's lock (SMB section case-folded, NFS export path normalised): a share that vanished while the job waited fails `NOT_FOUND`, an edit is bound to its stanza's or line's revision (`STATE_CHANGED` on a hand edit), a create re-checks existence in the job so no duplicate stanza or export line is written, and the `exportfs` baseline and reload run under the export's lock. The create/edit grant rides cr.37's revision-bound `setAccessGuardSpec`: the plan is prepared at the route and a moved ACL fails `STATE_CHANGED` in the job before any setfacl, chown or chmod argv runs, and the recursive confirm signs the composed grant — a resent edit of the entries is `CONFIRM_SCOPE_CHANGED`.
- **cr.44** `[done 2026-10-15]` The confirm window re-challenges a bound option in place. Before cr.36/cr.37. Opus. Pool destroy `cleanup`, dataset destroy `recursive` and rollback `force` are `confirmBind` items sent explicitly at their default (`?cleanup=false`), and ticking one re-sends the unconfirmed request and swaps the daemon's warnings and code into the same window (`confirm-bound-options.test.ts` pins "no code ⇒ 409" for every form of the three flags; Playwright N3 is the node pass's).
- **cr.45** `[done 2026-10-15; browser probe owed]` `pollJob` retry, lost contact, still running; cancel-run toasts on `completed` only. Local (Qwen); browser probe owed. A job is now polled to its real end — `onComplete` fires only on `completed`, a still-running job announces itself once past the attention window and keeps being polled at 5 s, and a vanished job or 60 s of poll failures alerts and recovers through `onLost`/`onFailed(null)` instead of dying silently. The attention and lost-contact windows are wall-clock: a poll that takes long to answer counts as watched/contact-missing time, so a slow node advances both windows instead of stretching them.
- **cr.46** `[done 2026-10-15]` Replication/snapshot-schedule delete through `runJob`; poll responses applied to the selection they were made for. Local (Qwen). The replication task and snapshot-schedule deletes now submit through `runJob` — the completion toast, the failure alert and the grid reload all ride the job — and the replication/backup/scrub poll loaders read the grid's selection when the response arrives and drop any response older than the newest request (a per-grid `_loadSeq`, the `_previewSeq` idiom), proven by `poll-selection.harness.mjs`. A delete that ends failed or lost reloads its grid through `onFailed` (pollJob's lost terminal calls it with `null`), the snapshot-schedule loader now carries the same per-grid sequence guard and selection-at-response read, and the newest response clears a loading mask whether its own load was quiet or not (a stale one never touches it).

Every mover's claim set pinned; export, destroy, namesake import and the guid-fallback destroy all wait behind a mover — the shared name key rides both ways; the `unlocked` sentence is the disclosed advisory the story allows (an unresolvable path takes no key and the run says so), not the destructive verbs' fail-closed. **Release-note line owed for nf.1 D2 (shipped in 0.4.3, access.1/access.2):** "A recursive permission change on a dataset or share also changed ownership and permissions inside other filesystems mounted below it (an NFS or CIFS mount, another pool's dataset). - **nf.1** `[done 2026-10-15]` Node-pass fixes D1/D2 (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave3.md): a by-id partition as the new device of attach, replace or raidz expand is checked as a partition at both doors (it exists, sits on a disk the inventory knows and that is not hands-off, is not mounted or held, and `blkid -p` finds no signature) under its whole disk's `disk:` key, and a recursive permission apply walks with `find -xdev`, prunes every mount it did not name (named child datasets are walked from their own mountpoints; the rest are listed in the confirm and the result as `not changed: <path> (<fstype>)`), binds that scope in the plan's revision, and refuses before touching anything when an NFS/CIFS mount below the tree is dead. *(Cross-model review, DeepSeek 2026-10-15, construct-it: 0 defects. The walk boundary is `-xdev` per root PLUS a `-path … -prune` for every mount below the root the request did not name (a same-filesystem bind mount is stopped by the prune alone); a named child dataset is walked from its own mountpoint; a mount appearing after the route's read is caught by the bound walk scope (STATE_CHANGED at the resend and at the job); an unreadable or garbled mount table refuses at the door; odd path characters survive as single argv elements. Partitions: a free partition on a pool-member disk is accepted by design (#66), a member partition refused by its signature, create and add-vdev still refuse partitions. Three findmnt readers at deliberately different altitudes (one parser) — hygiene, not a defect. Residual: the standard post-reverify TOCTOU window.)*
- **nf.2** `[done 2026-10-15]` Node-pass minor fixes D3–D7, D9 (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave3.md; D8 shipped as cr.33b): the mount busy gate lists holders with lsof when the node has it and otherwise with the busy diagnosis's own `fuser -m` run and parser, and when neither answers the unmount, disable and remove confirms say the holders could not be listed and the job does not force the unmount (a forced unmount whose holders cannot be listed again in the job refuses, nothing done) (D3); a destroy with cleanup lists, binds and wipes one set, the ONLINE data, log, special and dedup leaves, and names each cache, spare or not-ONLINE leaf it keeps and why (D5); the foreign-PV advisory says Destroy refuses the teardown (D4), the lost-contact sentence keeps its default subject lower-case (D6), the scope-change sentence separates the two sets as `was: …; now: …` (D7), and a truncated cloud preview keeps the last stderr line rclone wrote (D9). *(Cross-model review, Qwen 2026-10-15, construct-it: 0 defects. D3: lsof absent + fuser busy lists the holders; both absent → the "may be busy" confirm and a plain umount, never `-l`; psmisc 23.7's fuser exits 1 for idle AND error, the code reads only the rows, so an error reads as no holders — safe, since a force needs a NAMED holder. D5: one `cleanupLeaves` answer feeds scope, locks, confirm and job; a leaf gone or re-claimed at job time is skipped with its reason, never zapped. D4/D6/D7/D9 match the record kind-for-kind. Node-only: the ISO lsof question, real hung-NFS tool behaviour. Cosmetic: kernel-path leaves print `/dev/…` in skip lists vs bare by-id ids.)*
- **nf.3** `[done 2026-10-15]` Node-pass 3b fixes D10/D11 (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave3b.md): a busy unmount, disable or remove binds the holder set it showed (pid plus the name cut to lsof's nine characters, `unknown` when no tool answered), so a different set at the resend is 409 `CONFIRM_SCOPE_CHANGED` naming both sets and the job refuses any holder outside the confirmed set (D10); a forced rollback whose sweep includes a snapshot with a user hold is refused 409 before anything runs, at the route and again in the job, naming each held snapshot and its holder (`held by replication task 'x': anas-repl-…`), and an unreadable `zfs holds -H` refuses too (D11). *(Cross-model review, Qwen 2026-10-15, 12 constructed probes + both mutations: 0 defects. Proven right but unpinned by the builder — a holder gone at the resend (SCOPE_CHANGED naming both sets), pid reuse at the resend and in the job, an unknown==unknown re-listing accepted but never forced, a hold on the TARGET snapshot allowed, bookmarks never reaching `zfs holds`, an exit-0 garbled holds row — adopted as tests in nf.3b. Possible, safe direction: lsof's 9-column cut vs fuser's comm may disagree on a non-ASCII name → at worst a spurious SCOPE_CHANGED. Node-only: real `zfs holds -H` output, lsof's truncation, EBUSY-on-hold ordering.)*
- **nf.3b** `[done 2026-10-15]` Pins the six behaviours the nf.3 cross-model review proved right but found unpinned by any test: a holder gone at the resend (409 `CONFIRM_SCOPE_CHANGED` naming both sets, no job), a pid reused by another program at the resend (`SCOPE_CHANGED`) and inside the job (`STATE_CHANGED` naming `<name> (pid N)`, zero umount argv), an unknown==unknown resend accepted while the job runs a plain umount, never the lazy detach (exact argv asserted), a hold on a forced rollback's TARGET allowed (the sweep excludes it and the rollback runs), bookmarks never reaching `zfs holds` (the argv names exactly the swept snapshots), and an exit-0 garbled (tab-less) `holds` row read as no hold per the documented fail-open parse — each in the door's own test file and each shown to fail when its property is broken (the review's mutations, run and restored); tests only, no production change.

- **Operator rulings 2026-10-16 on the open design notes of the wave-2/2b records (recorded, no code change):** **F1** (wave-2 F1: a locked encrypted dataset makes every automatic uid/gid pick refuse `id-check-unreadable` until its key is loaded; an explicit id works and the sentence says so) — an edge case, stays fail-closed as built. **F2** (snapshot rename keeps its dataset key EXCLUSIVE because the node proved `zfs rename -r` is stopped by neither a hold nor a running send) — "ground truth wins": EXCLUSIVE is the ruling, closed. **cr.33d coarse grain** (one failed array read = the whole AHR topology unreadable) — "not great if one array affects another": → rider *ahr.r1* (§3 open riders), next release. **login.defs absent-key over-estimate** (PVE ships `USERGROUPS_ENAB`) — a no-op in practice, closed as documented. **Secret minimum length** (the secret schemas set none, so a secret under the 4-character floor rides through the mask) — not ANAS's to enforce: bad practice is the operator's choice, the mask's floor stays, closed. **cr.48 progress line** (only the preview dialog shows one; the remote test and the catalog listing poll without one) — later, if users raise it; not a story.

- **Pre-push smoke of the RC (2026-10-16):** the five specs the stale mdadm.conf had blocked (ahr-cache-api, ahr-cache-review, ahr-cache-ui, ahr-expand-zero-gain, smbsvc-api) run green on df28fbfc after four stale expectations were re-cut to cr.20/cr.22 and the AHR fixtures learned to unpin what a refused destroy leaves (docs/LIVE-PROOF-0.4.4-wave2b.md, post-pass section). One daemon finding, **S1 → story cr.50 `[done 2026-10-16]`:** `detachAhrCache` reports a cache disk released on sgdisk's exit code alone; when the kernel refuses the whole-table re-read (a holder at that instant — shape 1's disk hot-added two seconds earlier) the partition stays in the kernel, the disk reads `other` and nothing says why. Build (operator 2026-10-16: self-heal first, fail only after the attempt) = after `sgdisk -d`: settle, check the kernel still lists the partition; if so `partx -d <n> <dev>` (BLKPG, no exclusive re-read), settle, check again with a short bounded retry (the holders are brief: blkid, pvscan, a poll); released with a progress line saying the kernel kept the old table and the partition was removed directly, so the journal shows the race without a failure; fail naming the disk and the command only when nothing cleared it. One function in `detachAhrCache`, tests over the fake executor: partition gone after sgdisk → released, no partx; still listed → partx -d runs and the job is released with the heal line; still listed after the retries → the job fails naming the disk. The journal line is the fixture. True now: `detachAhrCache` after `sgdisk -d` settles, asks the kernel (via `readDiskTree`) whether the slice is gone, removes it directly with `partx -d <n> <dev>` when the kernel kept the old table, settles and re-checks with a short bounded retry (10 × 200 ms, busy partx retried), and only then — if the partition is still listed — fails naming the disk and the one command to run, so the journal shows the race without a failure and a healed disk still releases. *(Cross-model review of the build, DeepSeek 2026-10-16, construct-it: 1 DEFECT reproduced — a null disk tree after `sgdisk -d` (the per-disk lsblk failed, exactly what a hot-add udev storm produces) read as "gone" and released the disk without the heal — fixed by the coordinator (three-state verdict: an unreadable kernel view is never "gone"; the kernel name falls back to realpath; the failure quotes partx's last answer; 3 tests, mutation-proven), the fix reviewed by ai1 (0 defects, every loop exit tabled: `released` is reachable only through a READ tree that lists no slice). Also from the review: a two-slice detach whose first heal fails leaves the second slice's PV orphaned after the vgreduce — re-running Detach re-finds both labelled slices and completes, so the error's "re-running Detach is safe" holds; the route suite now takes the heal path on every detach (the fixture answers carved then blank), so the clean-adopt shape at route level is covered only by the service test.)*

#### Wave 4 — the 2026-10-16 whole-project review, ALL findings in this release (operator 2026-10-16: "address the lot of them now … rather than flinging out a whole bunch of point releases"; H4 ruled not a finding, §2)

Scope is `docs/CODE-REVIEW-2026-10-16.md` (1C/4H/9M/27L/5W at main eeda9fcc); each story names its findings, the finding text is the spec, the **Fix:** line is the build, every [R] is confirmed at the line before it is fixed, every story is cross-model reviewed construct-it and the lot gets a node pass before the fleet bake. Stories are cut along file lines so parallel lanes do not collide; waves are ordered by shared files (pools.ts, mounts.ts, backup.ts, cloud.ts).

**Wave 4a (disjoint files, parallel):**
- **cr.51** `[done 2026-10-16]` **C1** True now: `SnapshotSchedule.name` is the shared `SingleLine` (the `ScheduledSnapshot.name` copy was dropped in cr.51r — type-only, never parsed at runtime), and every unit writer refuses a control character before anything is written (backup and replication joined in cr.51r) — the snapshot writer guards the name in both `Description=` lines and quotes the id ExecStart token, the cloud writer guards the name and the raw OnCalendar expression, the scrub writer the cadence and the pool argv; `quoteExecArg` (lifted from replication, now refusing NUL/TAB as the schemas do) and `refuseControlChars` live ONCE in systemd-unit-store, both units render before the first write, and the route answers 400 with the schema's sentence for a newline name — each writer's and the route's injection test mutation-proven against its own guard. *(Cross-model review, Opus 2026-10-16, every C1 vector through POST and PUT as a Sys.Modify-only user, every unit writer tabled field by field, systemd-analyze verify 259 on the written text, full suite 6599/6599: NO DEFECT. Confirmed harmless: U+0085/U+2028/U+2029, a trailing backslash, a literal `\n`, `%n`/`$HOME`/`;` reach Description= only (systemd breaks lines on LF/CR/NUL only). Artifacts in the session scratchpad cr51-review/.)*

True now: ONE builder, `remoteSecretValues` (services/rclone-config.ts), makes a remote's secret list from rclone.conf in process — per secret-rule value the stored bytes and its `revealObscured` decode, bool literals excluded, crypt `password2` included (the one IsPassword key outside the name rule in the captured 1.60.1 catalogue, a test re-derives it), walked over every remote it wraps (`remote`/`upstreams`/combine `dir=`, cycle-safe) and the secret-rule parameters of an inline connection string — and the remote Test, the task preview and the task run all mask with it; the preview and the run read it THEMSELVES at the boundary (`runPreview`, `runCloudSync`), so no route can build deps without it and no test can feed it (W5: the route and the unit tests build the preview's deps through the one `cloudPreviewDeps`, and the preview tests hold the credential in a real rclone.conf). - **cr.59** `[done 2026-10-16]` **M6** True now: an automatically picked uid/gid skips every id a POSIX ACL entry names, access or default (`user:N:` for a uid, `group:N:` for a gid), under the share and export paths, every mounted ZFS filesystem whose `acltype` is posix (one `zfs get acltype` for all; a filesystem with ACLs off applies no grant, so it is not walked) and every mounted AHR pool — the share paths plus the access editor's targets. Each root is walked once per guard run, bounded as the ownership walk is (nf.1: `-P`, `-xdev` except on btrfs, every mount below pruned and looked at as a root of its own, network filesystems and automounts never entered, `.zfs` pruned), by ROOT and not by depth: `find … ! -type l -exec getfacl -n -p -c -E -s {} + | sort -u`, so a recursive grant over a whole tree arrives as a few distinct lines (links are filtered: getfacl follows one, and a dangling link fails the walk). The walk shares the check's one 10 s budget; a walk, sort, `zfs get` or AHR read that fails or runs out refuses the create as `id-check-unreadable` (a root that does not exist holds nothing, cr.29b's probe), and the job door's re-check of a picked id reads ACLs too. A skipped id says `an ACL entry under '<root>'`. Cost: the walk is one `getfacl` per file that is not a link — measured 2026-10-16 on the dev host, 440k entries in 1.9 s warm (the no-match ownership `find` over the same tree: 0.5 s; directories only: 0.5 s) — so a share or ACL-enabled filesystem past roughly two million files, or a cold cache, runs out of the budget and the automatic pick refuses; the out stays the explicit id. Also fixed while there: a local non-ZFS mount BELOW a share that is not itself walked (a share on ZFS, left to the accounting) is reached by both walks — cr.29's ownership walk skipped it. Not read: snapshots (as for ownership), a filesystem whose ACLs are off but still stores old ACL xattrs (they come back if `acltype` is switched on again later). Files: services/identity-ids.ts (+ test); the route test's create fixtures answer the three new reads. *(Merge note 2026-10-16: node fact settled — ZFS 2.4.4 prints `posix` for both `acltype=posixacl` and `acltype=posix` (`zfs get -H -p -o name,value acltype`), so the walk's accepted spellings hold. Two decisions for the operator: (1) COST — one deduplicated getfacl walk per managed root per pick (measured 1.9 s warm over 440k entries; ~2M files under ACL-enabled roots or a cold cache exceeds the shared 10 s budget and the automatic pick refuses, the explicit id remains); a directories-only walk would cut it ~4× and miss only hand-written file-level grants — not built, operator's call; (2) COUPLING — the pick now reads the AHR topology, so a failed `mdadm --detail` on ANY array refuses every automatic create with id-check-unreadable (the cr.33d coarse grain, rider ahr.r1). Also fixed in passing: a local non-ZFS mount below a ZFS share was never walked by the ownership check (cr.29 gap).)* *(RULED 2026-10-16: keep, narrowed to a DIRECTORIES-ONLY ACL walk (default entries live on directories; a hand-written file-level grant is the accepted miss) — "still on the margins" of the cr.29c cost ruling; the AHR coupling stays until rider ahr.r1.)* cr.59n (2026-10-16): the ACL walk is now DIRECTORIES-ONLY (`-type d` replaces `! -type l` in `find … -execdir getfacl -n -p -c -E -s {} + | sort -u`; a directory's getfacl prints its access AND its default entries, so a deleted account's inheritable grant is still seen — a grant written by hand on a single FILE is the accepted miss), ~4x the walk it replaces.

The routes' own topology reads (cr.33d) already fail first on the same read; these are the act-layer readers behind them. **(L17)** a LUN add holds `iscsi-target:` plus the backing's keys SHARED, a grow/write-cache change and a delete hold `iscsi-target:` + `iscsi-lun:` plus the backing's: a zvol's dataset keys (`zfsDatasetClaims` — the volume's `zfs:` key, its ancestors and the pool shared, the pool's name key shared; the name key exclusive when the pool guid is unreadable) EXCLUSIVE for a zvol grow (a `zfs set volsize`, as the Datasets door) and for `destroyBacking` on a zvol (a `zfs destroy`), shared otherwise; an image file's filesystem keys (the movers' `pathSourceClaims` of its directory: `zfs:`/`ahr:` and `mount:`) always SHARED, `destroyBacking` included — one `lunBackingClaims` in services/iscsi-mutate.ts; a dataset destroy accepted while a LUN add on that volume runs waits for the add's lock and then refuses on the LUN that now holds it, and the reverse waits too (both proven with the paused queue). **(L18)** `inFlightGate` asks `findActive(…, { before: self })` in the job (the queue's own submission order, never wall time): a job submitted after the confirmed destroy — a timer's scrub while it waits for a slot — no longer fails it (it queues behind the destroy's exclusive key and its own re-check finds the pool gone); an older job submitted in the window between the route's gates and its submit still refuses it, naming that job. **(L21)** every legacy `confirmGate` door in the AHR routes — expand (`routes/ahr-expand.ts`, was `{pool, addDisks, replace}`), replace (was `{pool, oldDiskId, newDiskId}`) and resume (was `{pool, intent, approvedDisks}`) — is on `guardConfirm`, bound to the pool's identity (the family's `poolView` id: sorted band array UUIDs + VG UUID) and signing the whole validated request (expand `{pool, addDisks, replace}` with absent ≡ `[]`/`null`; replace `{pool, oldDiskId, newDiskId}`, address pool + oldDiskId; resume `{pool}` with the intent id as the bound revision), no presentation-only field: a same-name pool re-created inside the TTL (array UUIDs or VG UUID moved) is 409 `IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, a changed body field 409 `CONFIRM_SCOPE_CHANGED` naming it, another intent 409 `STATE_CHANGED`, each with a fresh code and nothing submitted. Not in this story's fence and still on the legacy gate: `routes/jobs.ts` cancel (`{id}` — the whole request), `routes/shares-smb.ts` interface binding (`{section:'global'}`, deliberate per WAVE3-DESIGN §5.1), `routes/cloud.ts` remote retarget (`{remote, changes}`), `routes/backup.ts` in-place image restore (no `ns`, already folded into cr.56), `routes/mounts.ts` non-empty mountpoint (`{mountpoint}` only — cr.60's file). Review correction: the expand and replace confirms already signed every request field; what they lacked was the bound identity and the named-field 409. *(Review 2026-10-16, qwen3.8-flash-next-iq3_s on ai1, construct-it over the real routes, 110 turns / 15 min: 0 DEFECTS; every claim held under construction — unreadable mdstat answers 503 at GET /v1/scrub with the read in the sentence and never a half list; the boot re-attach keeps, halts and notifies an intent whose identity read failed; all four LUN lock shapes and both serialisations proven with the queue states pasted; the three L18 orders and five L21 confirm replays (incl. a cross-object code laundering attempt) all refused as designed; five mutations each failed 2–9 tests. 1 POSSIBLE → cr.64b2 (Q): `runningAhrCheck` (services/ahr-scrub.ts:406) reads mdstat with no exit check, unreachable in practice because the same gate's `reads.pools` throws first on the same failure. 4 NOT-A-DEFECT readings recorded: the scrub note-diff `.catch(()=>[])` is presentation-only and unreachable in an unreadable state; the boot scan's null identity fails loud (halt+notify); an image-file LUN delete under a backup's shared key is consistent because the backup snapshots first and unlink is atomic; the replace confirm cannot be laundered across outgoing disks. Coverage note: the destroy/resume/retire callers' fail-before-act is proven at the reader level only.)*

- **cr.52r** `[done 2026-10-16]` **cr.52's five reviewed defects + possibles** — (1) D1: the refusal applies to ANCESTORS of every protected root, not only to paths at or under one (a mountpoint that is an ancestor of `/home/*/.ssh`, `/var/spool/cron`, `/var/lib/pve*`, `/mnt/pve`, `/root`, … is refused: `/home`, `/home/alice`, `/var/spool`, `/var/lib`, `/mnt`, `/var`); (2) D2: realpath the LONGEST EXISTING ancestor (walk up until a component exists), never skip the step on ENOENT; (3) D3: the comment on `mount -c` is wrong (it is `--no-canonicalize`); the remote-mount and AHR mount jobs mount through a directory the daemon itself opened — lstat-walk every component (refuse any symlink), open the final directory `O_PATH|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW`, pass the fd to the child and mount with target `/proc/self/fd/<n>` (Node `spawn` `stdio: [..., fd]`), so a swap after the open cannot redirect the kernel; where ZFS mounts itself (`zfs create -o mountpoint`, `zpool create -m`) the window is ZFS's own and is documented as such, with every ancestor required root-owned and not group/other-writable (refuse otherwise, naming the directory) so a non-root writer cannot stage the swap; (4) D4: `POST /pools/import` runs `altroot` through the same refusal and refuses an import whose stored mountpoints (read with `zpool import` listing / `zfs get -r mountpoint` after a `-N` import) land on a refused path, unmounting nothing it did not mount; (5) D5: a pool created without `-m` is checked as `/<name>`; (6) P1: `/var/lib/vz`, `/var/lib/ceph`, `/var/lib/dpkg`, `/var/log` join the list with the reason per entry; (7) P2: the re-mount verbs (mount state action, AHR remount, AHR snapshot rollback's remount, the boot re-attach) run the gate on the stored path before mounting; (8) P3: a job re-gate test per gated door (the paused-queue pattern); (9) docs: the True-now sentence, the `-c` comment, one 409 code at every door for `/mnt/pve` and `/etc/pve` (PVE territory keeps its own reason). Files: services/mounts.ts, routes/mounts.ts, routes/pools.ts, routes/datasets.ts, routes/ahr-mutate.ts, services/ahr-create.ts, services/ahr-snapshots.ts, services/ahr-boot-scan.ts, executor (fd passing seam), their tests, docs/EPICS.md. Lane: LOCAL (DeepSeek, the builder) once free. **True now:** the mount-target refusal refuses an ANCESTOR of every protected and system root (a mount at `/home/alice`, `/var/spool`, `/var/lib`, `/mnt` hides the `.ssh`/cron/pve* trees — D1) and resolves the mountpoint against its LONGEST EXISTING ancestor, refusing any symlink in the existing part and never skipping the step on an ENOENT intermediate (D2's `l -> /etc`, `l/newdir/data` refuses); the mounts the daemon itself runs — remote mount, AHR create and move, the AHR remount and snapshot-rollback remount, the boot re-attach — mount DIRECTLY from the configured entry through a directory the daemon opened O_PATH|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW (corrected cr.52s-f F4: `fs.constants.O_PATH` is undefined on every node version measured, 22 and 26, so the open is really `O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` and the readlink proof is the guarantee), the child inherits the fd (`exec` `inheritedFds`) and `mount -c` targets `/proc/self/fd/3` (util-linux's `-c` is `--no-canonicalize`; without it the fd path is canonicalised into a swap-able string), so a parent swapped after the open cannot redirect the kernel mount; where ZFS mounts for itself (`-m`, `-o mountpoint=`, import) every existing ancestor of the mountpoint must be root-owned and not group/other-writable (refused otherwise, the residual documented as ZFS's own window) *(cr.52s-a: the leaf too, and a missing name's would-be parent without the sticky exception)*; `POST /pools/import` runs `altroot` through the refusal and imports `-N` first — `zfs get -H -r -o name,value mountpoint` gates every effective mountpoint BEFORE any mount, exporting the pool again on a refusal so nothing is half-imported; a pool created without `-m` is gated as `/<name>`; `/var/lib/vz`, `/var/lib/ceph`, `/var/lib/dpkg` and `/var/log` joined the list with a reason each; the re-mount verbs (mount state action, AHR remount, the rollback's remount and the boot re-attach) re-run the refusal on the STORED path before mounting, the boot path logging and skipping rather than throwing; `/mnt/pve` and `/etc/pve` answer ONE 409 CONFLICT reason `system-mount-target` at every setting door, the PVE-territory reason named in the sentence. *(Merge notes 2026-10-16: three DeepSeek sessions (400 + 250 + 300 turns; the code in session 1, the test pass in 2–3), squashed over 14 merges — five composed conflicts (import unions; the create door keeps cr.60's fail-closed live-mount read behind cr.52r's swap-stage gate) and two expected test shifts (the inline-CIFS test's line is now built per test because the mountpoint must be a real directory; the pool create tests answer findmnt because a create without a mountpoint is now checked at `/<name>`); main suite 6699/6699. Decisions worth the operator's eye: (1) every daemon-run mount(8) is now a DIRECT mount through `/proc/self/fd/3` with the entry's own fstab tokens *(narrowed, cr.52s-a: every daemon-run mount(8) of a filesystem onto a directory — the remote mount, the AHR create, move, remount, rollback remount and boot re-attach, and the Previous Versions `@snapshots` mount — is a direct fd-pinned `-c` mount; the update verb's busy-fallback `mount -o remount <mp>` stays path-based by decision (it re-flags the mount already there, found by its target path; reason in routes/mounts.ts); and until cr.52s-a the AHR verbs rebuilt ANAS's default options instead of the entry's own)* — `mount -c <mp>` cannot find an fstab entry by an fd path and without `-c` util-linux canonicalises the fd back into a path (measured); (2) the import runs `zpool import -N` → gates → `zpool export` → `zpool import` so ZFS mounts the datasets exactly as a single import would (a second-import failure leaves the pool unimported, never half-mounted) — a node pass row; (3) sticky world-writable root-owned ancestors (`/tmp`, 1777) pass the swap-stage rule because the sticky bit gives the same rename protection *(corrected, cr.52s-a: the sticky bit protects only a name the swapper does not own and that already exists — a user-owned leaf and any missing name under 1777 passed; the rule now checks the leaf and a missing name's would-be parent, see cr.52s-a)*; (4) a real code bug found by its own red test: the boot cache-recover re-attach gated the LV device path of a DOWN pool, so it always refused — it now reads the pool's own fstab line for the configured mountpoint *(corrected, cr.52s-a 2026-10-17: main never had that bug — before this story the rung was UNGATED and ran `mount -- <lv>`, which found the line by its spec and logged `result=ok` (cross-model review Q4c, the pre-commit code run over the fake); the always-refusing gate was this story's own draft, never shipped)*; (5) the mount.nfs4/mount.cifs helper is a GRANDCHILD of mount(8) — whether the fd inheritance reaches it is a NODE question (the node pass must mount a real NFS and CIFS entry through the daemon). Harness: socket-perms.test.ts falls back to /tmp for its socket dir when TMPDIR would overflow sun_path. Review owed: Opus (DeepSeek built).)* *(Cross-model review, Opus 2026-10-17, construct-it over the real routes with a real temp tree and a renameat2 flip loop, 91-row door table: 7 DEFECTS, none caught by the 316 touched tests → cr.52s-a / cr.52s-b — (1) a failed `zpool list` BEFORE the import makes `before` empty, so the job gates, EXPORTS and re-imports every pool the second listing names (testpool exported by an oldtank import, job `completed`, no lock held on testpool); (2) a `zpool export` that fails after a refusal is swallowed (`.catch` on an exec that resolves with an exit code) and the job still says the pool was exported — it stays imported in zpool.cache with its `/etc` mountpoint; (3) an import by GUID whose post-import listing fails has no `req.name` to fall back to, runs no `zfs get` and imports over `/etc` — fail-open on an unreadable read; (4) `openMountTarget` walks for symlinks and opens in separate steps, so an intermediate exchanged for a symlink between the two is followed (`O_NOFOLLOW` guards only the leaf): 19,596 of 70,972 opens landed on the victim — the remote-mount and AHR create/move doors have no swap-stage rule and rest on this pin; (5) the swap-stage 'sticky exception' never checks the LEAF and skips MISSING names, so `/tmp/alice` (user-owned) and `/tmp/newpool` pass under 1777 `/tmp` — the builder's own test rides it; (6) the boot cache-recover re-attach, the AHR remount and the snapshot-rollback remount now mount with ANAS's DEFAULT AHR options (`noatime`, `compress=zstd:3` dropped) instead of the pool's fstab line — a regression against 'config files are the API' and the True-now sentence; (7) a re-attached pool that cannot be mounted makes the boot rung report the CACHE recovery failed ('Detach the cache') although the cache was dropped — `mountAhrPool` throws into the rung's catch. 7 POSSIBLES recorded (unreadable fstab at boot logs as 'no entry'; a failed `zfs get` leaves the pool imported-unmounted; two path-based mount(8) calls remain in share-selfservice.ts:291 and the update verb's remount fallback; `/var/lib/systemd`, `/var/cache`, `/opt` pass the doors; a dataset create under a pre-cr.52r parent is not gated at its inherited mountpoint; `remountEntry` re-mounts without the refusal; import gating refuses over `canmount=off` datasets that never mount). NOT A DEFECT: a parent swapped AFTER the open does not move the mount; the executor fd seam is sound (fd at stdio 3 only, closed in a finally, not leaked to a sibling exec). DOC DRIFT: decision (4) above describes a bug main never had — before this commit the rung ran an UNGATED `mount -- <lv>` that logged `result=ok` (the always-refuses bug was the builder's own draft); 'every daemon-run mount(8)' over-claims (P3); the `mountThroughOpenedDir` comment says `-c` is added by the helper (every caller passes it). (5) the swap-stage rule checks every EXISTING component of the mountpoint INCLUDING the leaf (root-owned), lets a sticky directory be writable only as the parent of an existing root-owned name, and treats a missing name as its subject — its would-be parent must be root-owned and not group/other-writable with no sticky exception — so `/tmp/alice` (user-owned) and `/tmp/newpool` (missing under 1777) refuse and `/tmp/rootdir` passes; (P3) the Previous Versions `@snapshots` mount is a direct fd-pinned `-c` mount of its stored line, while the update verb's busy `-o remount` fallback stays path-based by decision (a remount re-flags the mount already at the path and util-linux finds it by that path — the reason is in routes/mounts.ts) and `mountThroughOpenedDir` itself guarantees `-c`; (P6) the update verb runs the mount-target refusal on the stored path as a gate — at the route and in the job, BEFORE the line is rewritten. *(Decisions: the move's line keeping its own options goes one step past the three sites named, under the same rule; the remount and rollback services default to `ANAS_FSTAB_PATH`/`/etc/fstab` because their routes (routes/ahr-mutate.ts, routes/ahr-snapshots.ts — outside this story's files) do not pass the server's `fstabPath` yet, which a mock server would need; Lane: LOCAL (DeepSeek, after cr.64b2-i merges). **True now:** (1) a `zpool list` the import job cannot read BEFORE the `-N` import fails the job `503 UNAVAILABLE` naming the read and nothing is imported, and the set to gate — and, on a refusal, to export again — is ONLY the pool this job imported, by name the request's pool (`zpool import <name>` imports exactly it) and by guid the name the post-import listing gives with the cr.64b2-i scan's predicted name as the fallback, never 'everything the second listing names that the first did not' (a failed BEFORE listing used to return `[]`, turn `after - before` into every pool the second listing named, and a refusal would export them all); (2) the `zpool export` that undoes a refused `-N` import CHECKS its exit — an export that fails fails the job with the export's stderr and a sentence that says the pool is STILL IMPORTED (with `-N`, unmounted), at which refused mountpoint it would mount, and what to run, never the old 'exported again'; (3) an import by GUID whose post-import listing cannot be read exports the imported pool again (CHECKED, as (2)) and refuses `503 UNAVAILABLE` naming the `zpool list` read — there is no `zpool import` (the real one) without a gate read, and a guid the import scan named nothing for refuses naming the state (still imported unmounted, what to run) because no export name can be read; (4) a failed `zfs get` after the `-N` import does the same — the pool is exported again (checked) and the job refuses naming the read, so the op is never left half-imported; (5) the mountpoint gate reads `canmount` in the SAME `zfs get` (`-o name,property,value` over `mountpoint,canmount`, one row per property per dataset) and skips a dataset whose `canmount` is `off` — it never mounts at import, so a stored `/etc` on it is not a path ZFS will take (a `canmount=on` dataset at `/etc` still refuses). *(Merge notes 2026-10-17: DeepSeek, the builder; 7 new tests over the two pools-import suites; implemented P7's argv change first because every other test fixture rides the `zfs get` shape. Decisions: the two-property `zfs get` takes `-o name,property,value` (with `-o name,value` the mountpoint row and the canmount row of one dataset are two indistinguishable `name<TAB>value` lines); a by-guid import whose NAME read fails exports by the cr.64b2-i scan's predicted name — the '-'N import just succeeded under it — and falls back to the listing diff when the scan named nothing, refusing with the node's state when both reads fail; the job-level refusals are GuardRefusalErrors, so `job.error.code` reads `UNAVAILABLE` (a 503 the route would send) rather than `JOB_FAILED`; WAVE3-DESIGN §4.2 needed no change (the lock keys are untouched). Fixture provenance: the failing `zpool list` uses the stderr the cr.64b2-i review already pins (`zpool: cannot read configuration: access denied`, exit 127); the failing export's stderr is the captured busy-export line `iscsi/error-texts.txt` §10 re-pointed at the pool's refused mountpoint in-test, the established iscsi-test pattern; a failed `zfs get` carries the captured `iscsi/zfs-list-t-volume-absent.stderr.txt` 'cannot open' shape re-pointed at the pool; the passing `zfs get` answers are the cr.52r lines extended with their canmount rows. Defect (6)'s `zpool export`-argv comments and cr.52s-a's contract untouched.)* *Cross-model review, Opus 2026-10-17 (construct-it, 9 route probes, 3 mutations): 2 DEFECTS reproduced and fixed by the reviewer (one root): a by-guid import whose scan named no pool and whose post-import diff was EMPTY ran the real mounting import with no `zfs get` (the gate loop ran over nothing); a TWO-name diff (another import/create landing in the window under other keys) gated both and on a pass exported the foreign pool and never re-imported it — now the diff must name exactly one newcomer, else the job refuses naming the state (still imported unmounted, `zpool list -o name,guid`, export by name), nothing exported. POSSIBLES recorded, not built: (P-a) `canmount=noauto` is refused like `on` although ZFS never mounts it at import — an over-refusal, `-R` is the workaround; (P-b) a `zfs get` that exits 0 with no `mountpoint` row for the pool root (or empty stdout) gates nothing and the mounted import runs — real zfs always prints the root's rows, but the "read that could not see" rule applied to the root would export (checked) and refuse; (P-c) the export-failure sentence after a failed read drops the triggering read's reason and does not say a reboot or `zfs mount -a` mounts the pool at unchecked paths until it is exported; (P-d) the two read-failure undo exports have no failing-export test (an unchecked mutation survives); (P-e) no captured fixture holds the `-H -o name,property,value` row shape — a stunt-node capture of that argv with a zvol and a snapshot under `-r` is owed (node pass). A better by-guid resolver: `zpool list -j` carries `pool_guid`; picking the post-import entry by guid would name the pool exactly even when the scan is empty — story-sized, noted.*
- **cr.52s-d** `[done 2026-10-17]` **the fd-pinned mount target is `/proc/self/fd/3/.`, not `/proc/self/fd/3`** — found by the interim integration run on the stunt node (2026-10-17, util-linux 2.41.5 / kernel 7.0.14): every daemon-run mount failed EINVAL ("wrong fs type, bad option, bad superblock") and the AHR create rolled back ten times. Reproduced by hand and straced: libmount's new-API path ends in `move_mount(fs, "", AT_FDCWD, "/proc/self/fd/3", MOVE_MOUNT_F_EMPTY_PATH)`, and `move_mount` does not follow a TRAILING symlink, so the bare fd path is the magic link itself; the classic `mount(2)` path (`LIBMOUNT_FORCE_MOUNT2=always`) accepted it, which is why no fake could see it. True now: `FD_MOUNT_TARGET` is `/proc/self/fd/3/.` — the magic link is a middle component every walk follows and the final `.` is the opened directory; proven on the node with `-c` for btrfs (`subvolid=5`) and ext4, `umount <real path>` works, and a directory swapped for a symlink AFTER the open still gets the mount on the real directory (the victim stays empty). Node fact recorded for the follow-up: with `-c`, libmount's `/run/mount/utab` records `TARGET=/proc/self/fd/3/.` for a mount whose `-o` carries userspace tokens (`x-systemd.*`, `_netdev`) and the entry outlives the umount — harmless (mount ids are not reused) but accumulating. Files: services/mounts.ts (the constant + its docstring), the 14 test files pinning the target, docs/EPICS.md. *Node pass 2026-10-17: PASS — see the node-pass entry below cr.52s-e.*
