- **cr.52s-f** `[done 2026-10-17]` **node-pass residuals in the mount pin** — (F3, LOW) a failed remote-mount create leaves the mountpoint directory it created while its sentence says "Nothing else was written": rmdir the leaf through the parent fd when this job created it (never a pre-existing directory), or name the directory in the sentence; test at the route: a wrong-password CIFS create at a new leaf → no directory, the sentence exact. (F4, LOW) `fs.constants.O_PATH` is UNDEFINED on node 22 (v22.23.3), so `openMountTarget`'s `O_PATH | O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW` opens the directory plain `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY` (strace) — it works for root on plain directories and the readlink proof holds, but the code comments and the cr.52s-a/-d text claim O_PATH; lever `?? 0o10000000` (Linux's O_PATH), which needs a node proof first (an O_RDONLY open of an automount trigger would fire the automount — the one edge not tested); the cr.52s-a review showed the constant is undefined on node 26.12 as well, so the lever is not node-22-specific, and an O_RDONLY open of a directory the daemon cannot read answers EACCES where O_PATH would succeed (never for root). **Node probe 2026-10-17 (stunt node, kernel 7.0.14-23-pve, a transient `systemd-mount --automount=yes` tmpfs): an `O_PATH|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` open of the autofs trigger FIRES the automount exactly as `O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` does (findmnt showed `autofs tmpfs` after the O_PATH open alone), so the `?? 0o10000000` lever changes nothing for automount targets and F4 reduces to making the code comments and the cr.52s-a/-d text say what the pin is on every node version measured (22, 26): an O_RDONLY directory open whose readlink proof is the guarantee. Docs-only; fold into the next commit that touches services/mounts.ts.** (F6, LOW — cr.52s-a review) the AHR create and mountpoint-move doors still make the mountpoint with a textual `mkdir(mountpoint, { recursive: true })` (services/ahr-create.ts ~:407 and ~:655) — the swap cr.52s-c closed for the remote create (a parent exchanged for a symlink between the re-gate and the mkdir creates directories THROUGH the link as root; the fd-pinned mount then refuses, the created tree stays) applies verbatim there; make them `createMountpointThroughOpenedParent` with the same tests. (F7, LOW, sentence-shaped — cr.52s-a review) the forced-read-only Remount door admits a pool whose own fstab line says `ro` (it is `mountedReadOnly`), re-mounts its line (right) and then fails with the fault-attributed "STILL read-only … btrfs re-applies the flag when the fault … is still present" for a pool that never had a fault — when the line carries `ro`, say so ("the pool's own fstab line mounts it read-only") and refuse at the door; and no test pins the STILL-read-only verify path at all (services/ahr-create.ts ~:601). (F8, LOW — cr.52s-c review) the remote-mount create's rollback: a rollback umount whose exec THROWS (the binary absent, the executor rejecting) must be treated as a failed umount (STILL MOUNTED sentence) and never skip the credentials removal; the credentials `rm` result feeds the sentence ('removed' only when it was, else the file named); a `daemon-reload` failure after the written line reports the surviving state (mounted, line written, units stale — what to run) instead of the raw reload error; the credentials WRITE failing sits outside the rollback try (the leaf this job made is then left, raw error) — bring it inside. (F9, test/wiring — cr.52s-c review) ahr-remount.test.ts's fstabPath pin uses a real kernel option (`noatime`) so a dropped `{ fstabPath }` fails it (today's `subvol=@data` equals the fallback argv); server.ts passes `fstabPath` to the snapshot routes. Cosmetic: the swap-stage symlink refusal still ends with "Make '<link>' root-owned and not group/other-writable". Files: services/mounts.ts, services/ahr-create.ts, routes/mounts.ts, routes/ahr-mutate.ts, their tests, docs/EPICS.md. Lane: LOCAL (ai1) + a node probe for F4. **True now:** (F3) a remote-mount create whose mount or fstab write fails removes the mountpoint leaf IT CREATED — `createMountpointThroughOpenedParent` reports `{created}` and the rollback rmdirs at `/proc/self/fd/<parentFd>/<leaf>` through the re-opened+proved parent (a directory that existed before the job is never removed; `rmdir(2)` itself refuses a non-empty or swapped leaf, and nothing was mounted so the leaf is empty), and the sentence stays "Nothing else was written" — only an rmdir failure replaces it with the directory named and left behind plus the failed reason, to be removed by hand (route tests: wrong-password CIFS at a new leaf → no directory, sentence exact; the same at an existing empty directory → it stands; an injected EACCES at the fd-path rmdir → the sentence names it); (F6) the AHR create and mountpoint-move doors make the mountpoint with `createMountpointThroughOpenedParent` (pin + readlink proof, missing parent refused naming it, existing directory leaf success, non-directory refused) and both doors GATE it: a new mountpoint's missing parent answers 409 naming it at the route and again in the job — for the move the gate re-runs BEFORE the body, so a pool is never unmounted toward a path with nowhere to be made — a symlinked parent refuses at the route, an existing empty leaf proceeds (route tests over the fake executor + real temp tree, plus service tests pinning that no chain is ever created); (F7) the forced-read-only Remount door's first gate reads the pool's own active fstab line (three-valued `readAhrPoolLine`) and a line carrying `ro` answers 409 naming the option and the file — that read-only is configuration, not a btrfs fault, and re-running the line would land read-only again — never unmounting such a pool, while a line without `ro` (or no line) meets the old door; and the verify path is pinned both ways: a remount that lands read-only per findmnt fails with the exact fault-attributed STILL-read-only sentence, one that lands rw resolves `readOnly: false`; (F4) the pin's comments (services/mounts.ts at the open and the handle type, routes/mounts.ts) and the cr.52r/-a/-c True-now claims now say what is true on every node version measured (22, 26): `fs.constants.O_PATH` is UNDEFINED in node, so the open is really `O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` and the readlink proof is the guarantee — the node probe showed an O_PATH open of an autofs trigger fires the automount just as a plain open does, so the `?? 0o10000000` lever would buy nothing and the code and its flag are UNCHANGED (comments/docs only, with a DON'T-add-the-lever note at the lookup); (F8) the create job's rollback now survives its OWN failures — a rollback umount whose exec THROWS (spawn ENOENT, the executor rejecting) is a failed rollback exactly like a non-zero exit (STILL MOUNTED with the thrown reason; the credentials removal and leaf rmdir still run), the credentials `rm` result feeds the sentence ('the credentials file was removed' only when it was, else `the credentials file <path> could not be removed (<code>) and is left on disk`), a `daemon-reload` failure after the written line fails the job with the SURVIVING state named (the mount is up, the line is written in the fstab, systemd's units are stale — run 'systemctl daemon-reload'; nothing was rolled back — the documented decision stands, only the sentence changes), and the credentials WRITE moved inside the rollback try so a failed write removes the leaf this job made and reports through the same sentence (route tests pin each branch's exact sentence, argv, fstab bytes, creds-file and leaf presence; the umount-wrap mutation fails its test); (F9) the ahr-remount fstabPath pin rides a real kernel option the no-line fallback cannot produce (the stored line gains `noatime`, argv `-o noatime,subvol=@data` — dropping the route's `{ fstabPath }` now fails the test, proven: the argv reverts to the fallback's `-o subvol=@data` alone), and server.ts registers `ahrSnapshotRoutes` with the server's `fstabPath` like the mutate routes (cr.52s-c's one wiring line — the snapshot route tests register with `fstabPath` themselves, so nothing else observes it). *(Decisions: the missing-parent 409 covers the DEFAULT AHR mountpoint too — the door computes the same `<base>/<name>` the job builds, so on a node where the mount base does not exist the first create answers 409 naming the base instead of building a chain (or a build-then-rollback); the F3 rmdir re-opens the parent (pin + proof) rather than keeping the create's fd, so the rollback runs its own proof; the move's leaf mkdir stays after the unmount in the service (moving it before would change the busy-umount error a shipped test pins) — the pre-unmount refusal is the gate's.)* *Cross-model review, DeepSeek 2026-10-17 (construct-it, five probes one door each — 74 turns, full report): 0 DEFECTS — (1) a mount that succeeded, a line write that failed and a rollback umount that THROWS with the leaf rmdir answering EBUSY produces both the STILL MOUNTED clause and the left-behind clause (judged honest: two true states in the order the operator resolves them; a sentence boundary smoothed the same hour); (2) over a real tree, a leaf replaced by a populated directory or by a symlink to a victim between create and remove refuses ENOTEMPTY / ENOTDIR with the victim untouched; (3) the move door re-gated in the job after the parent was removed refuses before any umount argv, the default-mountpoint create with the base gone 409s naming the base, and a base that exists as a FILE or a SYMLINK makes the startup mkdir silent (EEXIST) and the first create refuse 'is not a directory' — a clean 409, never a write; (4) the F7 door refuses `ro` and `rw,ro`, opens on `ro,rw` — the parser's last-token-wins agrees with mount(8) — and an unreadable fstab passes the door while the service refuses before the unmount; (5) a reload failure without mountNow does not claim a mount is up, with mountNow names all three. COVERAGE GAPS recorded, not built: the throwing-umount + EBUSY-leaf combination and the `rw,ro`/`ro,rw` variants have no committed test. POSSIBLE folded: the reload-failure sentence now names the surviving credentials file beside the line. Node fact from the probes: `fs.rmdir(path, { recursive: true })` is removed on node 26 — the repo already uses `fs.rm`.*

- **cr.52s-e** `[done 2026-10-17]` **strip userspace fstab tokens from the daemon-run mount's `-o`** — `mountOptionTokens(entry)` hands the fstab line's whole option string to the direct `mount -c`, including tokens that only systemd/fstab consume (`x-systemd.device-timeout=`, `x-systemd.requires=`, `_netdev`, `nofail`, `noauto`); mount(8) ignores them, but with `-c` libmount writes them to `/run/mount/utab` under `TARGET=/proc/self/fd/3/.` (cr.52s-d's node fact), one stale entry per mount. Rule: the direct mount carries only kernel/helper options (drop `x-*`, `_netdev`, `nofail`, `noauto`, `auto`); the fstab LINE keeps them all. Test: an entry with `nofail,x-systemd.device-timeout=10,noatime` mounts with `-o noatime`; the written line unchanged. True now: `directMountOptionTokens(entry)` — with `directMountOptionArgs` returning the `-o` argv fragment, and NO `-o` at all when nothing survives (`mount -o ''` is not the same as none) — drops `x-*`, `_netdev`, `nofail`, `noauto`, `auto` and `comment=*` by whole token (trimmed lower-cased key) from every direct-mount argv: routes/mounts.ts `doMount`, ahr-fstab.ts `mountAhrPool` (ahr-create, ahr-snapshots and the boot-scan rung reach it through that one), share-selfservice.ts; the fstab LINE (`mountOptionTokens`, the serializer, addMount/replaceMount) keeps every token, and ahr-boot-scan's `isSubvolLayoutMount(mountOptionTokens(entry))` is not an argv so it stays. Files: parsers/fstab.ts (the two helpers + the whole-token test), the three call sites, their tests (fstab.test.ts unit; re-pinned argvs in ahr-remount, shares-smb, ahr-boot-scan-cache, ahr-create, ahr-own-line, share-selfservice; new argv-vs-line side-by-side route tests in mounts.test.ts and ahr-remount.test.ts), docs/EPICS.md. *Node pass 2026-10-17: PASS — see the node-pass entry below cr.52s-e.* *Cross-model review, Qwen3.8 IQ4_XS (dgx3) 2026-10-17, read-only, 93 turns: CLEAN — the token table constructed through the real functions (22 tokens), the drop set checked against `man 8 mount` (`X-*`/`x-*` never reach the kernel or the helper; the lowercase form is what libmount records — the utab fact) and against real tmpfs mounts in an unprivileged mount namespace; the KEEP of `user`/`users`/`owner`/`group` proven right (they are not userspace-only — they set `nosuid,nodev[,noexec]` kernel flags, so dropping them would make the daemon mount weaker than the boot mount); `commentary=1`, `foo=nofail` kept by the whole-token rule; the route block's line-keeps/argv-drops assertions read side by side. Noted: a `user`-carrying line still leaves a utab record (mount(8) writes the mounting user there) — information, not a defect; `mountOptionTokens` on a DISABLED entry serialises without the `x-anas.disabled` decoration (fine for its argv callers; the fstab writer never uses it). The same review covered b7d06dbf4 and a639a58c3 (node-pass F1/F2): 0 defects over a 20-site audit of every mount-table reader (the rest are target-based or realpath-based); two COVERAGE GAPS closed by the coordinator the same hour — the expand's fs-grow had no LV-path pin (the reviewer's mutation showed the committed suite could not see the fix) and the identity.3 journal line had no test (a capturing logger on the M5 strict test now pins exactly one line) — and services/mounts.ts's `ahrManaged` comment corrected (the source carries the fsroot suffix and, since cr.52r, either spelling).*
